- The legacy `ProfilesDialog` is gone — per-workspace env-var management lives inside `WorkspaceForm`.

## 6. IPC surface

All channels are `ipcMain.handle`/`ipcRenderer.invoke` (promise-based) except the PTY data stream, which uses one-way `webContents.send` from main to renderer.

### Workspace
Identity is the **ULID** `id` (immutable, 26 char Crockford base32). The user-facing `name` is a mutable label. Channels that operate on workspace identity (`list`, `create`, `start`, `getManifest`) take `id`; channels that operate on the live container itself (`stop`, `pause`, `remove`) take the Docker `containerId` because their target is the container instance, not the workspace identity.

- `workspace:ping` → `boolean` — is the backend reachable (for the Docker backend, is the daemon up).
- `workspace:list` → `Workspace[]` — merged list of live (running/stopped) workspaces plus deleted workspaces (those with a manifest on disk but no live container). The merge (backend container/local listing + every on-disk manifest) is served through a **1s-TTL promise cache with in-flight dedup** (`ttlCache.ts`): it is the hottest main-process path (the renderer's 5s poll plus ~7 internal callers, several per user action), and uncached it re-listed Docker + WSL and re-read every manifest per call. Mutating handlers (create/start/stop/pause/remove/writeManifest, committee pause/unpause, loadout install/uninstall) invalidate the cache so a post-mutation read is fresh; state changed outside the app (docker CLI, container death) surfaces within TTL + poll latency, same as before. The freshness window starts when the underlying fetch *resolves*, so a fetch that outlives the TTL (event-loop stall, slow daemon) is shared rather than stampeded. When the Docker daemon is unreachable (daemon-connect errors only — `isDaemonConnectError` in `workspaceMerge.ts`; any other docker error still rejects), the merge degrades instead of failing: container-kind manifests are synthesized as `state: 'unreachable'` with `lastKnownState` (`running|paused|stopped|deleted`) from an in-memory map refreshed on every successful merge (never persisted — an app started mid-outage shows all container workspaces cold). A last-known `'deleted'` stays `'deleted'`. Local workspaces are unaffected. `WorkspaceState` is `running | paused | stopped | deleted | unreachable`.
- `workspace:create(input: WorkspaceCreatePayload)` → `Workspace` — create + start a runner workspace AND write its manifest to `<userData>/state/<id>/workspace.json`. The renderer mints the ULID and ships the payload (id, name, description, labels, color, workspaceSubdir, kind, image, authMode, endpointId, env: {plain, secretKeys}, resources, mounts, and optionally `knowledgeCapture` — normalized server-side, persisted in the manifest, and the source of a container's initial `cleanupPeriodDays`). **No `workspaceRoot`** — the backend derives the workspace's private folder (`<fleetRoot>/<id>`) and shared folder (`<fleetRoot>/shared`) from the app-level fleet root and creates both. Secret env values land in the vault under `<id>:<key>` *before* this call so the main process can resolve them at container-start time. For `kind: 'container'`, `mounts` is validated server-side by `validateExtraMounts` (`src/main/extraMountsHost.ts`) before the spec is persisted or the container is created: each `hostPath` must be an absolute, existing directory and must not be a network UNC path (`\\server\…` or `//…`; checked on both the given path and its realpath — "network paths (\\server\share) can't be mounted. Use a local drive or a WSL path (\\wsl$\<distro>\…)."). On win32 only, a WSL path (`\\wsl$\<distro>\…` or `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…`, parsed by `uncToLinuxPath`) is allowed and checked inside the distro instead of with host realpath, because Windows realpath over 9P does not follow Linux symlinks. The distro name must match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$` and must not start with `docker-desktop` ("that's Docker Desktop's own WSL distro and can't be mounted."). `wslRealDir` then makes one call, `wsl.exe -d <distro> --exec sh -c '<fixed script>' sh <path>`, whose script (the path only arrives as `$1`) runs `test -d`, prints `realpath -e` (falling back to `test -e` + `readlink -f` where `realpath -e` is missing), a `--MOUNTS--` marker line, and `/proc/self/mounts`; main splits at the last marker (exactly one trailing newline removed from the path) and decodes the mount table's octal escapes (any failure → "doesn't exist or isn't a folder"). The resolved path is refused when any segment falls outside `[A-Za-z0-9._@+ -]`, is `.`/`..`, or ends in `.` or a space, since Windows re-parses the `\\wsl$` Source ("that folder's path has characters Windows can't pass to Docker safely. Rename it or pick another folder."); when it is at or under `/mnt`, or at, under, or an ancestor of any mount point of type `9p`, `drvfs`, `v9fs`, or `virtiofs` (Windows drives wherever wsl.conf/fstab put them, and Docker Desktop's `/mnt/wsl`: "that's a Windows drive or Docker Desktop folder inside WSL. Use the C:\… path instead."); at, under, or an ancestor of a `cifs`, `smb3`, `smbfs`, or `nfs*` mount point ("that folder is on a network share inside WSL and can't be mounted."); at or under `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/run`, `/var/run` ("that's a system folder"); exactly `/root` or `/home/<user>` ("that's a whole home folder (it holds .claude and .docker)"); equal to or an ancestor of any of those folders or `/home` ("that folder contains system or Docker folders", e.g. `/`, `/var`, `/home`); or with a `.docker` path segment ("that folder holds Docker's settings"). When `<userData>` or the fleet root is itself a WSL path in the same distro (case-insensitive), it is resolved the same way and the userData and fleet-root rules below apply to the in-distro paths. The Docker `Source` for a WSL mount is always the `\\wsl$\<distro>\<real>` form; the manifest keeps the user's `hostPath`. For non-WSL paths: its realpath must not be inside or an ancestor of `<userData>`, nor equal to or an ancestor of a Docker socket folder, nor equal to, inside, or an ancestor of `~/.docker` (`dockerSocketDirs()` → `{ socketDirs, dockerConfigDirs }`: `socketDirs` holds both the literal and the resolved folder of the socket dockerode connects to — the `DOCKER_HOST` `unix://` path, else `/var/run/docker.sock`; a symlinked socket such as WSL Docker Desktop's `/mnt/wsl/docker-desktop/…` protects its real folder too; none for `tcp://` or on win32, where the socket is a named pipe — so folders inside the socket folder, like `/run/media/<user>/<drive>`, stay mountable; `dockerConfigDirs` is `~/.docker`; each realpath'd and skipped when absent; "that folder holds Docker's control socket and can't be mounted."); under the fleet root (deny by default) only `<fleetRoot>/shared` and this workspace's own `<fleetRoot>/<id>` are allowed — another workspace's `<fleetRoot>/<otherId>` is refused by name ("that folder is another workspace's private folder"), and an ancestor of the fleet root is refused too (it would expose every workspace's private folder). `containerPath` is normalized, defaults to `/mnt/<basename(hostPath)>` when blank, is refused at `/` or exactly `/workspace` (`/workspace/<sub>` is allowed); at or under `/shared`, `/home/fleet/.claude.json`, `/home/fleet/.claude`, `/run/broker`, `/fleet/mcp`, or a system folder (`/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/etc`, `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/lib64`, `/lib`, `/var/run`, `/var/lock`); and at any ancestor of the app's own mount targets (`/home`, `/home/fleet`, `/run`, `/fleet`: "<path> contains the app's own folders (<target>)"), since a bind there would hide them. It must be unique across the workspace's mounts. A validation failure rejects the call; other kinds never persist `mounts` (dropped on manifest read, §7).
- `workspace:start(id)` → `Workspace | null` — start an existing (live, possibly stopped or paused) workspace by ULID (looked up via the `com.claude-fleet.id` label). Paused containers are `unpause`d; stopped containers are `start`ed. Returns null if no container is labelled with that id (caller should recreate from the saved manifest using the same id via the create flow). This channel does **not** refresh the image — that's `workspace:resumeImageStale`, which the Saved-tab Resume flow calls first for stopped containers.
- `workspace:getManifest(id)` → `WorkspaceSpec | null` — read the persisted manifest.
- `workspace:writeManifest(spec)` → `void` — update a workspace's manifest in place without touching the container. Validates name-uniqueness across the fleet (excluding the row's own id). Used by the Saved-tab Resume flow to apply edited fields before calling `workspace:start`. Container-level fields (env, image, authMode, resources, mounts) won't take effect until the container is recreated — Phase 2's restart-to-apply banner surfaces this. `mounts` runs through the same `validateExtraMounts` check as create when it differs from the stored mounts (`mountsForWrite`); unchanged mounts skip the host (filesystem) checks but still pass the container-path rules, so a rename or committee toggle doesn't fail while a mount folder is temporarily missing (`toDockerMounts` re-checks at create). `mounts` is **authoritative**: an edit that omits `mounts` (every mount row removed in the form) writes `undefined` and clears them, same as any other field this handler fully replaces.
- `workspace:stop(containerId)` → `void` — stop with 5s grace; ignores 304/404.
- `workspace:pause(containerId)` → `void` — `docker pause` (cgroups freezer). Idempotent: 409 (already paused) and 404 (container gone) are treated as no-ops.
- `workspace:remove(containerId, opts?: { deleteState? })` → `void` — force-remove; if `deleteState`, also `rm -rf <userData>/state/<id>` (manifest, transcripts, broker socket). The renderer additionally calls `vault:deleteAllForWorkspace(id)` so secrets don't leak past the state dir.
- **`kind: 'remote'`** (a bookmark of a `fleet-host`, §7 *Remote workspaces*): `workspace:list` includes one row per bookmark, `running` or `unreachable`; `workspace:remove` forgets the bookmark (deletes `<userData>/state/<id>/`, including this desktop's `sessions.json`; the GitHub token and the host are untouched); `start`/`pause`/`stop`/`ensureImage` reject with "This remote workspace is managed by its deployment". `workspace:create` and `workspace:writeManifest` refuse a spec whose kind is `remote` or that would overwrite a remote manifest (`assertNotRemoteSpec`; bookmarks come only from `remote:add`/`remote:rename`, so a renderer-built spec can't point a bookmark, and the token, at an arbitrary URL). `workspace:remove` refuses when its `containerId` and `opts.id` are both set, differ and resolve to different kinds (`resolveRemoveKind`).
- `workspace:create` and `workspace:writeManifest` accept a `launcher` field (local workspaces only, #253). The manifest parser applies a strict allowlist (`sanitizeLauncher`); `mode: 'wsl'` is silently dropped on non-win32.
- `workspace:ensureImage(channelId, image?)` → progress over `workspace:ensureImage:progress:${channelId}`. Pulls `image` — the **user-selected ref** the create/resume flow passes (blank/omitted falls back to the base `RUNNER_IMAGE`). This is what lets a brand-new image (e.g. the devops runner, or any registry ref typed in the picker) get pulled here, with live progress in the modal, instead of 404'ing later at `docker create`. Always asks the registry, so improvements to `:latest` (broker landing, claude version bumps) reach existing users on subsequent creates. Docker's pull semantics no-op when local layers match the remote digest. **Pull errors are honest:** the daemon reports a failure (auth denial, `manifest unknown`, a mid-layer rate-limit) as an *in-stream* JSON line carrying `error`/`errorDetail` and then ends the stream cleanly, so `docker-modem`'s `followProgress` would otherwise resolve as success and the caller would silently keep — or recreate against — a stale image. `ensureImage` scans progress events for that error (`imagePull.ts:pullErrorFromEvent`) and rejects. If the registry is unreachable (or a pull errors) **and a local copy exists**, it falls back to the cached image with a warning; if neither, the error propagates so the caller can surface it. Every failure is logged to the error DB as `image-pull-failed` (level `warn` when a cached copy exists, else `error`) so a recurring "image lookup broke" leaves durable evidence. (`createWorkspace` resolves the image as `spec.image?.trim() || RUNNER_IMAGE`, so a blank ref can't slip through to `docker create`.)
- `workspace:resumeImageStale(channelId, id)` → `boolean`, progress over `workspace:ensureImage:progress:${channelId}`. Resume-time image refresh: reads the workspace manifest, `ensureImage`s its ref (so a newer `:latest` is downloaded), then reports whether the container is now running a **stale** image — its baked image id no longer matches what the ref resolves to locally. Scoped to **stopped** containers only (`imagePull.ts:shouldCheckResumeImage` — running/paused hold live/suspended sessions a recreate would destroy; local workspaces have no image), so it returns false for anything else. The renderer's Saved-tab Resume uses this: a `true` verdict triggers the same `applyContainerEdit` recreate as the restart-to-apply banner, so the resume runs the newer image instead of silently reusing the old container's. The verdict is fail-safe — absent/live container or unresolvable ids ⇒ false ⇒ plain start.

### Local workspace (#253)
WSL discovery and probing channels (win32 only; read-only, no side effects):
- `local:listWslDistros` → `{ distros: string[]; defaultDistro: string | null }` — enumerates installed WSL distributions by parsing `wsl.exe --list --verbose` (UTF-16LE output), filtering utility distros (docker-desktop, rancher-desktop, podman-machine). Returns `{ distros: [], defaultDistro: null }` on non-win32 or when WSL is absent.
- `local:probeWslDistro(distro)` → `{ shells: string[]; loginShell: string; home: string; claudePath: string | null; interopEnabled: boolean }` — probes the named distro for its login shell (`getent passwd`), `$HOME`, available shells from `/etc/shells`, Windows interop status (**#259**: the binfmt_misc handler under either the legacy `WSLInterop` name or the newer `WSLInterop-late` one that systemd-enabled distros register, and only when that entry's first line reads `enabled` — a registration can be switched off without the file disappearing), and a claude binary resolved via the same ladder as the host resolver. Rejects on non-win32; rejects if the distro name contains characters outside `[\w.-]`.

### Images
- `images:list` → `ImageEntry[]` — every image known to the library, including labels.
- `images:remove(ref)` → `void` — remove an image entry. The image itself is not deleted from the Docker daemon; only the library entry goes away.

### Sessions
Two distinct things share this namespace: the per-workspace **terminal-tab inventory** (`read`/`write`) and the global **Sessions table** (`list`/`rename`/`delete`/`resume`).

The tab inventory is the tab list shown above the terminal body. Renderer-owned read/write of the whole file; main has no notion of tab lifecycle.
- `sessions:read(workspaceId)` → `SessionInventory` — read `<userData>/state/<id>/sessions.json`. Returns an empty inventory (`{ version: 1, sessions: [], nextNum: 2 }`) if the file is missing or malformed.
- `sessions:write(workspaceId, inventory)` → `void` — atomic write of the whole inventory.

The Sessions table (#3) is a global, workspace-filterable list of every Claude session the watcher has indexed, each resumable via `claude --resume`. Backed by the sqlite `sessions` table (see §7 *JSONL→SQLite cache*).
- `sessions:list(workspaceId?)` → `SessionListItem[]` — newest-active first. Omit `workspaceId` for the global list; pass it to scope to one workspace. Each item is the `sessions` row (`id`, `workspaceId`, `aiTitle`, `firstUserMessage`, `userSetName`, `startedAt`, `lastActiveAt`) plus derived `eventCount` + `usd` (one grouped pass over `events`, no N+1), overlaid with `workspaceName`, `workspaceColorHue`, `workspaceState`, `tags: string[]` (tags from the session's latest tagged summary chapter, relevance-ordered; `[]` when no summary chapter exists yet — fetched via one grouped query over `session_summaries`, no N+1), and `capture: boolean` (true iff `isCaptureFork(id)` — a knowledge-capture fork, #483 — so the Sessions rail can file it under its own group; see *Open / Recent grouping* below). **Eligibility:** a session appears iff its workspace's manifest still exists — a truly-deleted workspace (manifest removed) drops out of `listAllWorkspaces` and its sessions are filtered; a closed-but-kept workspace (manifest present, no live container → state `'deleted'`) still appears. Enforced in the IPC layer, the only place that knows about on-disk manifests. The renderer's display title is `userSetName ?? aiTitle ?? firstUserMessage ?? '(untitled)'`.
- `sessions:listRemote(req?: { origin, after })` → `{ pages: SourcePage<SessionListItem>[], unavailable: { origin, name, reason }[] }` (remote-workspaces (d), `federation/federationIpc.ts`). With no argument, it returns the first page (≤ 200, newest first) of every remote bookmark's sessions. With `origin` (a ULID) alone, it returns that host's first page; with `origin` and `after` (`{ lastActiveAt: safe integer ≥ 0, id: [A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,128} }`), it returns that host's next keyset page; any other shape is refused, and extra fields are dropped. Rows carry `origin` (the remote workspace id) and the bookmark's name, hue and state, never the host's. `sessions:list` is unchanged and its rows are local (`origin` absent). The renderer merges the two with `preload/federation.ts` `mergeSessionPages`: order `COALESCE(lastActiveAt, 0) DESC, id` (byte order), then origin; dedup on `(workspaceId, id)` with local first, then origins in byte order. A host whose cached liveness (§6 *Remote*, the 15 s probe) is unprobed or down answers `checking` or `unreachable` at once, with no network call; one that errs or exceeds 3 s (`SOURCE_TIMEOUT_MS`, passed to the request and raced) is listed under `unavailable` (`unreachable`, `checking`, `timeout`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `standby`, `not-signed-in`, `protocol`) and its rows are absent. Identical concurrent calls share one request; nothing is cached after it settles (live-only). A remote row filed under another workspace id is dropped. Main keeps no cursor state, so a renderer can send any well-formed cursor to a bookmarked host under the user's own bearer; the host scopes every read to its own workspace.
- `history:search(query, { scope, limit? })` → `{ hits: SearchHitItem[], unavailable }` (remote-workspaces (d2), `federation/federationIpc.ts`; `window.api.history.search`). Semantic search over transcript turns and summary chapters, local and remote, merged by score. `query` is 1–2000 characters (trimmed); `scope` is `'all'` or a workspace id (`[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,128}`); `limit` is clamped to 1–50 (default 20). Main embeds the query once, through the same `makeEmbedder` selector the indexer uses (`setFederatedEmbedder`); no embedder throws `transcript search index is unavailable` and nothing is sent. The local half is `searchEmbeddings` over the local (non-remote) manifests in scope; a remote half is the host's `history:search` RPC with only the query vector, model id and limit (`[{ vector: number[384], modelId, limit }]`), sent only to the remote bookmarks in scope: under `'all'` every bookmark, under a workspace id only that one, and none for a local workspace. No local text, id or hit is sent. The same liveness gate, 3 s bound, in-flight dedupe and reply validation as `sessions:listRemote` apply (`parseSearchReply`: ≤ 50 hits, else `protocol`; a hit with a bad id, another workspace id, a bad `kind` or `ts`, or a score that is non-finite or outside ±1.0001 is dropped; text has control and format characters other than newline and tab blanked and is capped at 1000). `SearchHitItem` is `{ origin, sessionId, workspaceId, workspaceName, kind, ts, text, score }`; `workspaceName` is the manifest's or bookmark's name, never the reply's. Hits merge by score descending, then origin, `sessionId` (byte order) and `ts`, cut at `limit`, not deduplicated (`federation/searchMerge.ts`). Scores are comparable because every source scores cosine similarity on the same model (§7 *JSONL→SQLite cache*, *Search*). A host whose reply names another `modelId` is dropped and listed with reason `model`; so is a host that refuses the query's model id (400 `model mismatch: …`, which `RemoteClient` maps to `RemoteError` kind `model`); a host with search turned off answers 503 `search unavailable` (`SEARCH_UNAVAILABLE`), which `RemoteClient` maps to `RemoteError` kind `disabled` and is listed with reason `disabled`. Every other reason is as in `sessions:listRemote`. Nothing is kept after the call (live-only).
- `sessions:rename(sessionId, name)` → `void` — set the manual name override (`user_set_name`); an empty/whitespace name clears it back to NULL so the auto title resurfaces.
- `sessions:delete(workspaceId, sessionId)` → `void` — a remote workspace id is a no-op. Otherwise drop the session's `events`, `broker_sessions`, and `sessions` rows, then unlink the on-disk transcript (`<state>/<id>/.claude/projects/-workspace/<sessionId>.jsonl`). The watcher's `unlink` handler only clears its in-memory offset state, so the DB rows (including the saved `transcript_offsets` row) are removed explicitly. Best-effort unlink (a missing file is fine).
- `sessions:resume(workspaceId)` → `{ containerId } | null` — for a remote bookmark id, returns `{ containerId: <id> }` without starting anything; the resume tab attaches with `--resume`, which needs `session` on that host (the host tier-checks the `CREATE`). Otherwise: bring the workspace's container up (`startWorkspace` unpauses a paused one, starts a stopped one, no-ops a running one) and return its `containerId` so the renderer can open a resume tab. **Null** when the container is gone and can't be brought up here (e.g. closed-but-kept workspace with no recreatable container) — the renderer surfaces a non-fatal "couldn't resume" notice. The actual `claude --resume <id>` happens through the normal attach flow (see §6 *PTY*).

### Vault
Per-workspace secret storage backed by Electron `safeStorage` (see §4 *Stack* for the WSL rationale). Profiles are gone; the whole vault is a single JSON object `{ "<workspaceId>": { "<envVarName>": "<value>" } }`, encrypted via `safeStorage.encryptString` and written base64 to `<userData>/secrets.enc`. The decrypted store is cached in memory; mutations are serialized through a write-lock so concurrent `setSecret`/`deleteSecret` calls (the env editor writing several rows) can't clobber each other. The renderer never sees a secret value it didn't just write, except when the user clicks the env editor's show button (see §9) — values come back over `vault:getSecret`, and the main process consumes them directly when constructing the container env via `resolveEnv` (`src/main/vault.ts`). Migration: secrets previously in keytar are NOT carried over (re-enter once; OAuth workspaces store none).
- `vault:available` → `boolean` — `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()`, cached. False only when even the AES fallback is unavailable; the API-key auth mode degrades to disabled in that case.
- `vault:listKeys(workspaceId)` → `string[]` — the secret-env-var keys stored for the workspace (object keys of its bag).
- `vault:getSecret(workspaceId, key)` → `string | null` — fetch a single value. Null when missing. The renderer's only caller is the env editor's user-initiated reveal (`secretReveal.ts`).
- `vault:setSecret(workspaceId, key, value)` → `void` — upsert into the workspace's bag. Throws when encryption isn't available.
- `vault:deleteSecret(workspaceId, key)` → `void` — delete a single value; drops the workspace's bag when its last key is removed.
- `vault:deleteAllForWorkspace(workspaceId)` → `void` — purge the workspace's whole bag. Called at workspace-delete time so credentials don't outlive the manifest.

### Identity
GitHub sign-in for the desktop (remote-workspaces sub-project (b), `src/main/identity/`, IPC in `src/main/identityIpc.ts`). The OAuth token lives in the host-only vault scope `identity:github` (§7 *Vault layout*) and **never crosses IPC**. The renderer sees only `AccountStatus` (`src/preload/identityTypes.ts`): `{ configured, persisted, signedIn, identity: { provider, login, id, name, displayName, avatarUrl, teams } | null, scopes, packagesAccess, sso: { url, org, orgCount, appSettingsUrl } | null, notice }`. `avatarUrl` is null unless it is `https:`.
- `identity:status({ refresh? })` → `AccountStatus`. Only a literal own `refresh: true` is honoured; other fields are dropped. With `refresh: true` it re-verifies the saved token with GitHub (`GET /user`, `GET /user/teams`). A 401 clears the token only if it is still the stored one, and sets `notice`. Any other failure keeps the cached profile and sets `notice`.
- `identity:signInStart()` → `{ userCode, verificationUri, expiresAt, intervalMs }`. It starts the GitHub OAuth **device flow** (`POST https://github.com/login/device/code`, scopes `read:org read:packages`) with claude-fleet's own OAuth app. The device code stays in main. With no client id it throws `not-configured: …`. A new start discards any earlier flow.
- `identity:signInPoll()` → `{ state: 'pending', intervalMs } | { state: 'done', status } | { state: 'error', message }`. Each call is one step. Main never polls `POST https://github.com/login/oauth/access_token` faster than GitHub's interval (it honours `slow_down`, and adds 5 s after an HTTP 429/5xx), keeps at most one request in flight, and treats a network blip as `pending`. The returned `intervalMs` is capped at 60 s and at the time left before `expiresAt`; polling early is answered locally. On success it probes the profile and saves the profile, then the token. A profile read that fails stores nothing and returns `error`.
- `identity:signInCancel()` → `void`: abandons the pending flow.
- `identity:signOut()` → `void`: purges the `identity:github` scope. The token isn't revoked on GitHub (that needs the client secret), so the UI links GitHub's Authorized OAuth Apps page.

Vault writes and clears in `IdentityService` run on one mutex, and `status()` and `packagesToken()` read through it. Sign-out, cancel and a new start bump an epoch, so a refresh or sign-in that started earlier never saves after them; a sign-out queued behind an in-flight save still wins.

The pure modules (`types`, `grants`, `githubHeaders`, `githubProvider`, `verifyCache`, `deviceFlow`, `clientId`, `identityService`) import no Electron (`noElectron.test.ts`), so `fleet-host` reuses them. `grants.resolveTier(who, { grants, machines })` returns the highest matching tier (`read` < `steer` < `session`) or `null`, meaning no access. Users resolve by team or login; machines resolve only by the verified `appId` (`MachineIdentity.appId`), and only after the provider proved the App installation's repositories all belong to the rule's org. `/user/teams` failures other than an SSO-hinted 403 or a missing-`read:org` 403 throw `upstream` and are never cached; all GitHub requests use `redirect: 'error'`; cached identities are frozen.

### Remote
Add and rename remote workspaces (`src/main/remoteIpc.ts`, payload types `src/preload/remoteTypes.ts`). No response carries a token; every reply is built field by field.
- `remote:probe(url)` → `RemoteProbe`. `url` must be a string of at most 2048 chars; it is canonicalised (`remoteUrl.normalizeHostUrl`: `http(s)` only, no credentials, query or fragment, trailing slashes dropped). Order: unauthenticated `GET /healthz` (a non-fleet-host or unreachable URL stops here, with no dialog and no token) → token consent (§9) → `GET /whoami`. `RemoteProbeOk` is `{ ok: true, url, workspace: { id, name, description, color }, user: { login, name, avatarUrl }, tier, warning, existing, existingUrl }`: `name` ≤ 64 and `description` ≤ 500 chars with control characters stripped, `color` only when `hue` is finite in 0–360, `warning` is the plain-http text or null, `existingUrl` is the current bookmark URL when this workspace id is bookmarked at a different URL. `RemoteProbeErr` is `{ ok: false, kind, message, ssoUrl?, existingUrl? }`, `kind` one of `invalid-url`, `not-signed-in`, `unreachable` (check network/VPN), `unauthorized`, `forbidden` (no grant: ask the host's admins for a team), `sso` (a 403 while `AccountStatus.sso` is set: "Authorise claude-fleet for *org*", with GitHub's link), `standby`, `protocol`, `clash`, `moved`, `declined`. Host text in messages passes `RemoteClient.redact`; other errors become a fixed string.
- `remote:add(url, opts?: { sameHostNewAddress?: boolean })` → `RemoteProbe`. Probes, then writes the bookmark manifest under the host-reported workspace id. `opts` is strict (only that boolean key). Re-adding a known id at the same URL refreshes its tier and login; at a different URL it returns `moved` (with `existingUrl`) unless `sameHostNewAddress: true`, which then needs main's re-point confirm (§9) and keeps the local name. An id that belongs to a local or container workspace returns `clash`. A new bookmark's name is the host's name, deduplicated with `-2`, `-3`, ….
- `remote:rename(id, name)` → `void`. Local name only (validated like any workspace name, unique across the fleet); the host keeps its own.
- `remote:liveSessions(id)` → `{ id, alive, mine }[]` — the host's `sessions:live`, capped at 1000 entries. An entry is dropped unless `id` matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$` (the host's `validSessionId`) and `alive` is a boolean; `mine` must be a boolean when present (absent ⇒ `false`).

`rename` and `liveSessions` require a renderer-valid id whose manifest is `kind: 'remote'`. Mock mode lists no remote workspaces.

### PTY
- `pty:attach(containerId, brokerSessionId, cols, rows, resumeOf?, foreground?)` → `ptyHandleId: string` — opens a connection to the workspace's in-container broker (Unix socket at `<state>/<id>/broker/broker.sock`) and either re-attaches to an existing broker session or creates one. `brokerSessionId` is the stable id from `sessions.json` (so re-attach across an app restart finds the same live PTY). Main retains a `BrokerClient` plus the resulting Duplex, returns an opaque `ptyHandleId` the renderer uses for subsequent input/resize/detach calls. **`resumeOf`** (a Claude session UUID) makes this a *resume* attach: the broker `CREATE` spawns `claude --resume <resumeOf>` instead of a bare `claude`. **Session identity is host-assigned (#195):** when CREATE runs without `resumeOf`, the host generates a UUID and passes `--session-id <uuid>`, so the broker→claude mapping is learned **deterministically at CREATE time** for both paths — never guessed from JSONL appearance order (the legacy pending-attach FIFO could pair a new JSONL with the wrong tab; a wrong `broker_sessions` row makes a later tab Refresh silently resume a different conversation). Learning happens only when CREATE actually runs — a plain ATTACH to a live session never relearns, since the running claude keeps whatever id it already has. The pending-attach queue remains as a fallback pairing for sessions the host did not name (e.g. seeded in e2e); an ambiguous consume is logged as `mapping-ambiguous-consume`. `resumeOf` only takes effect at CREATE time; on a re-attach where the broker session is already alive, ATTACH succeeds first and the resume args are correctly ignored (no second claude spawned). **`foreground`** is true when the attaching tab is visible (active tab of the selected workspace); only the local backend reads it, to spawn that tab ahead of hidden ones (see *Local spawn queue*, §7). The renderer seeds its resize latch, its last-observed size and its repaint-nudge baseline with the cols/rows it sent, then runs its resize reconcile once, so a pane that re-fit while the attach was in flight still gets the resize push, the repaint and the Ctrl+L nudge. **On failure** the handler captures the last ~100 lines of broker stdout/stderr via `docker logs` and writes them to `error.log` under a `pty-attach-failed` entry alongside the thrown message — the broker is otherwise invisible to the host, and the worst-case "ATTACHED timed out" + "unsolicited frame type 4" pattern after a pause/resume is impossible to diagnose without seeing what the broker was actually doing.

Broker RPC timeout is 30s (`RPC_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/main/broker.ts`). 10s was the original budget but routinely fired during the first ATTACH after a workspace pause/resume — the broker's `CREATE` path spawns the `claude` binary via `pty.StartWithSize`, and that first spawn (auth checks, MCP server warm-up, occasional network call) regularly takes 15–25s. The host's late-arriving response then lands with no waiter, producing the "unsolicited frame type 4" warning. 30s covers the observed worst case with margin without making honestly-stuck sessions hang the UI indefinitely.
- `pty:input(ptyHandleId, data: string, ts?: number)` → `void` — write user input to the broker as an INPUT frame on the channel. `ts` is the renderer's epoch-ms keystroke stamp (present only while perf recording is on); main records `receipt − ts` as an `input_hop` latency sample.
- `pty:resize(ptyHandleId, cols, rows)` → `{ ok, cols, rows, actual?, reason? }` — send a RESIZE frame and **report the outcome**. `ok: false` with `reason: 'unknown-handle' | 'not-delivered' | 'error'` means the resize never reached the PTY — the normal cases being the attach/re-attach window, a reaped handle after tab-close, and an exited PTY. The renderer's resize pusher (`resizePusher.ts`) advances its "last delivered size" latch **only** on `ok`, retrying otherwise up to a bounded attempt count; recording a dropped resize as delivered pins claude at a stale width for the life of the session, because the no-op-resize dedupe then suppresses every later push at that same size. `actual` is the size the PTY reports holding afterwards, when the backend can read it back (`PtyHandle.getSize`, node-pty only — the container/broker path cannot). A mismatch between the requested and actual size is logged as `width-divergence`; see §*Width agreement* below.
- `pty:detach(ptyHandleId)` → `void` — send a DETACH frame (session lives on inside the broker) and close the socket.
- `pty:closeSession(ptyHandleId)` → `boolean` — send a **CLOSE** frame, which terminates the broker session entirely (kills the PTY / claude and drops it from the broker's session map), then closes the socket. Returns whether a live handle was found. CLOSE is only honored on a channel the calling connection actually holds, so this must go through the attached `PtyHandle` (the renderer addresses it by `ptyHandleId`). Used by the **loadout reload** (§7): close the session, then `pty:attach` the *same* broker session id with `resumeOf` ⇒ the broker has no such session ⇒ it `CREATE`s it with `claude --resume <uuid>`, so the same tab resumes the conversation under the freshly-installed config.
- **Remote attach.** On a `kind: 'remote'` workspace `pty:attach` re-fetches `/whoami` (tier refresh, §7), opens the host's `/attach` WebSocket and drives it with the same `BrokerClient` (constructed over `ws.createWebSocketStream`; one `write` per whole frame, `INPUT` split at `MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD − 4`). ATTACH → "no such session" → CREATE → ATTACH as for containers, but CREATE runs only when the fresh tier is `session`; otherwise the tab fails with "This session is not running on the host, and your tier cannot start one". No `broker_sessions` row is written (remote data is live-only). A host close code ends the tab with fixed text (4401 sign in again, 4403 access removed, 1013 too slow, 1008 limit, 1001/1011 restarting); the host's reason string is never shown. `BrokerClient` destroys a generic Duplex endpoint on `end` (the ws stream is `autoDestroy: false`) and rejects every pending RPC when its transport closes, for containers too — so `waitForBrokerReady` against a broker that accepts then closes fails each attempt at once (12 × 250 ms ≈ 3 s) instead of waiting out per-attempt timeouts.
- `pty:closeSessionByBroker(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)` → `boolean` — same reap as `pty:closeSession` (broker CLOSE ⇒ claude exits; for the local backend, the child process is killed) but addressed by `(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)` instead of a per-attach `ptyHandleId`. For a local tab whose attach is still queued it cancels that pending spawn (returning `true` even with no live handle). Used by **tab-close** (`TerminalPane`), which runs from the parent pane and only knows the stable broker session id, not the child `TerminalSession`'s live handle. Main resolves the pair to its live handle (`findLiveHandleId` — at most one per workspace+broker session) and reaps it. Awaited *before* the tab is dropped from renderer state so the child's unmount `pty:detach` (which for local leaves claude alive) cannot win the race and re-orphan the process. This is the **explicit tab-close** path only; app quit does **not** reap (`before-quit` leaves broker sessions alive so a relaunch re-attaches — §11 broker durability). On a `kind: 'remote'` workspace it only **detaches** (`ptyHandleLookup.tabCloseVerb`): a host session is shared, and outlives one desktop's tab.
- `pty:endSessionByBroker(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)` → `boolean` — always CLOSEs the session behind the live handle. The remote tab menu's **End session** uses it; the host refuses CLOSE from anyone but the session's creator. On a remote the desktop waits at most 3 s for `CLOSED`, then drops the socket.

Per-session events from main to renderer:
- `pty:data:${sessionId}` — `Buffer` chunks from the container's stdout/stderr, plus an epoch-ms send stamp as a second argument (the chunk itself stays a raw Buffer) for the renderer's `output_hop` measurement.
- `pty:end:${sessionId}` — stream ended.
- `pty:error:${sessionId}` — stream error (stringified).

The renderer's `window.api.pty.onData/onEnd` register listeners and return unsubscribe functions.

### Ports
Dev-server preview: auto-detects listening ports inside a running container and relays browser traffic over the broker socket (see §5 *Broker frame protocol* for `DIAL`/`DIALED`/`LISTPORTS`/`PORTS`/`KILLPORT`/`KILLED` frames; see §8 *Dev-server preview* for the user flow; implementation in `src/main/portforward.ts`).

`ServingPort` shape: `{ port: number, pid: number | null, cmdline: string | null, sessionId: string | null, firstSeenAt: number, label: string | null }` (epoch ms; pid/cmdline absent when broker could not attribute ownership; `sessionId` = owning broker session id, null when unattributed). The snapshot holds only HTTP-probe-passed ports. `label` is the `expose_port` label when declared — added by main from `PortExposureService`, not part of the broker snapshot.

- `ports:detected` (main → renderer event) — `{ workspaceId, port, label, reason, cmdline, declared }`. Fired by `PortExposureService` (`src/main/portExposure.ts`), **not** by raw detection: when a port declared via the fleet-state `expose_port` tool passes the HTTP probe (`declared: true`), or when the undeclared fallback fires (`declared: false`; only when `ports.undeclaredToastAfterSec` > 0, §*Settings*). Ports the scan finds but nobody declared appear only in the Serving rail. The renderer shows a sticky toast — *"<workspace> wants to show you <label> on :<port> — <reason> · <cmdline>"* (reason/cmdline each appended only if present), or for the fallback *"Server on port N in <workspace> (<cmdline>)"* with eyebrow `Preview · undeclared` — with an **Open preview** button, keyed `port:<workspaceId>:<port>` so a re-fire replaces rather than stacks. Closing it with ✕ calls `ports:dismiss`.
- `local:claude-update-available` (main → renderer event) — `{ workspaceId, distro, pinned: { path, version|null }, best: { path, version } }`. Fired (fire-and-forget, never blocking the start path) after `workspace:start` / `sessions:resume` brings up a **wsl-launcher** workspace whose distro has a claude strictly newer than the manifest-pinned `launcher.claudePath` (`wslClaudeFreshness.ts`: version-compares the pinned path, the login shell's `command -v claude`, and the well-known install dirs in one wsl.exe batch; a pinned binary that no longer runs makes any working candidate an offer). The renderer shows a sticky toast keyed `claude-update:<workspaceId>` with **Use newer** / **Keep** actions; plain ✕ snoozes until the next start.
- `local:claude-update-decision` (invoke) — `(workspaceId, { action: 'adopt', path } | { action: 'ignore', version })`. adopt repins `launcher.claudePath` (new sessions only; live PTYs keep their running binary) and clears `ignoreClaudeVersion`; ignore persists `launcher.ignoreClaudeVersion` so the toast only returns for something strictly newer.
- `ports:open(workspaceId, containerPort, via?)` → `{ hostPort: number | null }` — `via` (`'popup' | 'rail' | 'fallback'`; anything else ⇒ `'rail'`) is recorded on the port's `port_exposures` row on every successful open. For a **local** workspace's declared port (no broker; `PortExposureService.isLocalServing`) main re-probes `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `::1:<port>` from the host and opens `http://localhost:<port>` directly — no forward. Otherwise it first re-probes the container port (the toast is sticky; the server may be gone by click time) and returns `{ hostPort: null }` when nothing answers, which the renderer surfaces as an error toast instead of opening a dead tab; then creates (or reuses) a `PortForward` for `containerPort`: a loopback `net.Server` bound to `127.0.0.1:0` (ephemeral host port). Each inbound browser connection gets a fresh broker channel via `DIAL`, then traffic flows through the existing `INPUT`/`OUTPUT` relay. Calls `shell.openExternal('http://127.0.0.1:<hostPort>')` and returns `hostPort`. In `MOCK_MODE` (no broker) the handler returns a stub host port without dialing.
- `ports:dismiss(workspaceId, port)` (invoke) — the user closed a preview toast with ✕; records outcome `dismissed` unless the row already has one. The port stays in the rail.
- `ports:changed` (main → renderer event) — `{ workspaceId, ports: ServingPort[] }`. Broadcast on every membership or owner change in the Serving snapshot for a workspace: a port was added (probe passed), removed (stopped listening), its pid changed (server restart resets `firstSeenAt`), or its `sessionId` resolved late (updated in-place — `firstSeenAt` preserved; only a pid change resets it). An **empty array** is broadcast when the workspace's monitor stops (pause/stop/remove), so listeners clear immediately rather than waiting for a stale row to time out. Full per-workspace replace — the renderer discards its previous list for `workspaceId` and replaces it with the new one.
- `ports:list()` → `Array<{ workspaceId, ports: ServingPort[] }>` — returns the current Serving snapshot across all monitored workspaces. Called once at renderer mount/reload to seed the rail without waiting for the next poll tick. Workspaces with no serving ports are omitted.
- `ports:kill(workspaceId, port)` → `{ ok: boolean, error?: string }` — routes a `KILLPORT` frame over the target workspace's own broker socket. The broker re-resolves port→PID at kill time (never trusts the host); kill travels only over that workspace's socket. The snapshot row disappears via the next `ports:changed` once the socket closes. On a pre-KILLPORT broker the unknown frame is logged and dropped, the RPC times out, and the handler maps any `/timed out/` error to `{ ok: false, error: 'runner image too old — recreate the workspace to enable kill' }`; the renderer toasts this verbatim.

**`PortForwardManager`** (replaces the earlier standalone `PortMonitor` class; owns the Serving snapshot) — manages one `PortMonitor` per running workspace. Calls `reconcile(runningWorkspaceIds)` on workspace state changes: starts a monitor for any new running workspace, stops one for any that left. Each monitor polls `LISTPORTS` every 3s via a dedicated `BrokerClient`, diffs against the last-seen set, HTTP-probes newly-appeared ports (broker `DIAL` + minimal `GET / HTTP/1.0`, reply must start with `HTTP/`), and maintains the per-workspace `serving` map. State transitions: probe-pass → add to snapshot (+ fire `onDetected` for the toast); port disappears → remove from snapshot; pid changes → replace the row and reset `firstSeenAt`; monitor stops → snapshot cleared + `onChanged(workspaceId, [])` broadcast. A stale in-flight poll that resolves after `reconcile` has stopped the monitor is detected by identity check and discarded — it cannot resurrect rows. `LISTPORTS` reports every LISTEN socket in the container (Chromium DevTools ports, node inspectors, short-lived tool servers), and unprobed toasts gave the user previews that opened to nothing. A port whose probe fails is re-probed on the next ticks; after `MAX_PROBE_ATTEMPTS` (3) consecutive failures it is written off (kept in the seen-set, never reported) until it stops listening and returns. **`PortForward`** — one per forwarded container port. The `net.Server` is torn down automatically when the workspace pauses/stops/is removed. In `MOCK_MODE` the `ports:detected`/`ports:changed` events are driven by a scheduled fake-ports sequence (10 s / 25 s) unless `CLAUDE_FLEET_E2E=1`, in which case mock ports are suppressed and e2e drives state via the `__test:setServingPorts` IPC handle instead; `ports:open` returns a stub. The mock's first fake port (port 3000) is stamped with the workspace's first inventory session id via a `resolveSessionId` callback wired in `ipc.ts` (`sessions.readInventory(workspaceId).sessions[0]?.id`) — so the session chip is exercisable under `CLAUDE_FLEET_MOCK=1` without a container. The second fake port (port 8765) carries no attribution and stays unattributed.

**`PortExposureService`** (`src/main/portExposure.ts`) — joins `expose_port` declarations with the Serving snapshots (`ports:changed` feeds `onSnapshot`; `PortForwardManager.onDetected` is unused). Rules: ports in the kernel ephemeral range **32768–60999** (what `listen(0)` hands out — test fixtures) are rejected by `expose_port` and never recorded or toasted when scanned; a declaration for a port not yet serving waits **30s** for it to pass the probe, then closes as `not_serving`; `expose_port` is limited to **10 calls/min per workspace**; re-declaring a shown port updates its label without a second popup. **Local** workspaces have no scan: a declared port is probed host-side (`localPortProbe.ts`, `GET / HTTP/1.0` to `127.0.0.1` or `::1`) every 3s; it pops up on the first pass and ends after two consecutive failures. Each port appearance (first scan-pass or declaration, whichever is first, until it stops listening) is one `port_exposures` row (§7) written through `PortExposureStore`; at startup `sweep()` closes rows a previous run left open (`not_serving` if never probed, else `ignored`) and prunes rows that ended more than 90 days ago. Startup sweep runs exactly once, kicked off from `registerIpc` after the state DB opens (not from `index.ts`); until it settles, `PortExposureService`'s own DB writes queue behind a `gatedSink` so a port appearing during startup can't be closed by the sweep that's cleaning up the *previous* run.

### Observability
- `observability:eventsForSession(sessionId, sinceEventId?, limit?)` → `EventRow[]` — rows from the `events` table for the given session, ordered by `id` ascending, restricted to `id > sinceEventId`. Caller polls with the highest `id` it has seen to get incremental updates. Returns up to `limit` rows (default 500).
- `observability:summaryForWorkspace(workspaceId)` → `WorkspaceSummary | null` — picks the most-recently-active Claude session in the workspace and returns `{ sessionId, title, model, startedAt, lastActiveAt, eventCount, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadInputTokens, cacheCreationInputTokens, usd, lastTurnContextTokens, contextWindowTokens, topTools[], recentToolCalls[], costSeries[] }`. `recentToolCalls[]` is the latest tool calls newest-first (`{ name, input, durationMs, status: 'ok'|'error'|'pending', ts }` — duration/status from the tool_use→tool_result match). `costSeries[]` is per-turn USD over recent assistant turns, oldest→newest (the sparkline series; each assistant event with usage is one turn, priced per-row so mixed models/tiers are correct). Returns null when no events have been ingested for the workspace yet. `lastTurnContextTokens` is `input + cache_read + cache_creation` from the most recent assistant event — a context-window-fullness proxy that drives the terminal-pane context bar; null when no assistant event has been seen yet. `contextWindowTokens` is the session's effective context window (200K / 1M, see §5 *Main pane* for derivation rules). App.tsx fires this once per workspace at mount/resubscribe and then every 30s as a safety net; the hot path is the live `observability:summary` push (see §5 *Centralized observability distribution*). The summary and rolling-spend reads are served from event-invalidated in-memory async caches (`asyncKeyedCache`, `src/main/asyncCache.ts`), cleared on DB open; the rolling sum is keyed by 15 s bucket. Concurrent misses on a key share one in-flight recompute. A stale entry is recomputed and awaited, never served past its horizon. An `invalidate`/`clear` that lands while a recompute is in flight wins: that result goes to its callers but is not stored. A `markStale` that lands mid-recompute caps the stored result. Every invalidation runs after its write commits. A summary recompute (and `listSessions`) runs its queries in one read transaction, so they see one consistent state. `ingestBatch` calls `markStale(sessionId, 3s)` — a debounced invalidation: the cache entry is still served until the 3s grace elapses, so a streaming session recomputes at most once per 3s window rather than on every poll-after-every-event. Mid-burst pushes therefore serve summaries that may be up to 3s stale; the last push of a burst carries that stale data with nothing to follow it up. A trailing re-broadcast (`ipc.ts:makeTrailingRebroadcast`) fires once per workspace at grace+250ms after quiescence — by then the grace has fully elapsed and the cache recomputes fresh — delivering the final post-burst state. Hard freshness bound after quiescence: ≈3.25s. Hard `invalidate` is reserved for `deleteSession` and session rename, where stale data must not be served at all. The 30s TTL on summaries is a safety net only. Together these mean repeated renderer polls and the per-ingest broadcast cost one query per data change rather than one per poll, and the trailing re-broadcast adds one extra recompute per burst maximum. **Summary read path — rollup coverage (#390):** `max_context_tokens` (`observedMax`, drives `contextWindowFor`) is served from `session_stats` (a single PK seek, no events scan); `topTools[]` is served from `session_tool_counts` (bounded index scan, ORDER BY `count DESC, tool_name` for deterministic tie-breaking, no events scan). The only remaining `events` reads in a summary recompute are: `costSeries[]` (`costSeriesForSession` — LIMIT-bounded last-N assistant turns) and `tokenSeries[]` (`tokenSeriesForSession` — LIMIT-bounded last-N assistant turns), `recentToolCalls[]` (`recentToolCallsForSession` — bounded by `limit`), and `latestAssistant` (the most-recent assistant row, using `latestAssistantQuery` — an index seek on `idx_events_session_type(session_id, type)` + `ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1`, terminating at the first matching row).
- `sessions:resolveResumeTarget(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)` → `string | null` — resume-grade tab→conversation resolution: the claude session UUID this tab may `claude --resume`, or null unless the `broker_sessions` row is **verified** (learned deterministically at spawn — v7 column). Legacy FIFO-guessed rows never resolve here: resuming a guess silently swaps the tab onto a different conversation (#195). Display lookups stay on the summary endpoint below, which serves unverified rows fine.
- `observability:summaryForBrokerSession(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)` → `WorkspaceSummary | null` — per-tab variant. Resolves the broker→claude mapping in the `broker_sessions` table and returns that claude session's summary, or **null** when no mapping is known. **No workspace fallback at this layer** — a freshly-added tab carries an unmapped broker session id but legitimately has no data, and returning the workspace's most-recently-active session there surfaces the previous tab's numbers (the user-visible "new session shows the last session's info" bug). The renderer applies a workspace-summary fallback only for tabs loaded from `sessions.json` (where the mapping just hasn't caught up — pre-PR tabs, concurrent-attach skip cases); freshly-added tabs (the `+` button, close-last-auto-recreate) leave the pane on its empty state until the watcher learns a mapping and real per-tab data flows in.
- `inputwait:update` (main → renderer push) — `{ workspaceId, waitingSessionIds: string[] }`. The set of claude session UUIDs in that workspace currently blocked on `AskUserQuestion`. Sent whenever the set changes (a session enters or leaves the waiting state) and cleared on workspace stop, pause, or remove. Exposed as `window.api.observability.onInputWait(cb)`. Container/AskUserQuestion-only; see `sessionstatus:update` for the authoritative all-backend signal.
- `sessionstatus:update` (main → renderer push) — `{ statuses: Array<{ claudeSessionId, status: 'busy'|'idle'|'waiting', waitingFor? }> }`. The authoritative peer-status snapshot from claude's `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` files (#286, §5 *Authoritative session status*). Flat (not per-workspace) — claude session ids are globally unique; the renderer attributes them via its broker→claude mappings and merges them over the title glyph as the authoritative override. Sent whenever the reduced snapshot changes. Exposed as `window.api.observability.onSessionStatus(cb)`.
- `observability:summary` (main → renderer) — broadcast every time the watcher ingests new lines for a workspace. Payload: `{ workspaceId, summary: WorkspaceSummary | null }`, where `summary` is the same shape `summaryForWorkspace` returns. Exposed to the renderer as `window.api.observability.onSummary(cb)`, which returns an unsubscribe. One push per ingest batch (one JSONL flush ≈ one push); duplicate-only re-reads after compaction are suppressed at the watcher. The fan-out runs through `broadcastObservabilitySummary` (`src/main/observabilityBroadcast.ts`), which guards each target with `win.isDestroyed()`, `webContents.isDestroyed()`, AND a try/catch around `send` — during BrowserWindow teardown the render frame can be disposed while both destroyed-flags still read false, and `webContents.send` then throws "Render frame was disposed before WebFrameMain could be accessed". The watcher's emit path isn't an awaited handler, so an unswallowed throw unwinds into Node's EventEmitter internals; the per-target catch keeps one stale window from breaking the whole broadcast.
- `observability:getCost(sessionId)` → `{ inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadInputTokens, cacheCreationInputTokens, usd }` — token totals + USD for one session. USD is derived from the `events` rows grouped by `(model, service_tier)`; pricing comes from `src/main/pricing.ts` (hardcoded Claude 4.x rates, standard tier full price, batch tier 50%, unknown model/tier degrades to $0 + one-time `console.warn`). The pane reads the equivalent `usd` field from `summaryForWorkspace`; this endpoint exists for the sessions table (#3) and per-session detail views.
- `observability:getCostForWorkspace(workspaceId)` → same shape, aggregated across every session in the workspace.
- **Remote workspaces** (remote-workspaces (d)). `summaryForWorkspace` and `summaryForBrokerSession` route a remote bookmark's id (an in-memory `RemoteBackend.isRemote` lookup) to its host's `history:summary` (3 s bound, liveness-gated, validated by `federation/remoteRows.ts`). A remote tab gets the workspace's latest session, since the host keeps no tab mapping. An unavailable host answers null. Pushes come from the host's data-free `observability:summary` hint on `/events`: `federation/summaryRelay.ts` keeps one `/events` subscription per bookmark (re-synced every 15 s; a new one starts only while the bookmark's cached liveness is `running`, refining D58, and one that stops retrying, `retryInMs` null, is dropped so a later sync resubscribes), debounces hints 2 s per host, reads `history:summary`, and sends the usual `observability:summary` push; a failed read sends nothing, and App's 30 s safety poll takes the same routed path. Nothing remote is cached (live-only). `eventsForSession` and `getCost*` stay local (no renderer caller needs them remote).

### Error log
Both main and renderer hook the standard "uncaught" channels and forward each crash through a single sink to `<userData>/error.log`. Main installs `process.on('uncaughtException')` + `process.on('unhandledRejection')` directly. The renderer wires `window.addEventListener('error', …)` + `window.addEventListener('unhandledrejection', …)` in `src/renderer/src/main.tsx` *before* mounting React, so a crash during App's initial render still lands. Each row is one JSON object: `{ ts, source: 'main' | 'renderer', type, message, stack?, extra? }`. No rotation; users can delete the file at will.
- `app:logError({ type, message, stack?, extra? })` → `void` — renderer → main bridge. The renderer never writes the log directly (sandbox / cross-process consistency).
- `app:errorLogPath()` → `string` — absolute path of the log.
- `app:openErrorLog()` → `string` — open `error.log` in the OS default app (WSL-aware, same `explorer.exe` fallback as `fs:openPath`). Backs the "Open log" action on the MCP-unreachable toast.
- `mcp:status:get()` → `{ ok, detail? }` — current host MCP listener health, for a window mounting mid-outage. Live changes arrive on the **`mcp:status`** broadcast (`main → renderer`, change-only), fanned to every window by `mcpStatusBroadcast.ts` (mirrors `observabilityBroadcast.ts`). Drives the sticky "MCP unreachable" toast (§11). The win32 TCP listener flips it on bind success/failure; non-win32 stays healthy (per-workspace unix failures still log via `reportListenerError` but aren't a global "MCP down").

### Perf telemetry
Always-on main-process performance recording (default on; toggle via Settings UI or `perf_set` MCP tool). Owned by `src/main/perf.ts` using the OTel API (§4 *Stack*).

**What's recorded (Phase 1):**
- **Event-loop stall windows** — `perf_hooks.monitorEventLoopDelay` (resolution 10ms) sampled every 5s. Windows whose max delay exceeds 50ms write a `stall` row carrying `p50`/`p99`/`max` in `meta`. The same window's percentiles are also emitted as OTel `nodejs.eventloop.delay.*` gauges (for OTLP dashboards). Windows overlapping an OS suspend (Electron powerMonitor suspend/resume, plus one sample interval after resume) are discarded, so stall rows measure the loop blocked while the machine was awake — not sleep gaps. Main-process GC pauses ≥25 ms are recorded as app-global `gc` rows (`claude_fleet.gc.major/minor/incremental/weakcb`) via a PerformanceObserver — synchronous stops no span can capture. A worker-thread **stall sentinel** (armed via MCP `perf_sentinel_set`, never persisted) runs a second event-loop delay monitor; while armed, stall rows carry `meta.sentinel` (`{workerMaxMs, aligned, ageMs}` or `{stale:true, ageMs}` when the worker itself went silent, or `{dead:true}` if the worker crashed) — main-and-worker stalling together is the OS-starvation signature. Every stall row also carries `meta.cpu.utilization` (system-wide busy fraction between stalls, from `os.cpus()` deltas).
- **Slow-op spans** — every `ipcMain.handle` callback is wrapped in a `claude_fleet.ipc.<channel>` OTel span; the JSONL ingest batch in `jsonlWatcher.ts` is wrapped as `claude_fleet.ingest` (wall time of the whole batch, including its inter-chunk yields; its `lines` attribute counts non-empty lines — the span wraps one `ingestBatch` call over the already-trimmed, blank-filtered batch, not the raw `split('\n')` length). The per-ingest summary broadcast is spanned as `claude_fleet.observability.ingest_broadcast` (workspace-attributed) — it runs from the watcher emit, outside any IPC handler. Spans ≥25ms are persisted as `slow_op` rows by the SQLite exporter; the OTLP exporter (when enabled) receives all spans. A `stall` that coincides with a `slow_op` is self-attributing at query time. Dockerode and vault calls get dedicated child spans (claude_fleet.docker.create/start/pause/stop/attach_pty/ping, claude_fleet.vault.resolve_env; ping additionally wraps its synchronous dispatch portion as claude_fleet.docker.ping_dispatch — the only part that can block the loop — to discriminate blocker from victim in stall analysis) parented under the enclosing IPC-channel span via OTel context propagation — a slow child persists as its own slow_op row sharing the parent's trace_id. Every statement on a SQLite Kysely handle (state.db and inbox.db) is timed around its synchronous `all`/`run` call (`setSqliteQueryObserver` in `store/kysely.ts`), and `perfNoteSqlSync` adds it to every enclosing `perfSpan*` span (each one's context carries the chain of spans around it, with a running total per span; OTel context follows the caller through the connection mutex), and each span sets `sql_sync_ms` / `sql_queries` once when it ends, so a span's own SQLite cost, its children's included, is separable from time it spent queued behind other statements; an IPC span with a large `dur_ms` and a small `sql_sync_ms` was waiting, not working. State.db's open is recorded once per launch as `claude_fleet.db.open` (*Retention* → *Leftover WAL*). The launch-time handlers also span their own parts: `claude_fleet.sessions.list_db` (`listSessions` in `sessions:list`), `claude_fleet.usage.tokens_spent` (`usage:rollingSpend`), and `claude_fleet.automations.refresh` (workspace names + manifests, no DB), `automations.host_shells` and `automations.list_db` (inbox.db) under `automations:list` / `automations:roleWorkspace`. IPC spans for id-bearing channels carry workspace_id/session_id attributes (stamped from a per-channel arg map in perfIpc.ts, or via perfSetSpanContext for post-lookup cases like pty:attach), which the SQLite exporter lifts into the perf_events columns.
- **PTY throughput windows** — OTel counters `claude_fleet.pty.bytes` and `claude_fleet.pty.chunks` (DELTA temporality), keyed by `workspace_id`/`session_id`, aggregated per 5s window and flushed as `pty_window` rows only when nonzero.
- **Terminal latency hops (Phase 2)** — `input_hop` (renderer keystroke → main `pty:input` receipt), `output_hop` (main `pty:data` send → renderer `term.write` completion), and `echo_rtt` (keystroke → next `pty:data` arrival within 2 s) are recorded as `claude_fleet.terminal.*` OTel histograms with broker-session attribution and persisted one row per (kind, session) per ~5 s flush window: `dur_ms` = window max, `meta` = `{ count, sum, min, max, buckets }`. Renderer samples batch to main every ~5 s over the one-way `perf:samples` channel, gated renderer-side by the `perf:state` push; main drops batches while recording is off.
- **Memory samples** — every 60 s while recording, one app-global `mem` row (`claude_fleet.mem`): `meta.main` = the main process's `process.memoryUsage()` in MB (`rssMb`, `heapUsedMb`, `heapTotalMb`, `externalMb`, `arrayBuffersMb`); `meta.procs` = Electron `app.getAppMetrics()` per process (`pid`, `type` Browser/Tab/GPU/Utility…, `name?`, `workingSetMb`, `privateMb` Windows-only); `meta.byType` = working set summed per type; `meta.totalWorkingSetMb`, also stored in `dur_ms` so trends chart without `json_extract`; `meta.context` = load counts for normalizing (`ptySessions`, `workspacesAttached`, `windows`). `ipc.ts` injects the per-process source via `setPerfMemorySource` (perf.ts stays Electron-free); a failing source degrades to heap-only. Purpose: ground any runtime/rewrite decision in measured RAM rather than guesses.
- **Boot + embedding attribution spans** — startup steps after `initPerf` run inside `claude_fleet.boot.*` spans (`mcp_server`, `workspace_sockets`, `jsonl_watcher_start`, `peer_status_start`, `register_ipc`, the async `capture_fail_running`, `capture_fork_seed` and `claims_backfill` (started 30 s after the claims step like the embed backfill, which on SQLite is boot and on Postgres is the store coming up, so on the in-process fallback the model load stays out of boot), and the wall-time `embed_backfill`). The synchronous decode + split of a transcript's unread tail before each ingest batch is `claude_fleet.ingest.read` (`bytes` attribute). The semantic-search path's synchronous pieces are spanned so a stall they cause self-attributes: `claude_fleet.embed.spawn` (sidecar spawn → `ready`), `embed.create_process` (worker-thread `spawn` until the child has a pid; main is not blocked during it), `embed.run` (one embed call, sidecar or in process), `embed.require` (sync require of transformers + onnxruntime) and `embed.load` (async) — the last two only on the in-process fallback, so their presence on a host means the sidecar was not used, `claude_fleet.index.pending_turns`/`pending_summaries`/`pending_sessions` (async reads) and `delete_stale_models` (async delete) on the Kysely handle, and `claude_fleet.search.scan` (sync brute-force vector read + top-k on the MCP read-only connection).
- **Boot CPU profile** (`bootProfile.ts`, #464) — while recording is on, each launch runs the V8 sampling profiler (in-process `node:inspector` session, 1 ms interval) over main's first 60 s, starting right after `initPerf`. The window covers the delayed startup indexing, which begins at about 30 s. Its sampler thread keeps sampling while the event loop is blocked, so it attributes stalls that no span covers. The full profile is written to `<userData>/perf/boot-<iso>.cpuprofile` (newest 3 kept; opens in Chrome DevTools). One app-global `boot_profile` row (`claude_fleet.boot.cpu_profile`, `ts` = profile start, `dur_ms` = profiled window) carries the summary in `meta`: `seconds` (per wall second: JS and GC busy ms + the top 3 attributed frames) and `blocks` (up to 8 contiguous JS/GC-busy runs ≥ 500 ms, heaviest first, gaps ≤ 20 ms bridged: `startMs` from profile start, `durMs`, `gcMs`, top-8 `self` and `attr` frames, and the `stack` of the heaviest attributed frame), plus `sampleCount` and `file`. The sync analysis and serialization at window end run in a `claude_fleet.boot.cpu_profile_analyze` span. A frame is `<function> <file>:<line>` (`node_modules/` package path kept); the attributed frame is the deepest one outside `node:` internals, i.e. the app or library caller of a native. Native work with no JS on the stack shows as low JS ms in a second that a `stall` row covers.

**`perf_events` table (schema v11):**
```sql
CREATE TABLE perf_events (
  id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  ts           INTEGER NOT NULL,          -- epoch ms
  kind         TEXT NOT NULL,             -- stall | slow_op | pty_window | input_hop | output_hop | echo_rtt | gc | mem | boot_profile
  workspace_id TEXT,                      -- NULL for app-global rows (stalls, slow_ops)
  session_id   TEXT,
  name         TEXT,                      -- span/metric name for slow_op/pty_window
  dur_ms       REAL,
  trace_id     TEXT,
  span_id      TEXT,
  meta         TEXT                       -- JSON: span attrs, p50/p99/max, window values
);
CREATE INDEX idx_perf_events_ts ON perf_events(ts);
CREATE INDEX idx_perf_events_kind_ts ON perf_events(kind, ts);
```
`workspace_id IS NULL` rows (stalls, slow ops with no specific workspace) are app-global and visible to every MCP caller; workspace-tagged rows are scoped to the caller's allowed set. `trace_id`/`span_id` link local rows to an external OTLP backend when configured.

**`PerfStore` (`src/main/perfStore.ts`):** buffers rows in memory (cap 10,000 drop-oldest), batch-inserts in one transaction per ~5s flush (async, on the state.db Kysely handle; flushes are serialized, ≤500 rows per INSERT, and a failed flush restores its rows to the buffer). The flush itself runs inside `claude_fleet.perf.flush` span so telemetry self-reports overhead. Retention: rows older than 7 days deleted at `initPerf` time (startup).

**Export:** SQLite is always written while recording is on. OTLP/HTTP traces + metrics are added only when export is enabled (`perfOtlp.enabled` setting or `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` env var — env wins). Both signal types go to `<endpoint>/v1/traces` and `<endpoint>/v1/metrics` respectively. Auth headers via `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS`. Export never starts while recording is off.

**Controls and precedence:**
- Recording: `CLAUDE_FLEET_PERF=0` forces off (takes priority over any setting, never forces on). Otherwise the `perfTelemetry` config value (default `true`).
- Export: `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` env var forces OTLP export on to that endpoint (source `'env'`). Otherwise the `perfOtlp` config value. Export endpoint is Settings-UI or env only — `perf_set` cannot set it.
- Mock mode (`CLAUDE_FLEET_MOCK=1`): `initPerf` is never called (no DB); `perf:status` and the `config:setPerf*` handlers degrade to a truthful all-off `PerfStatus` (`enabled: false`, settings source, empty counts) instead of throwing.

**Settings UI:** a new **Diagnostics** section in the Settings modal (after Plan usage) with a *Performance telemetry* toggle row and live status line (`● recording · N events / 24h [· exporting → <endpoint>]` / `○ off` / `● forced off by CLAUDE_FLEET_PERF=0`), and an *Export via OTLP* checkbox with an always-visible (but disabled when unchecked) endpoint input field. The status line is the source of truth since `perf_set` can flip recording remotely.

### Filesystem
Host-filesystem helpers used by the workspace-create flow and the observability rail. The renderer is contextIsolated/sandboxed, so all disk access goes through main:
- `fs:isDirectory(path)` → `boolean` — whether `path` is an existing directory (workspace-root validation).
- `fs:mkdirp(path)` → `void` — create a directory and parents.
- `fs:openPath(path)` → `string` — reveal a host path in the OS file manager. On native macOS/Windows/Linux this is `shell.openPath`. **Under WSL** (detected once at load via `src/main/wsl.ts` — Linux platform + `WSL_DISTRO_NAME` or "microsoft" in `/proc/version`) `shell.openPath` can't reach a GUI file manager (no `xdg-open`/no Linux file manager), so the path is translated with `wslpath -w` and opened with `explorer.exe` (whose exit code is ignored — it returns 1 even on success). Resolves `''` on success or an error string; never rejects. Empty/non-string input returns `'No path provided'`. Drives the observability rail's Workspace **private/shared** folder rows; the renderer routes any error string to `app:logError`.
- `dialog:pickDirectory(defaultPath?)` → `string | null` — native open-directory dialog; null on cancel.

### Settings (app config)
App-level settings persist to `<userData>/config.json`: `{ fleetRoot?: string, disableHardwareAcceleration?: boolean, autoReloadLoadouts?: boolean, usageBudget?: { preset: 'pro'|'max5'|'max20'|'custom', customTokens: number }, uiPrefs?: { showBudgetBar?: boolean, showSessionCost?: boolean, maxSessions?: number, maxSessionAgeDays?: number }, perfTelemetry?: boolean, perfOtlp?: { enabled: boolean; endpoint: string }, knowledgeCapture?: boolean, knowledgeCaptureDailyCap?: number, ports?: { undeclaredToastAfterSec?: number } }`. All except `ports` are surfaced via the top-strip gear → `SettingsModal`.
- **Fleet root** — the single host dir holding every workspace's private folder (`<fleetRoot>/<id>`) and the shared folder (`<fleetRoot>/shared`).
- **disableHardwareAcceleration** — when true, the app calls `app.disableHardwareAcceleration()` at startup. Silences Chromium's noisy GPU-init failure on WSLg (rendering falls back to CPU). Must be read **synchronously** at module load, before the `ready` event — `config.ts:hardwareAccelDisabledAtStartup()` does a `readFileSync` of `config.json` rather than going through the async cache. Changing it requires an app restart to take effect.
- **autoReloadLoadouts** — **defaults on** (absent ⇒ on; only an explicit `false` disables it, so `config.ts:read()` preserves the key verbatim rather than coercing). When on, installing/updating a loadout into a **running container** workspace auto-reloads its active Claude session in place (`--resume`) so the loadout takes effect immediately — gated on the session being **idle** (deferred until claude stops working; never interrupts a live turn). When off, the user reloads manually and the loadout loads on their next `claude` start. See §7 *Run layer — reload*.
- **usageBudget** — the denominator for the observability rail's **plan-usage bar** (§5). `preset` is `pro` | `max5` | `max20` | `custom` (default `pro`). The non-custom presets resolve to `USAGE_BUDGET_PRESETS` (`config.ts`): estimated total tokens — input + output + cache create + cache read — available in one rolling window per Claude plan. Anthropic does **not** publish exact per-window token limits, so these are order-of-magnitude estimates anchored to the official Pro→Max multipliers (Pro 19M / Max 5× 95M / Max 20× 380M per `USAGE_BUDGET_WINDOW_HOURS` = 5h); `custom` uses `customTokens`. The user calibrates against their real ceiling (Settings → Usage, or the spend the bar shows when Claude Code reports a limit), so the absolute numbers being estimates is acceptable — the rolling-spend readout makes the bar self-correcting.
- **uiPrefs** — display-only preferences, all defaulting to "no change from prior behavior" (absent toggle ⇒ shown; absent/0 filter ⇒ unlimited). `showBudgetBar` gates the observability rail's plan-usage bar *and* the renderer's 15s `usage:rollingSpend` poll (no consumer ⇒ no poll). The poll waits until uiPrefs have loaded, so a launch with the bar off never queries spend. `showSessionCost` gates the per-session USD badge in the left-rail Sessions list. `maxSessions` / `maxSessionAgeDays` trim the Sessions list via the pure `sessionsView.ts:limitSessions()` helper (age filter, then newest-N cap) — applied to the **Recent** group only (open-tab sessions always render), bypassed while a text search or tag filter is active so old sessions stay findable, and reflected in the "All · N" badge (which keeps counting exactly what the All view lists — search excluded, as before). Values outside the settings dropdown presets (hand-edited config) are honored and rendered as an extra "N (custom)" option. Sessions stay in SQLite regardless — these never delete anything.
- **perfTelemetry** — whether the main-process OTel performance pipeline records locally. Absent or `true` ⇒ **recording on** (default); `false` ⇒ off. `CLAUDE_FLEET_PERF=0` forces it off regardless of this value. Live-toggleable via `config:setPerfTelemetry` (Settings UI or `perf_set` MCP tool) — no app restart required.
- **perfOtlp** — OTLP/HTTP export config `{ enabled: boolean; endpoint: string }`. Default `{ enabled: false, endpoint: '' }`. `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` overrides to on when set (env wins). Export never runs while recording is off. Editable only via the Settings UI or env var; `perf_set` deliberately cannot change this (a workspace must never redirect host telemetry to an arbitrary URL).
- **knowledgeCapture** — global switch for background knowledge capture (#483 — headless forks of a session that record durable decisions/facts/gotchas as fleet claims). Absent or `true` ⇒ **on** (default); `false` ⇒ no workspace runs capture forks, regardless of its own per-workspace `WorkspaceSpec.knowledgeCapture` settings (§7 *Workspace manifest*) — both must be on for a given workspace to capture. Live-toggleable via `config:setKnowledgeCapture` (Settings → Diagnostics checkbox); applied immediately, no restart or SDK reconfigure needed since the scheduler reads it per trigger.
- **knowledgeCaptureDailyCap** — app-wide cap on knowledge-capture runs *started* per local calendar day (#483 §5a). Absent ⇒ 40; clamped to 0–500 on read and write (`clampDailyCap`, `src/preload/captureSettings.ts`); 0 ⇒ no captures. Triggers over the cap are dropped and counted. Settings → Diagnostics; saves on Save like its Diagnostics siblings (not on change).
- **ports.undeclaredToastAfterSec** — hand-edited only (no setter channel, not in the Settings UI). Seconds an undeclared, non-ephemeral scanned port must keep serving before the fallback preview toast fires (`declared: false`). Absent, non-numeric or negative ⇒ `0` = never; fractions floor. Tune it from the `port_exposures` missed-declaration rate (§7).
- **identity.github.clientId** — hand-edited only (no setter channel). This is the client id of an org-owned GitHub OAuth app, for orgs that register their own instead of approving claude-fleet's. Resolution, first valid wins: env `CLAUDE_FLEET_GITHUB_CLIENT_ID`, then this key, then the build's `BUILTIN_GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` (`identity/clientId.ts`, empty until claude-fleet's OAuth app is registered). Client ids are public, and the device flow needs no secret. `read()` keeps `identity.github.clientId` (trimmed) across settings writes; other `identity.*` keys are dropped. Takes effect on app restart (config is cached).
- **store** — set by Settings → *Storage* for SQLite and the managed container (`store:setChoice`, §6 *Store*); an external Postgres block is hand-edited. Not in `config:get`. Validated strictly by `store/storeConfig.ts:parseStoreConfig`: unknown keys are rejected, and errors name keys, never values.
  - **SQLite:** absent or `{ kind: 'sqlite' }` ⇒ the SQLite files. `setStoreChoice('sqlite')` removes the key.
  - **Managed Postgres:** `{ kind: 'postgres', managed: true, poolMax? }` ⇒ the app-managed container (§7 *Managed Postgres*). No other key is allowed: the connection comes from the container and the password from the vault. `fleet-host`'s `db:` refuses it.
  - **Postgres:** `{ kind: 'postgres', … }` puts `state.db`'s and the inbox's tables (knowledge claims and capture runs included, §6 *Knowledge claims*) in one Postgres schema, with `schema` (default `fleet`; must already exist) and `poolMax` (default 4, max 32). The connection is exactly one of:
    - `connectionString`;
    - `env`, a variable holding one (e.g. `DATABASE_URL`), read at open time;
    - `host`/`port` (default 5432)/`database`/`user` + `password: { kind: 'env', name } | { kind: 'aws-rds-iam', region? }` + `ssl: 'disable' | 'verify-full'` (default `verify-full`).
  - **TLS:** `caFile` (absolute path) is the CA bundle for `verify-full`. It defaults to `/etc/ssl/certs/rds-global-bundle.pem` when that file exists. There is no `require`-style mode; the TLS rules are in §9.
  - **Secrets:** plaintext secrets (a `static` password, or a password in a connection string's userinfo or `?password=`) are refused, because `config.json` is not secret storage. `fleet-host`'s `db:` block refuses them too: sessions can read the host config (§9).
  - **No fallback:** an invalid block is a startup error, never a silent SQLite fallback (§8 *Startup*). `aws-rds-iam` requires `verify-full`.
- **Unreadable config.json** — a `config.json` that exists but can't be read or parsed is never overwritten: `read()` serves defaults and every settings write throws. At startup it raises the Quit / Continue-without-settings dialog (§8 *Startup*).
