- `config:unreadablePath()` → `string | null` — the `config.json` path when it is unreadable (the renderer then shows a sticky "Settings can't be saved" toast naming it), else `null`.
- `config:get()` → `{ fleetRoot, sharedDir, disableHardwareAcceleration, autoReloadLoadouts, usageBudget, uiPrefs, perfTelemetry, perfOtlp, knowledgeCapture, knowledgeCaptureDailyCap }` — includes the resolved display prefs (`uiPrefs`, all four keys present) and the perf fields (effective persisted values, not env-resolved). Fleet-root precedence: `CLAUDE_FLEET_ROOT` env override → persisted config value → `~/fleet` default.
- `config:setFleetRoot(path)` → `{ fleetRoot, sharedDir }` — persist a new fleet root (the dir is created). Takes effect for new containers and for existing ones on next restart (running containers keep their current mounts until recreated).
- `config:setHardwareAccelDisabled(disabled)` → `{ disableHardwareAcceleration }` — persist the HWA flag. `CLAUDE_FLEET_DISABLE_HWA=1` is an env override (dev shortcut) that forces it on regardless of the persisted value, matching the `CLAUDE_FLEET_MOCK` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` pattern.
- `config:setAutoReloadLoadouts(enabled)` → `{ autoReloadLoadouts }` — persist the auto-reload flag.
- `config:setUsageBudget(preset, customTokens)` → `{ usageBudget }` — persist the plan-usage preset and the custom token amount it falls back to (`customTokens` is rounded, clamped ≥0).
- `config:setUiPrefs(prefs)` → `{ uiPrefs }` — shallow-merge a partial display-prefs update (booleans kept as-is; numbers rounded, negatives dropped so the prior value wins).
- `config:setPerfTelemetry(enabled: boolean)` → `PerfStatus` — persist the `perfTelemetry` flag and reconfigure the OTel SDK (shutdown + re-init, no restart needed). Under `CLAUDE_FLEET_PERF=0`, the setting persists to config.json but the env override keeps recording off; reconfigure re-resolves and env wins.
- `config:setPerfOtlp(enabled: boolean, endpoint: string)` → `PerfStatus` — persist the OTLP export config and reconfigure the SDK.
- `config:setKnowledgeCapture(enabled: boolean)` → `void` — persist the global `knowledgeCapture` switch (#483). No SDK/reconfigure step — the capture scheduler reads the flag fresh on each trigger decision.
- `config:setKnowledgeCaptureDailyCap(n: number)` → `void` — persist the app-wide daily capture cap (#483 §5a). Non-number rejected; the value is clamped 0–500 (`clampDailyCap`) before being written.
- `perf:status` → `PerfStatus` — read-only snapshot: `{ enabled: boolean, source: 'settings'|'env-override', otlp: { enabled, endpoint, source: 'settings'|'env' }, eventCounts: Record<kind, count> }` (24-hour kind counts). In mock mode, returns a truthful all-off PerfStatus (`{ enabled: false, source: 'settings', otlp: { enabled: false, endpoint: null, source: 'settings' }, eventCounts: {} }`)
- `perf:samples` (one-way, renderer → main via `ipcRenderer.send`) — `{ sessionId: ptyHandleId, samples: [{ kind: 'output_hop'|'echo_rtt', durMs }] }` batches, ~5 s cadence per terminal session. Validated by `sanitizePerfSamples` (unknown shapes rejected, entries capped, `input_hop` deliberately not accepted from the renderer); dropped entirely while recording is off.
- `perf:state` (one-way, main → renderer via `webContents.send`) — `boolean` recording state, pushed to every window on each perf reconfigure (Settings toggle, MCP `perf_set`); the startup init predates the listener, so windows seed their initial value from `perf:status`. The renderer gates all latency stamping/batching on it.
- `usage:rollingSpend()` → `{ spentTokens, windowHours }` — the plan-usage bar's **numerator**: total tokens (all four types) spent across the **whole fleet** in the trailing `windowHours` window, via `db.ts:tokensSpentSince(Date.now() - windowMs)`. `events` has no `ts` index, and a plain `ts >= ?` filter scans the whole table, so the query skip-scans session ids out of `idx_events_session_ts` (a recursive `min(session_id)` CTE) and range-reads each session's window through the same index. Cost: one index seek per session plus the rows inside the window, on both stores. App.tsx polls this every 15s (separate, slower cadence than the per-workspace summary push — it's one cheap aggregate and the rolling window moves on the order of minutes).

### Store (async store part 4b)
- `store:getSettings` → `StoreSettings { configured, active, restartRequired, status, notice }`: `configured` is what config.json names now and `active` what this run opened (`'sqlite' | 'managed' | 'external'`); `restartRequired` is `configured !== active`; `status` is the live `StoreStatus`; `notice` is the last `setChoice` outcome when it needs saying (a refusal, a failure, or what was kept), else null.
- `store:setChoice(choice: 'sqlite' | 'managed')` → `StoreSettings`. Calls run one at a time. The same choice as configured is a no-op. Leaving a running managed store for SQLite is the switch back (§7 *Switch back to SQLite*): it copies the inbox back, writes config.json and relaunches, or stays on Postgres with a notice. Every other change only writes config.json and applies at the next start. Refused when config.json names an external Postgres or is unreadable.
- `store:status` (main → renderer push) → `StoreStatus { choice, phase: 'ready' | 'starting' | 'importing' | 'down', detail, progress: { source: 'inbox' | 'state', table, copied, total } | null, notice }`. `detail` is secret-free: why it is down, a start step, a failed service on a ready store (`History unavailable: …` / `Inbox unavailable: …`), or, on SQLite, why the inbox is closed. `notice` is the first-switch copy's (`skipped-nonempty`, `skipped-conflict`). SQLite is otherwise always `ready` with no detail.
- `app:relaunch` → `app.relaunch()` then `app.quit()`, so the normal `before-quit` cleanup runs.
- **Settings → *Storage* → History and inbox** (`StorageSettings.tsx`; copy in `storeView.ts`): a radio group, *SQLite (built in)* or *Postgres, managed by claude-fleet*. The managed option's text says it needs Docker, that other database roles see no history rows (§9 RLS), and that its volume outlives an uninstall. Picking the other option opens a confirm (`ModalBackdrop`) that states what happens: to managed, config.json now and at the next start the container, the copy, `inbox.db.migrated-<time>/`, what carries over (renames, tags, tab links, port exposures, usage; summaries and embeddings regenerate in the background), and the volume; back to SQLite from a running managed store, the copy back, the merge, the relaunch, that history for pruned transcripts or deleted workspaces stays in the Postgres volume, the refusals (an automation running, a separate `inbox.db` to move aside by hand) and the volume. While the switch back runs, the confirm says it waits for a bring-up in flight (*Waiting for the current copy to Postgres to finish…* / *…store to finish starting…*, `storeView.ts:switchBackProgressText`), else that it is copying. Undoing a choice that hasn't applied only rewrites config.json. With `restartRequired`, *Restart required* and *Restart now* (`app:relaunch`) show. `StoreSettings.notice` and `status.notice` show under the group. An external store shows one line saying config.json sets it. The renderer never sees a connection or credential.
- **Store banner:** `App.tsx` reads `store:getSettings` once (a pushed status wins) and follows `store:status`. `storeBannerText` gives *Starting the Postgres store (…)*, *Copying your inbox/history to Postgres: <table>, n of m rows*, *Postgres store unreachable … : <detail>*, *Postgres store is up, but <detail>. Retrying.*, or a SQLite `detail`; null when ready and clean. It renders as `.store-banner` (warn colours, no motion) inside `.app-banners`, the one grid row shared with the daemon and remote banners (`.app.has-banners`).

### Model endpoints (#250)
App-level registry of custom model endpoints (`<userData>/endpoints.json`). API keys stored in vault under scope `endpoint:<id>`, key `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`.
- `endpoints:list()` → `ModelEndpoint[]` — all registered endpoints.
- `endpoints:save(input)` → `ModelEndpoint` — create or update an endpoint. Input: `Omit<ModelEndpoint, 'id' | 'hasApiKey'> & { id?: string }` (id generated as UUID via `crypto.randomUUID()` if absent).
- `endpoints:delete(id)` → `void` — remove an endpoint and its stored API key.
- `endpoints:setApiKey(id, value)` → `void` — store or clear the API key for an endpoint (value `null` clears it).
- `endpoints:probe(baseUrl, modelId, apiKey?)` → `{ ok: boolean; status?: number; message: string }` — test whether an endpoint speaks the Anthropic Messages API (one test POST with max_tokens:1).

### Inbox (#407)
Renderer ↔ main for the inbox (Phase 1). All handlers live in `src/main/inbox/inboxIpc.ts` (wired from `index.ts`, in mock **and** real mode); the effects are in the pure `inboxService.ts`, shared with the MCP tools.
- `inbox:list(filter?)` → `Todo[]`. `filter = { status?: 'open'|'done'|'dismissed'|'all' (default 'open'), tags?: string[] (all must match), visibleTo?: workspaceId, limit?: ≤500 (default 200) }`, ordered urgent → low, then most recently seen.
- `inbox:get(id)` → `{ todo, events } | null` (events = append-only history in id order).
- `inbox:create(input)` → `Todo`. The user posts; `source = { kind: 'user', id: null }`. `input` is validated by `validatePostInput` (title ≤200 required, body ≤20 000, priority, key ≤200, tags).
- `inbox:update(id, patch)` → `Todo`. The user may change any todo (status / priority / title / `tagsAdd` / `tagsRemove` / `bodyAppend`).
- `inbox:counts()` → `{ open, urgent, high }` (open todos only).
- Push `inbox:changed { counts }` (`inboxBroadcast.ts`) after every write from either side. Preload: `window.api.inbox.{list,get,create,update,counts,onChanged}`; types in `src/preload/inboxTypes.ts` (main type-imports them, the web tsconfig includes that file).

### Notifications (#407 Phase 2)
Wired by `src/main/inbox/notifyIpc.ts` (registered with the inbox; the effects are in the pure `notifyService.ts`, the deliver/hold decision in the pure `notifyPolicy.ts`).
- `notifications:list(filter?: 'all'|'delivered'|'held', limit?)` → `NotificationRow[]` newest first (the Activity log).
- `notifications:settings()` → `{ defaultLevel, pause, downtimes, rules }`; `notifications:quietState()` → `{ pause, activeDowntime, nextDowntime, heldSinceQuiet }`.
- `notifications:scopesFor(item)` → the 🔔 popover's scopes (`key` "This item" when the item has a dedupe key, its `source`, its first tag); `notifications:effective(item)` / `effectiveMany(items)` → `EffectiveRule` (level, snooze, which rule).
- `notifications:setRule({scopeKind: key|source|tag, scopeValue, level, snoozeUntil?})` (upsert by scope) / `removeRule(id)` / `setDefaultLevel(level)`.
- `notifications:pause({until: epochMs|null, letUrgent})` / `resume()`; `saveDowntime(d)` / `removeDowntime(id)`; `test()`; `setViewing({workspaceId, inboxOpen})` (renderer → main, so needs-input notifications skip the workspace you're looking at); `permission()` → `{ supported }`.
- Pushes: `notifications:changed` (QuietState), `notifications:toast` (a delivered row, shown in-app when the window is focused), `notifications:open` (`{notificationId, todoId, workspaceId, kind}` after an OS-notification click; the window is focused first).
- Every write handler runs one ticker pass, so a resume produces its digest immediately; otherwise the ticker runs every 30 s, and a daily prune drops rows older than 30 days.
- Notification kinds: `post`, `todo`, `input-wait`, `post-rejected`, `digest`, `test`, `run-failed`, `proposal`, and `capture-settings` (#483 §5a: a workspace changed its own knowledge-capture settings over MCP; low priority, body = its reason).

### Presence (#407 Phase 2b)
- `presence:getKeepRunning()` / `presence:setKeepRunning(v)` (config.json `keepRunningInTray`, default false). Push `presence:openInbox` (the tray's "Open Automations").

### Automations (#407 Phase 3)
`src/main/automations/automationIpc.ts` (Electron) over the pure `automationService.ts`.
- `automations:list()` → `Automation[]` (with `nextFireAt`, `lastRun`, `weekStats`, `triggerSummaries`); `automations:get(id)` → `{ automation, runs (30 newest), yaml, versions }`.
- `automations:validate(text)` → `{ ok, errors[] }` (path-labelled); `automations:create(text)` / `automations:update(id, text)` (each save is a new version; created by `user`); `automations:setEnabled(id, v)`; `automations:remove(id)` (run history kept).
- `automations:run(id, args)`: a user manual run (form strings coerced; never rate-limited). `automations:starter()` returns a starter YAML naming your first workspace. Push `automations:changed`.

### Proposals (#407 Phase 5a)
- `automations:roleWorkspace()` → id | null; `automations:setRole(workspaceId | null)` (one per fleet, container-only; also sets `inbox.trusted`); `automations:discuss(proposalId)` (posts the review prompt into its live session) (#407 Phase 5b). `automations:draft(description)`, `automations:askChange(id, change)`, `proposals:refine(proposalId, index, change)` → the automations workspace id, after posting the authoring prompt into its live session; they throw when no automations workspace is set up, the text is empty, or (refine) the proposal isn't pending (#407 Phase 6).
- `proposals:list()` → `Proposal[]` (pending first, then newest); `proposals:yaml(id, index)`; `proposals:approve(id, {accept?: itemIndexes, editedYaml?, editedIndex?})` (unlisted pending items are declined); `proposals:reject(id, note)`; `proposals:revert(id, index)` (restores the replaced definition as a new version by the user). Push `proposals:changed`, plus `inbox:changed`, whose counts carry `approvals`.
- `kind: 'capture-settings'` proposals (#483 §5a) have empty `items` and carry `captureSettings: { workspaceId, before, after, changes, reasons }` (persisted in `proposals.payload`). `proposals:approve` applies only the changed fields onto the workspace's current settings (`captureSettingsService.applyApproved`, late-bound via `setCaptureSettingsApplier`); `proposals:reject` declines; `proposals:revert` doesn't apply. A newer one from the same workspace supersedes the older pending one. Each pending one counts as one toward the approvals badge. Rendered by `CaptureSettingsProposalCard`.

### Clipboard + context menu
The renderer cannot use `navigator.clipboard` reliably (focus/permission gotchas in Electron, and the renderer is contextIsolated). All clipboard access goes through main:
- `clipboard:write(text)` → `void` — `electron.clipboard.writeText` (no-op on empty).
- `clipboard:read()` → `string` — `electron.clipboard.readText`.
- `menu:showTerminalContextMenu({ hasSelection })` → `'copy' | 'paste' | 'selectAll' | null` — builds a native `Menu`, popups it on the focused window, resolves with the chosen action or `null` on dismiss. Copy item is disabled when `hasSelection` is false.

## 7. Data model

### Workspace manifest (on disk)
For each workspace, `<userData>/state/<id>/workspace.json` records the persistent spec. The directory is keyed by the immutable ULID `id`; the user-facing `name` is a mutable label stored inside the manifest. Renaming a workspace is a manifest edit only — no host paths, container labels, or vault accounts move. Every manifest write is **atomic** (`atomicWrite.ts` `writeFileAtomic`: a uniquely named temp file beside it, then `rename` over it, retried briefly on Windows `EPERM`/`EBUSY`/`EACCES`), because readers such as the 30 s automations-role refresh treat an unreadable manifest as an absent workspace.

**Fleet root + folders.** A single app-level **fleet root** (persisted in `<userData>/config.json`, default `~/fleet`, editable via the Settings dialog) anchors two kinds of host folder: each workspace's **private** folder at `<fleetRoot>/<id>` (mounted only into that container at `/workspace`) and one **shared** folder at `<fleetRoot>/shared` (mounted into every container at `/shared`, rw). For **container** workspaces the user no longer picks a per-workspace host directory; `workspaceRoot` in the manifest is the derived private folder. A startup migration (`migration.ts`) creates these folders for existing **container** workspaces and rewrites their stale `workspaceRoot` values, and `listAllWorkspaces` re-derives `workspaceRoot` from `<fleetRoot>/<id>` **for containers** so a pre-migration container's stale label is ignored. **Local workspaces are exempt from both** (`migrateFleetFolders` and the `listAllWorkspaces` re-derivation skip `kind === 'local'`): a local `workspaceRoot` is a user-picked path — a host dir for native, an in-distro Linux path for WSL — and is authoritative. Rewriting it to `<fleetRoot>/<id>` corrupted the manifest every boot, so a relaunched WSL session auto-resumed with `wsl --cd C:\…\fleet\<id>` (translated to an empty `/mnt/c/…` dir) and the transcript watcher re-registered the wrong projects dir.

```ts
type WorkspaceKind = 'container' | 'local' | 'remote';
type AuthMode = 'oauth' | 'apikey' | 'endpoint';

/** How a local workspace invokes `claude` (#253). Absent ⇒ native direct spawn. */
type WorkspaceLauncher =
  | { mode: 'native' }
  | { mode: 'wsl'; distro: string; shell: string; home: string; claudePath: string }
  | { mode: 'custom'; command: string };

interface WorkspaceSpec {
  id: string;              // ULID — identity, never changes
  name: string;            // mutable label, unique across the fleet (validated on save)
  description?: string;
  labels: string[];        // free-form, used for filtering in the Saved tab
  color?: { hue: number }; // one of 14 preset hues; random/hashed if unset
  workspaceRoot: string;   // container: derived `<fleetRoot>/<id>` (bind-mounted at /workspace). local: the user-chosen host dir claude runs in
  workspaceSubdir: string; // optional working subdir inside /workspace
  kind: WorkspaceKind;     // 'container' (Docker) or 'local' (host process, #16)
  image?: string;          // image ref for kind='container'; undefined for 'local'
  authMode: AuthMode;      // 'oauth' (default) or 'apikey' (requires ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in env) or 'endpoint' (model-endpoint registry, no shared-OAuth bind, #250)
  endpointId?: string;     // authMode 'endpoint' only: id into the app-level endpoint registry (<userData>/endpoints.json); resolved live at container-create/spawn time (#250)
  launcher?: WorkspaceLauncher; // local only (#253): spawn strategy; absent ⇒ 'native'. wsl requires win32 (sanitizeLauncher drops it elsewhere). wsl launchers may carry ignoreClaudeVersion (the "Keep" suppression from the newer-claude toast, #336). claudePath and ignoreClaudeVersion are manifest-owned once set: the form's wsl payload preserves them on a same-distro save instead of rebuilding from the fresh probe (#339 — the probe prefers well-known dirs and would silently revert an adopted path on every Saved-tab resume); a distro change takes full probe values and clears the ignore.
  env: {
    plain: Record<string, string>; // values live in the manifest
    secretKeys: string[];          // values live in the safeStorage vault (<userData>/secrets.enc)
  };
  resources?: { cpus?: number; memoryMb?: number };
  mounts?: { hostPath: string; containerPath: string; readOnly: boolean }[]; // container only: extra host folders (HostConfig.Mounts binds); dropped on other kinds at read
  remote?: {                       // kind 'remote' only: the fleet-host bookmark
    url: string;                   // canonical host URL (normalizeHostUrl(url) === url)
    tier: 'read' | 'steer' | 'session' | null;  // last tier /whoami reported; display only
    login: string | null;          // last login /whoami reported
    checkedAt: number;             // epoch ms of the last /whoami
  };
  mirror: {                        // durable transcript mirror (§11), factory on/delete
    default: 'on' | 'off';         // new sessions mirror unless overridden per-session
    cleanup: 'delete' | 'preserve';// pre-selected option in the close-time modal
  };
  createdAt: number;
  lastUsedAt: number;
}
```

The manifest is written on `workspace:create` and updated on successful `workspace:start`. Secret env values are NOT persisted here — only the *list* of keys (`secretKeys`). Values land in the safeStorage-encrypted vault (`<userData>/secrets.enc`) and are resolved at container-start time via `vault.resolveEnv(id, plain, secretKeys)`.

`workspace:remove(_, { deleteState: true })` removes the state dir (and thus the manifest) and the renderer additionally calls `vault:deleteAllForWorkspace(id)` so the workspace disappears from the past list and leaves no orphan keychain entries.

**Remote workspaces.** A `kind: 'remote'` manifest is a *bookmark*, not a spec: the host URL, the workspace id the host reports, and display fields (name, colour). Its directory is the host's workspace id, so a remote workspace has the same id on every desktop; `/whoami` ids must be ULIDs (`^[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}$`), since the id becomes a directory name. `readWorkspaceManifest` drops a remote manifest whose id isn't its directory name or a ULID, or whose `remote` block fails `sanitizeRemote`; `remote` is never kept on another kind, and an unknown `kind` makes the manifest unreadable. A remote bookmark has `workspaceRoot: ''`, no private folder (`migrateFleetFolders` skips non-containers), no MCP socket, JSONL watcher, peer-status dir, port forwarding, dropbox, loadouts or workspace skills. Downgrading past c2: a pre-c2 app reads `kind: 'remote'` through `kind ?? 'container'` and routes it to Docker, and its migration rewrites the manifest without `remote`, so the bookmark is lost and must be re-added after upgrading again. Forget remote workspaces before downgrading.

Manifests written before `kind`/`image` existed default to `kind: 'container'` and an undefined `image` (the runner image was used implicitly). The renderer treats undefined-kind as container, so older manifests Just Work.

### Image library (on disk)
`<userData>/imageLibrary.json` records every image a container workspace has been created against. Updated automatically on each `workspace:create` (container kind): if the ref is new the entry is inserted with labels pulled via `docker.getImage(ref).inspect()`; if it already exists, `lastUsedAt` and `useCount` are bumped and the labels are refreshed. The new-workspace modal's image picker filters this library by free-text substring across the ref and every label key/value.

```ts
interface ImageEntry {
  ref: string;                       // 'ghcr.io/org/img:tag'
  digest?: string;                   // resolved image digest (sha256:…)
  labels: Record<string, string>;    // from image inspect; LABEL directives from the Dockerfile
  firstUsedAt: number;
  lastUsedAt: number;
  useCount: number;
}
```

Writes are atomic (write-to-temp + rename). Reads tolerate a missing or malformed file by returning an empty library. Manual deletion of entries is exposed via `images:remove`; manual *addition* is not exposed today (the library is purely use-driven).

### Session inventory (on disk)
For each workspace, `<userData>/state/<id>/sessions.json` records the renderer's per-workspace tab list. Loaded by `TerminalPane` on mount, persisted on every change (add tab, close tab, switch active). PTYs themselves are not persisted — only the display ids, names, and which tab was active. On relaunch the renderer recreates the tabs and each `TerminalSession` opens a fresh `docker exec claude` per its tab. **Closing a tab reaps its session** — `TerminalPane` calls `pty:closeSessionByBroker` (broker CLOSE / local-process kill) *before* removing the entry, so dropping a tab from the list no longer leaves the underlying claude alive but unreachable (#287). This is deliberately asymmetric with **app quit**, which does not reap: broker sessions survive an app restart to be re-attached (the whole point of the broker), and a local claude dies with its parent Electron process anyway. In-memory context (anything Claude held in process memory) is not yet preserved across app restarts; the in-container broker that fixes this is a deferred follow-up (see §11 Open decisions).

```ts
interface SessionEntry {
  id: string;        // stable display id = the broker session key; NOT the PTY handle (per-attach)
  name: string;      // 'main', 'session 2', 'session 3', … or an auto-derived title
  createdAt: number;
  resumeOf?: string; // set on a resume tab — first attach runs `claude --resume <resumeOf>`
  mirror?: 'on'|'off'; // per-session durable-mirror override; absent = workspace default
  autoName?: boolean;  // when true, `name` tracks Claude's session summary (auto-rename)
}

interface SessionInventory {
  version: 1;
  sessions: SessionEntry[];
  nextNum: number;   // auto-increment for 'session N' naming; doesn't decrement on close
  activeId?: string; // tab to focus on attach
}
```

Writes are atomic (write-to-temp + rename). Reads tolerate missing/malformed files by returning `{ version: 1, sessions: [], nextNum: 2 }`. The first attach to a fresh workspace inserts a single `main` tab and persists it immediately. A **resume tab** (created from the Sessions list) carries `resumeOf` so that even after the broker dies (host reboot) the re-attach re-resumes the same Claude session rather than starting a fresh one.

**Session-tab actions (⋮ menu).** Each tab has a `⋮` trigger (a portaled dropdown, like the workspace chips; replaces the old bare `×`) with **Rename**, **Refresh**, **Auto rename**, and **Close**. *Rename* is an inline edit in the tab; committing sets `name` and clears `autoName` (a manual name takes ownership). *Refresh* exits and resumes **that tab's** session in place (`claude --resume`) as soon as it's idle — the same close+resume the loadout reload uses, but user-initiated and per-tab (see §7 *Run layer — reload*). It enqueues the session id; `TerminalPane` fires it once the session goes idle (deferred while claude works) and App shows the shared toast immediately on click (`Refreshing <name>…`, or `… when idle` while busy). Disabled on an ended tab (nothing to resume in place). *Auto rename* toggles `autoName` — when on, `TerminalPane` subscribes to `observability:summary` and mirrors the tab's resolved Claude session title (`summaryForBrokerSession(...).title`, capped 40 chars) into `name`, refreshed on each push; an auto-named tab shows a `✦` marker. The toggle is **opt-in (default off)** so the conventional `main`/`session N` naming is unchanged unless the user asks for it. *Close* routes through the same `requestClose` path as before (mirror-delete confirm when a transcript mirror exists). Drag-reorder is disabled while a tab's name is being edited.

### Workspace shape (returned over IPC)
The `Workspace` type joins the manifest with live backend state:

```ts
type WorkspaceState = 'running' | 'paused' | 'stopped' | 'deleted' | 'unreachable';

interface Workspace extends WorkspaceSpec {
  state: WorkspaceState;
  containerId?: string;           // present iff state !== 'deleted' and state !== 'unreachable'
  lastKnownState?: 'running' | 'paused' | 'stopped' | 'deleted';  // present only when state === 'unreachable'
  status?: string;                // backend status string when available
}
```

**State semantics:**
- **running** — the backend container is alive and its processes are executing.
- **paused** — the container is alive but all its processes are frozen via cgroups freezer (`docker pause`). Recoverable with `docker unpause`; preserves in-memory state.
- **stopped** — the container exists but its main process has exited (or it's in any other non-live state like `created` / `dead`).
- **deleted** — there is no live container, but a manifest is on disk. Recoverable by recreating via the create flow.
- **unreachable** — the Docker daemon is down; the workspace state is unknown. `lastKnownState` carries the last observed state from before the outage (absent if the app started during the outage). Local workspaces are never `unreachable`. A remote row is `unreachable` when its host fails `/healthz`, is in standby, or reports a different workspace id than the bookmark; one not yet probed this run is `unreachable` with `status: 'checking host'`.

Remote rows carry `remote` (from the manifest), `containerId = id` and `workspaceRoot = ''`, and are only ever `running` or `unreachable`.

### Backend dispatch (per-workspace, by `kind`)
Workspace operations are routed to a backend **per workspace**, not chosen globally. Both backends implement a single `Backend` contract (`src/main/backend.ts`: `ping`, `ensureImage`, `listLiveWorkspaces`, `createWorkspace`, `inspectImage`, `start/pause/stop/removeWorkspace`, `isResumeImageStale`, `attachPty`, `getBrokerLogs`) — the Docker backend (`docker.ts`), the local host-process backend (`local.ts`, #16), and the mock backend (`mock.ts`, behind `CLAUDE_FLEET_MOCK=1`). `ipc.ts` dispatches each call: `workspace:create` routes on the payload's `kind`; every other channel resolves the workspace's `kind` from its manifest via `backendRouter.ts:resolveKind(idOrContainerId)` (a Docker container id never matches a manifest path → defaults to `'container'`; a local workspace's containerId surrogate equals its id → resolves to `'local'`). `workspace:list` merges live results from both backends (deduped by id, since in mock mode both point at the same module). Docker-daemon-specific channels (`ping`, `ensureImage`, `inspectImage`) always target the Docker backend. In mock mode every workspace routes to the mock backend. Committee `pause`/`unpause` on an `unreachable` target throw a distinct "Docker daemon unreachable" error so callers can tell outage from deletion.

**Remote backend** (`remote.ts`, `createRemoteBackend`). `resolveKind` routes `'remote'` here; `workspace:list` merges `remoteBackend.listLiveWorkspaces()` (none in mock mode). It keeps one `RemoteClient` per URL (`remoteClient.ts`: RPC, `/whoami`, the `/events` socket and the token) and one watch per bookmark. `listLiveWorkspaces` reads manifests, syncs the watches and returns cached state without touching the network; each watch probes `/healthz` and reschedules every 15 s (`HEALTH_POLL_MS`, unref'd timer), and a probe that lands after its watch was replaced or forgotten records nothing. `ping` is always true; `start`/`pause`/`stop`/`ensureImage` throw "managed by its deployment"; `removeWorkspace` deletes only a verified bookmark's state dir and unwatches it; `attachPty` requires a bookmark. `refresh(id?)`/`stop()`; `stopRemoteBackend()` runs in `before-quit`. **Tier refresh:** `/whoami` runs in the background health poll, at most once a minute per bookmark (`TIER_REFRESH_MS`, failures count too; a change of signed-in login, via `IdentityService.currentLogin`, voids the throttle), and always before an attach. The poll waits `max(HEALTH_POLL_MS, Retry-After)` after a throttled `/healthz` or `/whoami`; a changed tier or login is written back to the manifest (re-read first, written only if the URL is unchanged) so the chip badge survives the host being down. The tier is display only: CREATE decisions use the fresh `/whoami`, never the cached tier. **`RemoteClient.subscribe`** (the `/events` socket; no desktop caller yet) reconnects with 1/2/5/10/30 s backoff (floor 1 s, reset after a socket stays up 30 s), `max(backoff, Retry-After)` capped at 10 min; 401/4401 retries slowly (30 s), 403/4403 stops; 429 maps to `unreachable` with `retryAfterMs`. It terminates a socket the host hasn't pinged in 60 s; ws auto-answers pings. A remote workspace is never a committee party (`decideControl` requires `kind: 'container'`).

### Local (non-container) backend (#16)
The local backend (`local.ts`) runs `claude` as a **host child process** via `node-pty`, against a **user-chosen host directory** — no Docker, no broker, no bind mounts. It's for hosts where Docker is unavailable/overkill or `claude` is already installed. Binary resolution is platform-aware (POSIX + Windows); node-pty automatically uses ConPTY on Windows. Pause/resume is not supported for native and custom launcher modes — there is no container to freeze and SIGSTOP/SIGCONT don't map cleanly onto a host process with in-flight network connections; the Pause button is hidden for those modes. WSL-mode workspaces can pause (see Launcher below).

- **Session manager (`localSessions.ts`)** — the in-process analog of the in-container broker. It owns each local PTY in a `Map` keyed by `<workspaceId> <sessionId>`, keeps it alive across renderer **detach/reattach** (workspace switches), and replays a capped ring buffer (256 KiB) on reattach so scrollback is restored — exactly the broker's HISTORY behavior, in the main process. **Replay must not begin inside an escape sequence (#268):** the ring evicts whole chunks and PTY chunk boundaries are whatever the OS delivered, so once the ring has trimmed, its retained head routinely starts mid-sequence. Replayed verbatim the terminal renders the tail as text — drop the ESC from `ESC[6n` and a literal `[6n` is printed into the transcript, permanently, since scrollback is never rewritten (this is the source of the reported far-left `6` and the `Re`/`Th`/`So.` fragments). `safeReplayStart()` advances to the first ESC or the byte after the first newline, whichever comes first — neither can be mid-sequence, and both are ASCII so the result is also on a UTF-8 boundary. Applied **only when the ring has actually trimmed**; an untrimmed ring still holds byte 0 of the session and is replayed as-is, or the first line of a short session would be eaten. Costs at most one partial line. The in-container broker's ring has the same hazard in a worse form — it is a *byte* ring, so it wraps at an arbitrary offset every time — and carries the mirror fix (`ReplaySnapshot()` in `broker/internal/session/ring.go`, used by `Attach`); `Snapshot()` keeps raw ring semantics. That both backends shared this bug independently is why container workspaces reproduced #268, which is what falsified its original ConPTY diagnosis. `detach()` leaves the process running; only `stop`/`remove` kill it. Pure module (no node-pty import — the caller injects a `spawn` factory), so it's unit-testable; `local.ts` supplies the node-pty-backed factory (lazy `require` so the native addon loads only when a session actually spawns).
- **Windows/ConPTY re-settle.** On Windows `node-pty` drives `claude` through ConPTY, which keeps its own pseudoconsole buffer and reprints/reflows it to the frontend on each update. `claude`'s TUI redraws incrementally (cursor-up + erase, no full clear), so whenever ConPTY's buffer width diverges from xterm's fitted width its reprints overlay rows xterm already drew → overlapping/ghosted text on every response (#268). Because the reflow is an **ongoing per-update condition**, the original one-shot post-spawn jitter (#269) did not hold in the field — and worse, it replayed the spawn-time cols captured in its closure, so a renderer fit landing inside its 250 ms window was silently reverted, pinning ConPTY at a stale width (the measured ~26-column divergence). The spawn factory uses a **debounced re-settle** (`conptySettle.ts`, pure/vitest-loadable): the spawn and **every subsequent resize** schedule a settle that fires 250 ms after the last one (the spawn-time settle stays because ConPTY only reliably applies its size once the child has attached, so the spawn size can be stale even when no resize follows), reading the pty's size **at fire time** and jittering the winsize one column away and back so ConPTY re-emits a clean frame — the programmatic equivalent of the manual window resize that heals it. One `conpty-settle` info row (`{pid, cols, rows, spawnCols, spawnRows}`) is written per pty, for its first settle; later settles are not logged, since each row is a synchronous write on main. Ongoing width divergence is the width-agreement monitor's job (below). Exit-guarded and disposed on pty exit (node-pty on Node 22 throws an *uncaught* "resize a pty that has already exited"). No-op on POSIX PTYs and on the container backend's Linux PTY, which don't exhibit this. Escape hatch: setting the user env var `CLAUDE_FLEET_CONPTY_DLL=1` (packaged builds included, unlike the dev-only flags) spawns with node-pty's `useConptyDll: true`, swapping the OS ConPTY for the newer `conpty.dll`/`OpenConsole.exe` bundled in node-pty 1.1.0's win32 prebuilds (already shipped via the blanket node-pty `asarUnpack`) — fallback #2 from #268, off by default until it has Windows soak time.
- **Local spawn queue (`spawnQueue.ts`).** A node-pty spawn is synchronous on the main thread (on Windows the ConPTY agent creates the pseudoconsole and child in native code, ~100–130 ms each), and app launch restores every open local tab at once. `attachPty` therefore routes every attach that will spawn (no live process for the tab) through a single module-level queue that runs **one spawn task per macrotask** (`setImmediate` between tasks) so IPC and first paint interleave instead of stacking into one long stall. `foreground` attaches (the visible tab, from `pty:attach`) run ahead of queued background ones, FIFO within each lane. Tasks are keyed by `(workspaceId, brokerSessionId)`: a newer attach for a tab already queued **replaces** the pending task (its caller rejects with `SpawnCancelledError`; a foreground replacement is promoted to the foreground lane), and an older attach whose async prep finishes after a newer one is dropped (per-tab arrival stamp). The resume lookup (`lookupResumableBrokerSession`, a state-DB query) runs in the async prep **before** enqueueing, bounded by a 3 s timeout that falls back to a fresh spawn and logs `resume-lookup-timeout` once per app run, so a slow DB never stalls the serial queue. The task itself does only synchronous checks: it is still the tab's newest attach, and the workspace is `started`, not `paused` and not mid stop/remove/pause (else it fails with `local workspace <id> is not running`). `pty:closeSessionByBroker` cancels the tab's pending task (`Backend.cancelPendingAttach`, local only). `stopWorkspace`/`removeWorkspace` mark the workspace as transitioning (a per-id counter, so overlapping transitions keep the guard up until the last finishes) before their first await (wsl `pauseWorkspace` right after reading its manifest, before signalling); attaches arriving until they finish are refused, including in prep before `started` is re-added. All three cancel every pending task for the workspace, and stop/remove clear `started` before their first await. `pty:attach` rethrows a cancellation without the `pty-attach-failed` log. A re-attach to a live process with nothing queued spawns nothing and bypasses the queue. The spawn uses the cols/rows of the attach request; a resize the renderer makes while the attach is queued is pushed after it resolves. Applies on every platform (POSIX spawns are cheap, so the queue only adds a macrotask there). Each spawn is still ~100 ms of sync main-thread time on Windows; hosting local PTYs in a `utilityProcess` would remove that and is not done.
- **Width agreement (renderer ↔ PTY).** The renderer fits xterm to the pane and pushes the new size over `pty:resize`; claude lays out its TUI at whatever width its PTY reports. If those two ever disagree, the transcript is corrupted at **write** time, not at resize time: claude's TUI (Ink) paints absolutely-positioned full-width rows, so a row laid out for a terminal `Δ` columns wider than xterm actually is overflows and wraps its tail onto the next line's **first `Δ` columns**. The bytes land in the scrollback buffer that way, so nothing repairs them — resizing afterwards cannot, and Ctrl+L only repaints the live screen. (Distinct from the xterm scrollback *reflow* corruption of #330, which is fixed by disabling reflow in `terminalOptions.ts`; both produce leading-column fragments, which is why they were long conflated.) A resize can be lost silently at several layers — `pty:resize` on an unknown handle (the attach/re-attach window, a reaped handle, an exited PTY), `local.ts`'s exit-guarded `safeResize`, and on WSL the ConPTY → `wsl.exe` → in-distro PTY relay — and the no-op-resize dedupe then suppresses every later push at that same size, so a single lost resize is permanent. Two mechanisms guard it: the renderer's **delivery latch** (`resizePusher.ts`) advances only on a confirmed `pty:resize`, retrying a drop up to a bounded attempt count and logging `pty-resize-abandoned` when it gives up; and main's **width-agreement monitor** (`widthAgreement.ts`) compares the size last pushed against `PtyHandle.getSize()` — the size the PTY reports actually holding — both immediately after each resize and on a 15 s sweep over live handles, logging `width-divergence` (`{wantCols, wantRows, gotCols, gotRows, deltaCols, at}`) once per distinct divergence. `getSize` is node-pty-only; the container/broker path cannot read its size back, so those handles are simply not checked rather than being assumed to agree.
- **PTY capture & replay (diagnostics).** Terminal-rendering bugs have repeatedly been argued from screenshots and inference — #268 burned through four root-cause theories that way, three of them wrong — because nothing could answer *what bytes did the terminal actually receive, and at what size?* Enabled from **Settings → Behavior → Capture terminal output** — an on/off toggle (`config.json`'s `captureEnabled`) plus a directory (`capturePty`). The directory is **remembered while capture is off**, so toggling doesn't discard it; capture runs only when the toggle is on *and* a directory is set. `captureEnabled` absent is inferred from whether a directory exists, so installs predating the toggle keep the behaviour they had. `CLAUDE_FLEET_CAPTURE_PTY=<dir>` overrides both. The directory must be absolute for the running platform — a Windows path on POSIX is a legal *relative* filename with backslashes, which would otherwise create a surprising directory beside the cwd rather than failing (`pty-capture-bad-dir`). Resolved per attach, so a change applies to terminals opened afterwards with no restart. It records, per attached session, a JSONL of the exact chunks forwarded to the renderer (base64, byte-exact across chunk boundaries) interleaved with `resize` events, from a single hook on the `pty:attach` data path — so it covers **both** backends, local and container, rather than only the one currently suspected. `scripts/replay-pty-capture.mjs` feeds a capture back through a headless xterm built from the app's **real** `buildTerminalOptions()` (transpiled from source, not copied — a replay against divergent options proves nothing) plus the same Unicode 11 tables, applies the resize timeline in order, and reports the overflow-tail signature: a full-width row followed by a short orphan, where **the orphan's length is how many columns claude was ahead of the terminal**. A capture is therefore both a diagnosis and a regression fixture. Off by default; bounded by `CLAUDE_FLEET_CAPTURE_PTY_MAX_MB`. A capture contains full terminal output, so treat one as sensitive.
- **Cross-restart continuity (warm state + auto-resume).** Unlike a container (whose broker is a separate process tree that survives an app quit), a local `claude` is a child of the Electron main process and dies with the app. Process liveness is in-memory (the `started`/`paused` sets), but the workspace's **warm state is persisted** to `<userData>/state/<id>/local-live.json` (`{ "state": "running" | "paused" }`; absent ⇒ stopped) on every transition and **rehydrated once** at the first `listLiveWorkspaces()` of a run — so a running local workspace keeps its chip in the warm strip across an app restart instead of demoting to the Saved tab. The file lives *beside* the manifest rather than in it because `workspace.json` is the user's spec and its strict-allowlist parser would drop unknown runtime fields on round-trip; `stop`/`remove` delete the file. Writes are serialized per workspace and each reads the in-memory state when it runs, so the last write always matches the latest transition (an attach's write cannot land after a concurrent stop's delete). Conversations are restored per tab on **first attach**: when an attach finds no live PTY for its broker session id and carries no explicit resume target, `attachPty` consults `lookupResumableBrokerSession` — the *verified* broker→claude mapping (#195), additionally requiring the mapped session to have ingested ≥1 transcript line (resuming a session that never wrote a transcript errors visibly in the tab) — and spawns `claude --resume <uuid>`: a fresh process picking the conversation back up off the on-disk JSONL, not the old in-memory state. No resumable mapping ⇒ fresh spawn; a live PTY ⇒ plain re-attach (resume args ignored — broker ATTACH parity); a dormant DB (mock mode) degrades to a fresh spawn. For a **paused WSL** workspace the SIGSTOP'd in-distro processes actually survive the app quit (signals other than CONT/KILL stay pending on a stopped process), so the rehydrated `paused` chip is truthful; unpausing CONTs them via the pidfiles, they die against their dead PTY, and the next attach auto-resumes — self-healing.
- **Working directory** — the user picks an existing host dir in the form (`dialog:pickDirectory` + a typed-path fallback); `claude` runs there. Safe to allow an arbitrary path because there's no bind mount. Stored as the manifest's `workspaceRoot` (the create + writeManifest handlers preserve it for local instead of deriving `<fleetRoot>/<id>`). Validated (exists + is a directory) in the `workspace:create` handler. For WSL mode, the working directory is a Linux path inside the distro (UNC `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` pastes are normalized on input); validated with `wsl.exe test -d`.
- **Uses the host's real claude config (no HOME isolation).** A local workspace *is* the user's host claude, so it inherits the real environment — crucially `HOME` — and uses the host's existing login, already-approved managed settings (the OTEL "managed settings require approval" gate), and its real install under `~/.local/bin`. An earlier isolated-`HOME` design re-triggered that approval gate on every workspace and made claude warn `$HOME/.local/bin/claude` was missing, so it was dropped: local provisions nothing under `<state>/<id>` except the manifest. Per-workspace differentiation is the working directory plus any injected env: `resolveEnv` layers the workspace's resolved env (e.g. a per-workspace `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) on top of the inherited host env. The `claude` binary is resolved (`claudeResolve.ts`, a pure electron-free module) by trying, in order: a `CLAUDE_FLEET_LOCAL_CLAUDE_BIN` override → `command -v claude` on the **inherited** PATH → well-known install dirs (`~/.local/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/bin`) → the user's **login shell** (`$SHELL -lic 'command -v claude'`, which sources their profile). The extra fallbacks matter because a GUI-launched Electron app (desktop icon, WSLg) inherits a minimal PATH that omits shell-profile additions — so a native-installer claude under `~/.local/bin` is invisible to `command -v` alone. On Windows the ladder is `where.exe claude` (preferring a spawnable `.exe` over an extension-less shim) → `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe`; the POSIX shell steps are skipped (a GUI process already gets the registry-backed user PATH). If none resolve, attach throws a friendly "install Claude Code" error surfaced by the attach-error overlay. (Trade: local workspaces share the host `~/.claude` — login/history/config — rather than each having a private one; that's the intended meaning of "local".) One thing is deliberately **not** inherited: `buildEnv` scrubs claude's child-session markers (`CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDECODE`) from the base env before spawn. A fleet-launched local claude is a fresh top-level session, but if fleet was itself started from inside a claude session (e.g. a `claude` Bash tool ran `npm run dev`), those markers ride in on `process.env`; inherited unscrubbed, claude reads `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION` and turns transcript saving **off** — so no `<uuid>.jsonl` is written, the watcher ingests nothing, and the session shows `$0.00` with no busy attribution (#285). An explicit workspace-env override of a marker is preserved (it re-appears via `resolveEnv`, which layers last).
- **Launcher (#253).** The manifest `launcher` field selects how `claude` is actually invoked; absent means **native** (direct spawn of the resolved host binary — the pre-253 behavior). `wrapSpawnForLauncher` (`localLauncher.ts`, a pure vitest-loadable module) rewrites the SpawnPty call transparently so `localSessions.ts` is unchanged:
  - **`native`** — passthrough; the host-resolved binary is spawned directly.
  - **`wsl`** (win32 only) — the actual spawn is `wsl.exe -d <distro> --cd <linuxCwd> --exec <shell> -lic 'echo $$ > /tmp/claude-fleet-<id>-<sessionId>.pid; exec <claudePath> <args…>'`. The node-pty cwd is a valid Windows directory (`homedir()`) because wsl.exe itself requires a Windows working dir; the Linux cwd flows via `--cd`. The manifest caches `home` and `claudePath` from the save-time probe (`local:probeWslDistro`); if the distro is reinstalled and the cache goes stale, the shell prints exec's error into the PTY — the next workspace start's freshness check (below) offers any working install, or pick a different distro and back in the form to force a full re-probe (a same-distro re-save deliberately keeps the manifest's claudePath, #339). `WSLENV` is set to forward `ANTHROPIC_*`, `CLAUDE_*`, and `TERM` across the boundary (`/u` flag = Win→WSL only). Pause sends `kill -STOP` to the in-distro PID (read from the pidfile); resume sends `kill -CONT`; stop also sends `kill -TERM`. Pidfiles live in the distro's `/tmp` and vanish with the WSL VM, so stale signals are harmless no-ops. Because claudePath is pinned at save time, workspace start fires a background freshness check that offers strictly-newer in-distro claudes via toast (#336) — see the IPC surface entries above.
  - **`custom`** (all platforms) — a user-supplied command template run via the platform shell (`sh -c` on POSIX, `cmd.exe /d /s /c` on Windows). `{claude}` is replaced with the POSIX-quoted host binary path; `{args}` with the POSIX-quoted fleet flags (appended after the command if the placeholder is absent). The template is stored verbatim in the manifest (`command` field); the host directory is used as cwd unchanged.
- **Transcript retention (#483 §5a).** The app only ever **raises** retention. Local and WSL launches share the user's real `~/.claude/projects` (host, or the distro's), and `--settings` overrides the user's `cleanupPeriodDays` for that run's cleanup, so a lower value would delete the user's own older transcripts. On each launch (sessions and capture forks) `retention.ts:ensureLocalRetentionSettings` reads the user's effective `cleanupPeriodDays` from their `settings.json` (native: `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude`; WSL: the distro user's `~/.claude` over `\\wsl.localhost`; unreadable or absent ⇒ Claude's default 30). Only when the workspace's `transcriptRetentionDays` is higher does the launch get `--settings <stateDir>/claude-retention.settings.json` (`{"cleanupPeriodDays": N}`, rewritten each time; translated to the in-distro path for WSL). Otherwise no `--settings` is passed and the user's own value applies. The user's own `claude` runs outside the app always apply their own `cleanupPeriodDays`, which can still delete transcripts the workspace wanted kept.
- **Lifecycle** — `createWorkspace`/`startWorkspace` mark the workspace running (processes spawn lazily on first attach); `pauseWorkspace` throws for native/custom modes (the Pause button is hidden in the UI for those), but succeeds for WSL mode via pidfile signals; `stop`/`remove` kill all live sessions (`remove` with `deleteState` also wipes the state dir). `listLiveWorkspaces` returns every `kind:'local'` manifest annotated `running`/`paused`/`stopped` from the in-memory `started`/`paused` sets, so a stopped local workspace shows in the Saved modal (never the `deleted` bucket unless its manifest is gone).
- **Transcript ingestion.** A native/custom local workspace's Claude transcripts live in the host's `~/.claude/projects/<encodeClaudeProjectDir(workspaceRoot)>/`. A WSL-mode workspace's transcripts live in the distro at `<home>/.claude/projects/<encoded>/`, accessed from the host over the `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>` 9P share. **The encoded segment must be built from the cwd claude actually has INSIDE the distro, not from the stored `workspaceRoot` (#313)** — `wrapSpawnForLauncher` hands the root to `wsl.exe --cd`, which rewrites a Windows path into its `/mnt/<drive>` automount form before claude sees it, so claude encodes the translated path. `wslInDistroPath()` applies the same rewrite (drive-letter → `/mnt/<drive>`, `\\wsl.localhost\…` UNC → plain Linux path, already-Linux → unchanged) and `wslLocalProjectsDir` runs it before encoding. Encoding the raw root instead pointed the watcher at a directory claude never writes to, so the workspace ingested nothing and appeared in neither the observability rail nor the session rail. Because the 9P share delivers no inotify change events, WSL workspace directories are registered via a **second polling chokidar instance** (`registerPolledLocalDir`, `jsonlWatcher.ts`) rather than the normal inotify watcher — same JSONL handlers, different filesystem driver. The watcher registers at startup and on `workspace:create`; unregisters on `workspace:remove`.
- **MCP wiring (#12 for local)** — the read-only fleet MCP server is exposed to a local `claude` **without touching the user's real `~/.claude.json`**: `attachPty` spawns `claude --mcp-config <state>/<id>/mcp-config.json`. A `--mcp-config` server is **auto-trusted (no approval gate) and merges** with the user's own servers (verified against the CLI docs). The config points `claude-fleet-state` at a tiny stdio↔socket **bridge** (`mcpLocalBridge.ts`, written to `<userData>/mcp/local-bridge.cjs`) run via **Electron-as-node** (`command = process.execPath`, `env.ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE = '1'`, plus the transport env below) — so it needs neither host `socat` (which the container uses but isn't on every host) nor host `node`. **Transport is per-platform (#295), and the *gate* follows it:** on Linux/macOS the bridge dials this local workspace's **own** per-id unix socket (`env.CLAUDE_FLEET_MCP_SOCKET = <userData>/mcp/<id>/mcp.sock`, §11/#117) and wiring is skipped until that socket exists; on **Windows** no such socket is ever created (the host can't `listen()` on one — see the Windows-transport bullet in §11), so the bridge instead dials the shared loopback-TCP listener and authenticates with this workspace's token (`env.CLAUDE_FLEET_MCP_TCP = 127.0.0.1:7071`, `env.CLAUDE_FLEET_MCP_TOKEN_FILE = <userData>/mcp/<id>/token`, sent as the bridge's first line — the same protocol `mcpContainerBridge.ts` uses), and wiring is gated on that **token** file. `local.ts:localMcpTransport()` is the single place that choice is made. Gating Windows on the never-created `mcp.sock` is what previously left every Windows local workspace (native *and* wsl) with no fleet MCP at all. Local claude shares the app's lifetime with the MCP server, so the bridge is a plain connect with a short startup retry (no reconnect loop, unlike the container's); on the TCP transport a connection only counts as established once the token has been read and sent, so a bridge that races `ensureWorkspaceToken` waits rather than opening an unauthenticated stream the server would drop. **For WSL mode** (`wslMcpServerEntry`): claude runs inside the distro, but Windows interop (binfmt_misc) lets it exec the app's own `.exe` directly across the boundary with stdio piped through — only the `command` path is translated to its `/mnt/c/…` automount form; `args` and the env *values* stay as-is (the bridge runs as a Windows process and dials the same host listener as native local — so the **token path stays a Windows path too**, since the Windows-side bridge is what reads it). The one addition is **`env.WSLENV`** (#397): WSL interop forwards an env var to a Windows process *only* if it's named in `WSLENV`, so the entry sets `WSLENV` to the `:`-joined keys of the transport env (no `/p`/`/l` flags — the values are already Windows paths or plain strings). Without it `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` never crossed the boundary, the exe launched as the app instead of the bridge, and every WSL workspace saw `claude-fleet-state (CONNECTION_CLOSED)`. This `WSLENV` replaces the session's own for the bridge process only, which is fine — the bridge needs none of the launcher's forwarded vars. **Skipped outright when interop is disabled** (#259): the probed `interopEnabled` flag is persisted onto the wsl launcher in the manifest, and `ensureMcpConfig` returns before writing anything when it is explicitly `false` — interop is precisely the mechanism the bridge needs, so wiring it would only leave a permanently-failed `claude-fleet-state` in the user's `/mcp` list. The flag being **absent** (any manifest written before #259) means "not probed" and still wires, so upgrading never silently strips MCP from an existing workspace. Also skipped when the exe path isn't on a drive letter (e.g. a UNC install, which has no `/mnt/<drive>` form). Verify with the in-session `/mcp` command (transient `--mcp-config` servers don't show in `claude mcp list`).

### Docker container labels (backend implementation)
Today the primary workspace backend is a Docker container. The container name is `cf-<id>` (must be unique on the host); lookup is always by the `com.claude-fleet.id` label, which means renaming a workspace does not require touching the container at all. Each managed container carries:
- `com.claude-fleet.managed` = `"true"` — discovery filter. `dockerode listContainers` filters on this label exclusively, so unmanaged containers never appear in the UI.
- `com.claude-fleet.id` — the workspace's ULID. **Stable identity lookup key**; survives renames.
- `com.claude-fleet.name` — the workspace's user-facing label at create time. Snapshot only; the source of truth for current name is the manifest.
- `com.claude-fleet.workspace-root` — the workspace's private folder (`<fleetRoot>/<id>`), stamped so `listLiveWorkspaces` can return it without a manifest read.
- `com.claude-fleet.subdir` — the optional working subdir inside `/workspace`.

### Docker container shape
- `Tty: true`, `OpenStdin: true`, `StdinOnce: false` — required for interactive `docker exec` later.
- `WorkingDir: /workspace/${subdir}` (or `/workspace` if subdir is empty).
- Binds: `<fleetRoot>/<id>:/workspace:rw` (this workspace's private folder), `<fleetRoot>/shared:/shared:rw` (the fleet-wide shared folder, mounted into every container), `<userData>/state/<id>/.claude:/home/fleet/.claude:rw` (per-workspace persistent Claude state), `<userData>/state/<id>/broker:/run/broker:rw` (the directory the in-container broker creates its Unix socket in), and `<userData>/mcp/<id>:/fleet/mcp:rw` (this workspace's **own per-id** read-only MCP socket dir, §11/#117 — the per-id *leaf*, never the shared parent `<userData>/mcp/`, so a container sees only its own `mcp.sock` and the host can derive an unspoofable caller id from which listener accepted the connection). The private + shared host dirs are created (`mkdir -p`) before the container starts. When `authMode === 'oauth'`, one additional **file-bind** is layered on top of the `.claude` dir bind: `<userData>/claude-shared/.credentials.json:/home/fleet/.claude/.credentials.json:rw` — so the first workspace's Claude.ai login covers every subsequent one and token refresh in any workspace propagates to all of them. The shared host file is created (touched empty) by `docker.ts:ensureSharedCredentialsFile()` before the container starts, because Docker refuses to file-bind a missing host path. `apikey` workspaces don't get the file-bind — auth comes via `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the env. A parallel OAuth-only file-bind maps `<userData>/claude-shared/remote-settings.json:/home/fleet/.claude/remote-settings.json:rw` (touched empty by `docker.ts:ensureSharedRemoteSettingsFile()`): claude fetches the org's managed settings (e.g. OTEL telemetry endpoints) into this file and shows a one-time "Managed settings require approval" gate whenever it finds managed settings not already on disk — so without sharing, every new workspace re-prompts. Approving the gate once writes the fetched settings into the shared file in place; every subsequent OAuth workspace then finds them already present and skips the gate. Security is preserved: claude re-fetches on each start, so a genuine change to the org's settings still re-triggers the gate.
- A second **file-bind** (all auth modes) maps `<userData>/state/<id>/claude.json:/home/fleet/.claude.json:rw`. This file lives in `$HOME`, *beside* `~/.claude` rather than inside it, so the `.claude` dir bind does not cover it — yet it is where claude stores onboarding/account state (`hasCompletedOnboarding`, per-project `hasTrustDialogAccepted`). Without persisting it, every freshly-created container starts blank and re-runs the onboarding wizard (theme / "trust this folder" / setup) even when the credential is already valid — which reads to the user as "having to log in again." `docker.ts:ensureWorkspaceClaudeJson()` seeds the host file (only if absent) with `{ hasCompletedOnboarding: true, projects: { "<workingDir>": { hasTrustDialogAccepted: true } } }`, where `<workingDir>` is the container's cwd, so the wizard is pre-completed. Seeding only when absent lets claude own the file once it runs (startup counts, MCP/project state accumulate and persist across restarts/recreation). Safe with no credentials yet: the seed skips the wizard but claude still performs the real OAuth login when the token is genuinely missing. Trusting `/workspace` is implied by the act of creating the workspace against that host directory.
- **Extra mounts** (`manifest.mounts`, container only) are added as `HostConfig.Mounts` `{Type:'bind', Source, Target, ReadOnly}` entries, not `Binds` strings (Windows drive letters make `host:ctr:ro` ambiguous; `Binds` silently creates a missing host dir as root). `toDockerMounts` (`src/main/extraMountsHost.ts`) re-validates each `hostPath` at create time (the same host check `validateExtraMounts` ran at save) and passes its resolved realpath as `Source` (for a WSL path, `\\wsl$\<distro>\<in-distro realpath>`), closing the gap where any symlink in the host path was re-pointed between save and create; a now-missing source fails the create with `extra mount <hostPath> no longer exists`, and a now-blocked target (re-pointed outside the allowed folders) fails with `extra mount <hostPath> now points somewhere that can't be mounted`.
- Env: `manifest.env.plain` merged with secret values resolved at create time via `vault.resolveEnv(id, plain, secretKeys)` (missing secret keys resolve to the empty string so the container still starts; claude itself surfaces the auth failure). `HOME=/home/fleet` is also set so tooling finds the bind-mounted `.claude/`.
- `User: <hostUid>:<hostGid>` so bind-mounted files are owned by the host user.
- Optional resource limits: `cpus` (→ `NanoCpus`), `memoryMb` (→ `Memory`).
- `AutoRemove: false` — containers persist across restarts unless explicitly removed.
- `RestartPolicy: { Name: 'unless-stopped' }` — survive a host reboot / docker daemon restart. On daemon start the container comes back, its broker re-launches, and the user can resume sessions from disk (transcripts + the `broker_sessions` mapping persist). `unless-stopped` (not `always`) respects an explicit `workspace:stop` — a deliberately stopped workspace stays down across reboots; only ones running when the daemon went away come back.
- `~/.claude/settings.json` (host: `<workspaceClaudeDir>/settings.json`) carries `cleanupPeriodDays` ≥ the workspace's `knowledgeCapture.transcriptRetentionDays` (#483 §5a). Merged into any existing keys (a malformed file is left alone); written at create, at app start for every container manifest, and when the setting changes. An existing higher value is never lowered (`mergeCleanupPeriod`), so lowering the setting doesn't shorten a container's retention.

### JSONL→SQLite cache
Each workspace's Claude transcripts (`<userData>/state/<id>/.claude/projects/-workspace/<session-uuid>.jsonl`) are tailed by a single SQLite cache at `<userData>/state.db` (WAL mode). JSONL stays authoritative — the DB can be dropped at any time and the watcher rebuilds it from the JSONLs on next start.

**Postgres store.** With `store: { kind: 'postgres' }` (§6 *Settings*) the same queries run on Postgres (`openDbPostgres(pool)`; `store/pgStores.ts:openPgStores` opens both stores on one pool).
- **Schema:** a baseline equal to the final SQLite schema (`store/migrations/statePg.ts`, state baseline = state v14; v15 `port_exposures` is step 15; v16 retention is step 16; v17 attachment dedupe is step 17). Types: `BIGINT` for every integer, `DOUBLE PRECISION` for real, `TEXT COLLATE "C"` for every text column (byte order, as SQLite compares), `BYTEA` for `events.raw_z` and `event_blobs.body_z`, identity ids, and `embeddings.vec vector(384)`, nullable (the one NOT NULL difference from SQLite: a placeholder has no vector). There is no vector index; search is exact.
- **Performance budgets** (`state/stateBench.test.ts` `PG_GATE`; `STATE_BENCH=1 STORE_TEST_DIALECT=postgres` measures, `STATE_BENCH_PG_GATE=<files>` gates). Postgres is gated on absolute budgets, not a ratio to SQLite: ingest and writes pay a loopback round trip and a commit per statement (plus the write gate's `begin`/`commit`, which the bench opens the store with, as the app does). Reads ≤ 16 ms (summary uncached, events page, list sessions, read-round stall), cached summary and mapping lookup ≤ 1 ms, search ≤ 50 ms, stall max ≤ 50 ms, ingest ≤ 3 ms/line fresh and 1.5 ms/line replay, record mapping and record error ≤ 10 ms. The SQLite bench is unchanged and keeps its A/B gate against the base.
- **Managed Postgres** (`store/managedPostgres.ts`, Docker via `store/managedDocker.ts`): `ensureManagedPostgres` runs at every start and on recovery, and is idempotent.
  - **Container:** `claude-fleet-postgres` from the pinned image (§4), labels `com.claude-fleet.store=postgres` and `com.claude-fleet.store.pg-major=16`, never `com.claude-fleet.managed` (which marks workspace containers). `RestartPolicy: no`. Data in the named volume `claude-fleet-postgres-data` (same labels) at `/var/lib/postgresql/data`; `initdb --locale=C.UTF-8 --encoding=UTF8`, database `fleet`. The env has `POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE=/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid` and no `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, so initdb's superuser password is random and never stored. A container is recreated (same port and volume) unless it has the pinned image, the store label and a `127.0.0.1` host-port binding.
  - **Volume lifetime:** the volume outlives the container and an uninstall of the app; `docker volume rm claude-fleet-postgres-data` removes the data.
  - **Port:** `5432/tcp` published on `127.0.0.1` at a host port picked free once and fixed in the container, so a restart keeps the pool's address. When the start fails with a bind conflict, the container is recreated on a new free port (same volume) and started once more; the pool can't follow, so a recovery that moved the port says to restart the app. TLS is off: the connection never leaves loopback.
  - **Bootstrap**, as the superuser over the container's own socket (`docker exec sh -c …`, local trust). The script writes `$PGDATA/pg_hba.conf` as `local all all trust`, `host all postgres all reject`, `host all all all scram-sha-256`; then runs `ALTER ROLE postgres PASSWORD NULL` first, role `fleet_app` (LOGIN, CREATEROLE) with the vault-held password as a SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier, schema `fleet` owned by it, `vector` in `public` (`ALTER EXTENSION vector UPDATE` too), and `pg_reload_conf()`. The SQL rides an exec env var; bootstrap output is redacted of verifiers before logging. The app connects as `fleet_app` (`CREATEROLE` is what `query` isolation needs, §9). The Postgres server log may carry the verifier (a failed `ALTER ROLE` logs its text), never the plaintext.
  - **Health:** `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -t 3` inside the container (TCP: during initdb the entrypoint's temporary server listens on the socket only), up to 90 s; a Docker healthcheck runs the same. Exec calls poll `exec.inspect()` until done and time out (10 s probe, 60 s bootstrap).
  - **Upgrades:** a container from another image digest is stopped, removed and recreated on the same volume and port. A volume labelled with another major, or not labelled by the app, is refused with an error naming it; `pg_upgrade` isn't run.
  - **Stop:** at quit, best effort (`docker stop -t 10`) inside the bounded quit (§8). Postgres's WAL makes an unclean stop safe.
  - **Not tested here:** the real container runs only in CI's `managedPostgres.docker.test.ts` (`MANAGED_PG_DOCKER=1`); the logic is unit-tested against a fake `ManagedDocker`.
- **First-switch copy** (`store/firstSwitch.ts:firstSwitchCopy`, `store/storeImport.ts:importSqlite`): after the stores migrate and before anything uses them, `inbox.db` then `state.db` are copied into Postgres with their ids. state.db is copied, not re-ingested: renames, tab mappings, usage signals and embeddings aren't in the transcripts, and summaries reference event ids.
  - **Retention data:** a compressed event line (`raw_z`) is inflated into `raw_jsonl` with `raw_z` NULL (a corrupt one is copied as it is, bytes kept), a deduped attachment line is rebuilt from `event_blobs` with `att_ref` NULL (a missing blob keeps the stored form), `event_blobs` itself is not copied, and an empty placeholder vector becomes NULL; `event_retention` and `maintenance` copy as they are.
  - **Per table** (`IMPORT_TABLES`, every inbox and state table including FTS): keyset by SQLite `rowid` in chunks bounded by 30 000 bind parameters, read with safe integers. Each chunk is `INSERT … RETURNING col::text` (under `SET LOCAL extra_float_digits = 3`), and the returned values must equal the source values canonically (ints as decimal strings, floats round-tripped, vectors as float32, bytea as hex, a BLOB in a text column read as UTF-8, NUL → U+FFFD) or the chunk rolls back. Each chunk commits with its progress row in `store_import(source, tbl, last_rowid, copied, done)` (track `import`), so a failed or interrupted copy resumes. At a table's end its row count must equal SQLite's, and every identity sequence is `setval` to `GREATEST(max(id), sqlite_sequence.seq)`. A unique violation becomes an `ImportError` naming the table and key.
  - **Restart, not resume,** when the file's fingerprint (size and mtime of the file and of a non-empty WAL; a read-only open's empty WAL doesn't count) changed since the run began (`store_import_run`): the source's tables are truncated and copied again. The fingerprint is checked once more before `finished_at` is set, up to 3 attempts.
  - **Refused:** a SQLite file at another schema version; a non-empty `-journal` (the error says to switch back to SQLite, which rolls it back); a target that already holds rows the import didn't write (`skipped-nonempty`: nothing is written). A finished inbox run beside an `inbox.db` with another fingerprint is `skipped-conflict`: both copies are kept. After a skip, Postgres is in use, the notice says so, and a switch back is refused until the user quits and moves `inbox.db` (with any `-wal`/`-shm`) out of `<userData>`.
  - **After:** `inbox.db` is retired (below); `state.db` stays as the cache a switch back resumes from. When state was already copied and state.db's fingerprint changed since (the app ran on SQLite in between), the user-state merge runs into Postgres.
- **Switch back to SQLite** (`firstSwitch.ts:switchBackToSqlite`, orchestrated by `store/storeSwitch.ts:switchBackLive` under the supervisor's `exclusive`): leaving a managed store keeps the authoritative inbox.
  - **Plan** (`switchBackPlan(userData)`, no pool needed): `inbox.db` present with no `inbox.db.on-postgres` marker ⇒ the forward copy never finished, so `inbox.db` is still the inbox: only config.json is written. Marker present and `inbox.db` holds rows (or can't be read) ⇒ refused with the move-aside notice. Otherwise copy; an empty `inbox.db` is retired as `replaced`.
  - **Quiesce** (live store): the inbox and history write gates close (`InboxWriteGate`: every write's `afterBeginWrite` throws `InboxWriteRefused`), the notify, automation and capture timers stop, and both gates drain the writes in flight. A running or queued automation run (`hasActiveRuns`) refuses the switch: checked first, so a refusal stops nothing, and again after the drain.
  - **Copy:** the Postgres inbox tables go into `inbox.db.from-postgres` in one `REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` snapshot per attempt, keyset-paged on the primary key (FTS by row hash), with progress tables inside the partial file; per-table counts must match. A changed Postgres fingerprint (per table count + `md5` of sorted row hashes) restarts the partial; the fingerprint is rechecked after the copy (3 attempts, then it stays on Postgres). The partial switches to WAL mode, the user-state merge writes state.db, the partial is renamed `inbox.db` and recounted, `store_import_run(inbox)` is marked `exported:<fingerprint>`, the marker is removed, and config.json is written. A failure at any step undoes the placement and stays on Postgres.
  - **After:** a committed switch relaunches the app (the gates stay closed until exit). Writes refused while the gates are closed are logged, not retried; that includes a tab's `new-session` mapping, which fires once. A failed one (or one refused after the drain) reopens the gates, restarts the timers of the same notify, automation and capture instances (`resume()`; not registered again, so no `recover()` cuts a run short), replays pending input-wait notifications and requeues the watcher. The next switch to Postgres TRUNCATEs the Postgres inbox and re-imports `inbox.db` only if the live Postgres fingerprint still equals the `exported:` mark; otherwise `skipped-conflict`.
  - **Not ready** (down, or the copy keeps failing): with no marker it commits, stops the bring-up and relaunches; with the marker it opens the store just for the copy, or says the inbox lives in a Postgres it can't reach.
- **User-state merge** (`store/userStateMerge.ts`, both directions; the outgoing store wins a collision): `sessions` (`id`; a collision overwrites only `user_set_name`, a missing row is inserted), `broker_sessions` (`workspace_id, broker_session_id`; every column), `session_tags` (`session_id, tag`; a union, nothing removed), `port_exposures` (`id`), `usage_events` (a multiset insert of missing `(ts, workspace_id, session_id, kind, detail)`; ids are local), `usage_anchors` (`dedup_key`; ids local). Events and rollups are left to the watcher's re-ingest; summaries and embeddings regenerate through the backfill. Forward it runs in one Postgres transaction with the fingerprint update; backward from one snapshot in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, then the state run's fingerprint is set to the merged state.db's.
- **On-disk files** in `<userData>` (`store/sqliteFiles.ts`):
  - `inbox.db.on-postgres`: JSON `{ status, at }`, `live` or the skip status; the Postgres inbox is the live one. Written as `pending` before `inbox.db` is retired and `live` after; `pending` counts as live only once `inbox.db` is gone (`sqliteFiles.ts:inboxOnPostgres`), so a crash mid-retire never opens an empty SQLite inbox. A SQLite start that finds it keeps the inbox closed (the status `detail` says why, logged `inbox-on-postgres`); `InboxStore.open` removes a stale one when it opens `inbox.db`.
  - `inbox.db.migrated-<ms>/`, `inbox.db.replaced-<ms>/`, `inbox.db.orphaned-<ms>/`: a retired `inbox.db` moves with its sidecars, under their own names, into a directory (staged as `<dir>.moving`), so the trio still opens in place. `orphaned` holds `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` files found with no `inbox.db` beside them (swept before any placement and by `InboxStore.open`), so a foreign WAL never reaches an inbox.
  - `inbox.db.from-postgres`: the switch back's partial copy.
- **Migrations:** recorded in `schema_migrations(track, version)`, one transaction per run under an advisory lock (`store/pgMigrate.ts`). A later schema change ships a `{ version, postgres }` step beside its SQLite `migrate()` step. `schemaParity.test.ts` pins each baseline (tables, columns, keys, named indexes) to the SQLite schema.
- **Rollup changes:** the first change to a rollup table (v13/v14) on Postgres needs a dialect-neutral rebuild step shared by both tracks, not two hand-written rebuilds.
- **Ownership:** the store never creates its schema, roles or database, and never holds master credentials. The schema must exist (`migratePostgres` checks `current_schema()`), and pgvector ≥ 0.8.0 must be installed (created when the role may).
- **Order:** every list read has a total `ORDER BY` with NULLs first, so results match SQLite. The 2a/2b characterization snapshots run unchanged on both dialects.
- **Text safety:** Postgres rejects a NUL (U+0000) in any text value outright, so the driver (`SerializingDriver`) replaces one with U+FFFD in every top-level string parameter before it reaches the wire; a lone (unpaired) surrogate also becomes U+FFFD, a side effect of Node's UTF-8 encoding of the query text. SQLite stores both unchanged.
- **Search:** `searchEmbeddings(qvec, scope, { limit, kind })` searches per dialect:
  - **SQLite:** brute-force dot product.
  - **Postgres:** an exact pgvector cosine scan, `ORDER BY vec <=> q, id`, scored `1 − distance`. An HNSW index can't serve the `id` tiebreak, and filtered HNSW measured 0.32–0.78 recall against exact results, so search is exact. Approximate search is deferred to remote-workspaces (d), where published partitions grow a host's corpus: it is revisited when p50 exceeds 250 ms on a real store or one caller's scope exceeds 250k rows, with a recall test at 20k/100k/250k before it ships.
  - **Both:** ties break by `id`. An empty scope, `limit ≤ 0` or a zero-norm query returns `[]`.
  - **Score parity.** Every stored vector is L2-normalized (`EMBED_DIM` 384), so SQLite's dot product and pgvector's `1 − (vec <=> q)` are both cosine similarity, and federated search merges them as one ranking (§6 *Sessions*, `history:search`). On float32 vectors the two agree to float rounding: `store/pgVectorSearch.test.ts` (PG suite) scores 20 random unit vectors both ways and requires agreement within 1e-5 and `topK`'s order outside ties under 1e-6. SQLite stores int8-quantized vectors (`encodeVectorInt8`), which shifts its scores by about 3e-4, so a local SQLite hit and a host hit of nearly equal score may swap places; the order is otherwise the same. Across machines, `fleet-host embed-check` pins the sidecar's output against committed reference vectors on every host image build: cosine ≥ 0.995 per text and ≥ 0.9995 on average, a bound set above the CPU int8-kernel spread (up to about 2e-3) and far below real failures (≤ 0.967) (§4 *Host images*).
  - **Measured latency** (PG 16, one connection, `limit` 10, one workspace in scope):

    | rows | p50 | p95 |
    |---|---|---|
    | 20,000 | 18 ms | 23 ms |
    | 100,000 | 116 ms | 155 ms |
    | 250,000 | 291 ms | 342 ms |

**Schema (v17, current):**

```sql
CREATE TABLE events (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  session_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
```
