```sql
  ts INTEGER,                          -- ms since epoch; null for light events
  type TEXT NOT NULL,                  -- assistant / user / system / etc.
  subtype TEXT,                        -- system subtype (turn_duration, compact_boundary, …)
  uuid TEXT,                           -- event UUID (heavy events only)
  parent_uuid TEXT,
  -- fast-access extracts (NULL when n/a):
  model TEXT,                          -- assistant.message.model
  input_tokens INTEGER,
  output_tokens INTEGER,
  cache_read_input_tokens INTEGER,
  cache_creation_input_tokens INTEGER,
  service_tier TEXT,
  tool_name TEXT,                      -- first tool_use.name in the event's content[]
  tool_use_id TEXT,                    -- tool_use.id (assistant) / tool_result.tool_use_id (user) — links a call to its result
  tool_input TEXT,                     -- short summary of tool_use.input (command/path/pattern/…)
  tool_result_is_error INTEGER,        -- 1/0 on the tool_result event; NULL otherwise
  raw_jsonl TEXT NOT NULL,             -- original line ('' when raw_z holds it); a retention stub once stripped
  dedup_key TEXT NOT NULL,             -- uuid when present, else sha256(raw_jsonl)
  raw_z BLOB,                          -- v16: zlib-deflated line (SQLite only; BYTEA, always NULL, on Postgres)
  att_ref TEXT,                        -- v17: event_blobs.hash when attachment/rendered are fleetRef markers (SQLite only; NULL on Postgres)
  UNIQUE(session_id, dedup_key)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_session_ts ON events(session_id, ts);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_workspace ON events(workspace_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_events_tool_use ON events(session_id, tool_use_id);

-- The tool_* columns (schema v4) feed recentToolCallsForSession: a tool_use
-- row LEFT JOINed to its tool_result row on (session_id, tool_use_id) yields
-- the call's name, input summary, wall-clock duration (result.ts − use.ts),
-- and ok/error/pending status. Added via additive ALTER so existing token/
-- cost history survives; tool detail populates as new calls land.
CREATE INDEX idx_events_type ON events(type);

CREATE TABLE sessions (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                 -- session UUID (= JSONL filename stem)
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  cwd TEXT,                            -- from first `system` event carrying it
  started_at INTEGER,                  -- first event ts
  last_active_at INTEGER,              -- max(event ts)
  ai_title TEXT,                       -- latest `ai-title.aiTitle`; Claude Code emits these lines natively (see below)
  first_user_message TEXT,             -- last-prompt or first user.content; synthetic command-wrapper messages skipped
  user_set_name TEXT                   -- manual override set via sessions:rename
);
CREATE INDEX idx_sessions_workspace ON sessions(workspace_id);

CREATE TABLE errors (
  id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  ts           INTEGER NOT NULL,
  workspace_id TEXT,                          -- NULL for global app-level crashes
  session_id   TEXT,                          -- claude session UUID; NULL when unknown
  source       TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- 'main' | 'renderer'
  level        TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- 'error' | 'warn' | 'info'; default 'error'
  type         TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- e.g. 'uncaughtException', 'mapping-unresolved'
  message      TEXT NOT NULL,
  stack        TEXT,
  extra        TEXT                           -- JSON blob of caller-supplied fields
);
CREATE INDEX idx_errors_workspace_ts ON errors(workspace_id, ts);
CREATE INDEX idx_errors_session ON errors(session_id);

-- v6: semantic transcript search (rebuildable from JSONL — additive).
-- vec holds a 384-d L2-normalized vector (int8 since v16, see vec below) produced by bge-small-en-v1.5
-- via the local onnxruntime-node backend (bge-small-en-v1.5 at dtype q8; the DB
-- model key carries the dtype -- 'Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5@q8' -- because q8 and
-- fp32 vectors are not comparable: a dtype change re-keys and re-embeds, and the
-- backfill purges rows under retired keys via deleteEmbeddingsForOtherModels).
-- Indexing batch (8) and per-text truncation (1000 chars) are MEMORY controls:
-- onnxruntime's arena holds its peak allocation for the process lifetime, and
-- fp32 at batch 64 x 2000 chars grew the main process past 3 GB; q8 at 8 x 1000
-- holds ~300 MB steady. Brute-force cosine search; no sqlite-vec.
-- dedup_key = 't<ref_event_id>' for turns, String(source_max_event_id) for summaries.
CREATE TABLE embeddings (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  session_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
  kind         TEXT NOT NULL,          -- 'turn' | 'summary'
  ref_event_id INTEGER,                -- events.id for turns; NULL for summaries
  ts           INTEGER,
  text         TEXT NOT NULL,          -- the text that was embedded
  model_id     TEXT NOT NULL,          -- e.g. 'Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5'
  dim          INTEGER NOT NULL,       -- 384
  vec          BLOB NOT NULL,          -- int8 [f32 scale][384 × int8] (388 B) or legacy float32 (1536 B);
                                       -- empty for a placeholder (text = ''). Postgres: vector(384), NULL for a placeholder
  dedup_key    TEXT NOT NULL,
  UNIQUE(session_id, kind, dedup_key)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_emb_workspace ON embeddings(workspace_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_emb_session   ON embeddings(session_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_emb_ref_event ON embeddings(ref_event_id);

-- v8: chaptered session summaries — one row per summarized window, not one
-- per session. A long wandering session produces an append-only sequence of
-- focused chapters (each covering ~20 completed turns) rather than one
-- over-generalized blurb. The dedup key (session_id, source_max_event_id) is
-- unchanged so replay is idempotent. Old v6 rows migrated as-is (one chapter).
CREATE TABLE session_summaries (
  id                  INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  session_id          TEXT NOT NULL,
  workspace_id        TEXT NOT NULL,
  summary             TEXT NOT NULL,
  tags                TEXT,             -- JSON array for this chapter; NULL on migrated rows
  source_max_event_id INTEGER NOT NULL, -- max events.id at summary-generation time; dedup key
  from_ts             INTEGER,          -- wall-clock start of summarized window
  to_ts               INTEGER,          -- wall-clock end of summarized window
  model               TEXT,
  generated_at        INTEGER NOT NULL, -- ms since epoch
  UNIQUE(session_id, source_max_event_id)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_session_summaries_session ON session_summaries(session_id, generated_at);

-- v8: union of concept tags across all chapters for a session. Built by
-- ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING as each chapter lands — a session accumulates tags
-- from multiple chapters without duplicates and without rewriting existing rows.
-- Queried for workspace-level tag facets and semantic filtering.
CREATE TABLE session_tags (
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  session_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
  tag          TEXT NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, tag)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_session_tags_workspace ON session_tags(workspace_id, tag);

-- v8: append-only value-signal log. Scores are always derived at read time
-- (Phase 3 compaction); never stored destructively. Four event kinds:
--   'search-impression' — a session id appeared in a search_transcripts result
--                         (one row per distinct session per call; detail.query)
--   'clickthrough'      — a session was fetched via get_session/session_summary/
--                         list_events/get_cost within 5 min of a search that
--                         returned it (in-memory ring keyed per caller in mcpServer)
--   'marked-useful'     — mark_useful called explicitly (detail.note optional)
--   'resumed'           — host recorded a claude --resume at CREATE time
--                         (docker + local backends; strongest implicit vote)
CREATE TABLE usage_events (
  id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  ts           INTEGER NOT NULL,
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  session_id   TEXT,     -- NULL for workspace-level events
  kind         TEXT NOT NULL,
  detail       TEXT      -- JSON blob (query text, note, etc.)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_usage_events_session ON usage_events(session_id, kind);
CREATE INDEX idx_usage_events_workspace_ts ON usage_events(workspace_id, ts);

-- v15: one row per port appearance — declared via expose_port and/or found by
-- the broker scan (PortExposureService). source: declared | scanned | both.
-- outcome: opened | dismissed | ignored | not_serving (NULL while open).
-- opened_via: popup | rail | fallback (first open). Ephemeral-range scanned
-- ports are never recorded. Rows ended > 90 days ago are pruned at startup.
CREATE TABLE port_exposures (
  id               TEXT PRIMARY KEY,   -- ULID
  workspace_id     TEXT NOT NULL,
  session_id       TEXT,               -- owning broker session id, when attributed
  port             INTEGER NOT NULL,
  cmdline          TEXT,
  label            TEXT,
  reason           TEXT,
  source           TEXT NOT NULL,
  first_scanned_at INTEGER,            -- first probe pass (scan, or host probe for local)
  declared_at      INTEGER,
  popup_shown_at   INTEGER,
  outcome          TEXT,
  opened_via       TEXT,
  open_count       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  ended_at         INTEGER
);
CREATE INDEX idx_port_exposures_workspace ON port_exposures(workspace_id, port);
CREATE INDEX idx_port_exposures_ended ON port_exposures(ended_at);
-- Missed declarations (scanned, never declared, opened anyway):
--   SELECT count(*) FROM port_exposures WHERE declared_at IS NULL AND open_count > 0;

-- v9: account-level rate-limit anchors — the only account-wide-true checkpoints
-- (429 reset messages + populated rateLimits payloads); rebuilt from JSONL like
-- every other table.
CREATE TABLE usage_anchors (
  id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  ts           INTEGER NOT NULL,
  workspace_id TEXT,
  session_id   TEXT,
  kind         TEXT NOT NULL,      -- 'limit-hit' | 'throttle'
  http_status  INTEGER,
  scope        TEXT,               -- 'session' | 'weekly' | 'opus-weekly'
  reset_at     INTEGER,
  window_start INTEGER,
  message      TEXT,
  rate_limits  TEXT,
  dedup_key    TEXT NOT NULL,
  UNIQUE(dedup_key)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_usage_anchors_ts ON usage_anchors(ts);

-- v13: per-session token rollup — a materialized GROUP BY over events keyed
-- by (session_id, workspace_id, model, service_tier). model and service_tier
-- use '' as the NULL sentinel so they can participate in the PRIMARY KEY.
-- Invariant: session_costs ≡ GROUP BY over events at every transaction boundary.
-- Maintained transactionally with each event insert (the upsert runs only when
-- changes > 0, i.e. a genuinely new event landed). Removed with the session on
-- deleteSession. Rebuilt from events by the v13 migration block (DROP + INSERT …
-- SELECT) so any drift self-heals on the next schema bump. Serves the lifetime
-- cost readers (listSessions, costForSession, costForWorkspace, summary totals);
-- time-windowed reads (rolling spend, tokensSpentSince) stay on events.
CREATE TABLE session_costs (
  session_id                   TEXT NOT NULL,
  workspace_id                 TEXT NOT NULL,
  model                        TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',   -- '' when NULL in events
  service_tier                 TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',   -- '' when NULL in events
  input_tokens                 INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  output_tokens                INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  cache_read_input_tokens      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  cache_creation_input_tokens  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  event_count                  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, workspace_id, model, service_tier)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_session_costs_workspace ON session_costs(workspace_id);

-- v14: composite index + two summary-stat rollups (#390).
-- idx_events_session_type is required so that (session_id, type='assistant')
-- lookups — latestAssistant and the pre-#390 observedMax scan — seek directly
-- to one session's assistant rows rather than falling back to the table-wide
-- idx_events_type and scanning every assistant row in the DB (#386: 84k-row
-- scan for one session's 4 rows, 56ms → 3ms with this index).
CREATE INDEX idx_events_session_type ON events(session_id, type);

-- Invariant: session_stats.max_context_tokens ≡ COALESCE(MAX(
--   COALESCE(input_tokens,0) + COALESCE(cache_read_input_tokens,0)
--   + COALESCE(cache_creation_input_tokens,0)), 0) over type='assistant' events
-- for that session — at every transaction boundary.
-- Maintained transactionally with each event insert under the changes>0 guard
-- (assistant events only; duplicate replays must not inflate the running max).
-- Removed with the session on deleteSession. Rebuilt from events by the v14
-- migration block (DROP + INSERT … SELECT GROUP BY session_id) so any drift
-- self-heals on the next schema bump. Pinned by dbSessionStats.test.ts.
-- Drives observedMax / contextWindowFor in the summary path without touching events.
CREATE TABLE session_stats (
  session_id          TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  max_context_tokens  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
);

-- Invariant: session_tool_counts.count ≡ COUNT(*) GROUP BY (session_id,
-- tool_name) over events where tool_name IS NOT NULL — at every transaction
-- boundary.  Same maintenance contract as session_stats (changes>0 guard,
-- removed on deleteSession, rebuilt by v14 migration). Pinned by
-- dbSessionStats.test.ts. Drives topTools in the summary path without
-- touching events; ORDER BY count DESC, tool_name guarantees deterministic
-- tie-breaking (GROUP BY left ties unordered).
CREATE TABLE session_tool_counts (
  session_id  TEXT NOT NULL,
  tool_name   TEXT NOT NULL,
  count       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, tool_name)
);

-- v16: retention bookkeeping (see *Retention* below). Per-session watermarks:
-- rows with ts < compressed_before have been considered for compression,
-- rows with ts < stripped_before for stripping.
CREATE TABLE event_retention (
  session_id        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  workspace_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
  compressed_before INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  stripped_before   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0
);
CREATE INDEX idx_event_retention_workspace ON event_retention(workspace_id);

-- v16: key/value bookkeeping: retention.first_pass_at, retention.last_pass_at,
-- embeddings.compacted_through_id, vacuum.last_attempt_at,
-- attachments.deduped_through_id (v17).
CREATE TABLE maintenance (
  key   TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  value TEXT NOT NULL
);

-- v17: deduped attachment payloads (see *Attachment dedupe* below). body_z is
-- the body, zlib-deflated; bytes its UTF-8 length; refs the number of
-- events whose att_ref is hash. Empty on Postgres.
CREATE TABLE event_blobs (
  hash   TEXT PRIMARY KEY,               -- sha256 hex of the body (JSON array of the moved values' text)
  body_z BLOB NOT NULL,
  bytes  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  refs   INTEGER NOT NULL
);
CREATE TRIGGER events_att_ref_ad AFTER DELETE ON events WHEN old.att_ref IS NOT NULL BEGIN
  UPDATE event_blobs SET refs = refs - 1 WHERE hash = old.att_ref;
  DELETE FROM event_blobs WHERE hash = old.att_ref AND refs <= 0;
END;
CREATE TRIGGER events_att_ref_au AFTER UPDATE OF att_ref ON events
  WHEN old.att_ref IS NOT NULL AND (new.att_ref IS NULL OR new.att_ref <> old.att_ref) BEGIN
  UPDATE event_blobs SET refs = refs - 1 WHERE hash = old.att_ref;
  DELETE FROM event_blobs WHERE hash = old.att_ref AND refs <= 0;
END;

-- v18: ingest resume point per transcript file (host path), see *Transcript offsets*.
CREATE TABLE transcript_offsets (
  path         TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  session_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
  byte_offset  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  tail_len     INTEGER NOT NULL,        -- the ≤ 4096 bytes ending at byte_offset ...
  tail_hash    TEXT NOT NULL            -- ... and their sha1, re-checked before resuming
);
CREATE INDEX idx_transcript_offsets_workspace ON transcript_offsets(workspace_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_transcript_offsets_session ON transcript_offsets(session_id);
```

`ERRORS_RETENTION = 2000`: every `recordErrors` batch prunes once to the most recent 2000 rows (DELETE by id rank). The table is an append-only ring buffer — no explicit expiry, no separate job.

`logError` is the single write path. `<userData>/error.log` (one JSON line per call) remains the primary, crash-safe output. Error-level lines (`level: 'error'`, or no level, which includes uncaught exceptions) are crash-safe: `logError` writes them, with everything buffered before them, before it returns. Info and warn lines are batched for up to 200 ms: `logError` buffers them, and an unref'd timer (`FLUSH_MS`) writes the whole buffer with one `appendFileSync`, so a native crash or a hard kill can lose up to 200 ms of info/warn lines. One function, `flushErrorLogSync()`, does every write (error-level `logError` calls, the timer, `process.on('exit')`, right after an `uncaughtException` or `unhandledRejection` is logged, before each `app.exit`, before **Open Log** / `app:openErrorLog` open the file, and before a test retargets the log), so there is no concurrent writer. Rotation: before a write that would take the file past 5 MB (`ERROR_LOG_MAX_BYTES`), it renames the file to `error.log.1`, replacing the previous one; the size is stat'ed once, then counted in memory, only after a successful append. A failed rename (on Windows, another process holding the log open) keeps appending to the current file and retries on the next write, so rotation fails soft. A failed append keeps the lines buffered and retries on a timer that doubles per consecutive failure from 200 ms to 10 s (`MAX_RETRY_MS`), back to 200 ms after a success. Past 5,000 buffered lines (`MAX_PENDING_LINES`) or about 1 MB (`MAX_PENDING_BYTES`) the oldest are dropped, and the next successful write starts with one `error-log-dropped` warn line giving the count. The DB sink is registered via `setErrorSink`, which still receives every row synchronously; main registers `batchedErrorSink(recordErrors)`, which buffers rows for 200 ms and keeps at most one write in flight; past 1,000 waiting rows (`MAX_PENDING_ROWS`) the oldest are dropped and the next batch starts with one `error-rows-dropped` warn row giving the count. `recordErrors` inserts the batch and prunes once to the newest `ERRORS_RETENTION` (2000) in one transaction. If that fails with a row-level error, each row is inserted in its own transaction so one bad row can't drop the rest; it stops at once on a connection-level error (`isConnectionLevelError`: socket codes, SQLSTATE class 08, 57P01–03, 53300, or a timeout/connect/pool/terminated/not-open message), so an unreachable store costs one attempt per batch, and stops when the first two retried rows fail with the batch's error. It rejects when no row was written. Text fields are stripped of NUL (Postgres `text` refuses it) and `extra` is serialized with BigInt as a string and cycles replaced by `"[Circular]"`. The bounded quit gives the sink's final flush at most 500 ms before `closeDb`, so a wedged store can't hold back the store shutdown. A wedged DB never breaks crash logging. The prune is `DELETE … WHERE id <= (SELECT id … ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1 OFFSET 2000)`, a PK seek. A batch whose rows all fail is dropped and swallowed by the sink. `LogPayload` carries optional `workspaceId`, `sessionId`, and `level` fields (absent ⇒ `null` / `'error'`), enabling operational diagnostics to be attributed to a workspace or session alongside crash rows. Beyond crash rows, the main process emits **operational diagnostics** through the same path (all queryable from a workspace via the `list_errors` MCP tool): `app-start` (info, once per app run right after the DB sink is wired — the version string plus `extra.sha`, i.e. the build identity (#298), so historical error/telemetry rows can be partitioned by build when two builds share a semver), `mcp-tool-error` (every MCP tool-call exception, with stack + tool + truncated args — #194), `mcp-call-stalled` (watchdog: a tools/call still running after 10s, tagged with the stage stuck in, `resolve-allowed` vs `run-tool`), `mcp-slow-call` (completed call over 10s total or 2s scope-resolve), `grant-check-slow` (a single `assertControl` over 1s during scope resolution), and the broker→claude mapping lifecycle (#195): `mapping-learned` (info, unambiguous pairing), `mapping-ambiguous-consume` (warn, FIFO consume with >1 tab pending — pairing is a guess; includes the queue snapshot), `mapping-remapped` (warn, a broker session's claude id overwritten with a different one — the cross-wired-tab event), `mapping-learn-failed` (warn, the fire-and-forget mapping write rejected), `usage-record-failed` (warn, the fire-and-forget resume `recordUsageEvent` call rejected). MCP connection lifecycle rows make request loss attributable end-to-end without a row per short connection (each `claude -p` and hook opens one): `mcp-conn` (info) once a connection has stayed open 10 s (`MCP_CONN_LOG_AFTER_MS`); `mcp-conn-closed` (transport, lifetime, rpc-line count, requests in flight) for such a connection, for one that closed with requests still in flight (`warn`: request loss), or for one that carried no rpc lines — a short connection that answered everything writes nothing; and `mcp-auth-failed` (warn; a TCP connection presented an unknown token). `new-session-dropped` (info): a `new-session` found no pending attach for its workspace while attaches were queued for others; with an empty queue (production never fills it, and every existing transcript is announced at launch) nothing is logged.

This `sessions` table is the index behind the Sessions table feature (§6 *Sessions*, §8 *Browse & resume a past session*): `listSessions` reads it joined to `session_costs` (not a grouped `events` pass — the rollup makes this an index seek rather than a full scan), `renameSession` sets `user_set_name`, and `deleteSession` removes the session's rows here + in `events`/`session_costs`/`session_stats`/`session_tool_counts`/`broker_sessions`/`embeddings`/`event_retention` before the IPC layer unlinks the JSONL. `purgeWorkspace(id)` does the same for a whole workspace (plus `usage_events`, `session_summaries`, `session_tags`, `port_exposures`), in id chunks (starting at 250 rows, cut when a delete takes over 35 ms, timed once it holds the store connection, down to 50), one transaction each, yielding a macrotask between chunks (the SQLite driver resolves only microtasks between statements, so back-to-back chunks would hold the loop): `workspace:remove` calls it, fire-and-forget, when `deleteState` is set (a remove that keeps state keeps the history), and the retention pass's orphan sweep calls it for a workspace that is gone (*Retention* below). `errors`, `perf_events` and `usage_anchors` keep their rows.

**Retention** (`state/retention.ts`, `state/rawCodec.ts`; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-06-statedb-retention-design.md`). Claude Code deletes transcripts after `cleanupPeriodDays`, so state.db is often the only long-term copy. Retention therefore never deletes an event row: it rewrites `raw_jsonl`. `id`, `dedup_key` and the extract columns stay, so rollups, tool counts and `filesEdited` don't change, and a re-ingested original line hits `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and the row stays rewritten.
- **Strip** rows with `ts` older than 30 days (90 when the session has a `resumed` usage event). The resumed rule only protects rows not yet stripped: a session resumed after its rows were stripped stays stripped (the JSONL may still hold the full lines, but dedup keeps the stub).
  - a `tool_result` line becomes a stub: `toolUseResult` dropped, each result block reduced to `tool_use_id`, `is_error` and its first 500 chars, plus `fleetStripped: { bytes, at }`;
  - an assistant line with a `tool_use` input over 2,048 chars of JSON keeps `file_path`/`path`/`notebook_path`/`command`/`pattern`/`url`/`description`/`skill`/`subagent_type` (≤ 4,096 chars each) and cuts every other string field to 200 chars + `…`.
- **Compress** (SQLite only): lines older than 30 days and ≥ 512 bytes are zlib-deflated into `raw_z`, with `raw_jsonl = ''`. Readers go through `rawLine()`. Postgres TOAST already compresses large text, so `raw_z` stays NULL there and the SQLite→Postgres import inflates it.
- **Embeddings:** a placeholder turn (no text) stores no vector. Callers pass float32; `insertEmbeddings` stores int8 on SQLite (`vectors.ts`) and float32 `vector(384)` on Postgres (no size gain from quantizing there).
- **The pass** runs 5 min after start, then daily: orphan sweep → attachment dedupe backfill (*Attachment dedupe* below) → events → embeddings compaction → incremental vacuum.
  - Events walk each old session through `idx_events_session_ts` between its `event_retention` watermarks. No per-row flag or new events index exists: building one would read the whole table at migration.
  - Embeddings resume from `maintenance['embeddings.compacted_through_id']`.
  - Work goes in slices that aim for 35 ms of main-thread time (`SLICE_TARGET_MS`). A slice reads keys and stored sizes first (`octet_length`, no payload read), then fetches bodies only up to its byte budget, so no row is read twice. One transaction each, spanned `claude_fleet.retention.slice` with `step` (orphans / attachments / events / embeddings / vacuum) plus the rows, bytes, current batch size, `read_ms` / `write_ms` (the write includes the commit and its WAL fsync) and, while the checkpoint worker runs, its last `wal_log` / `wal_ckpt` / `wal_busy` frames. The exporter keeps a retention slice as a `slow_op` row only from 50 ms (its target sits near the 25 ms default), so the rows are the overshoots.
  - Each slice gets a budget that expires at the target. Time counts from the slice's `begin()`, once its first query holds the store, so waiting on the store mutex (e.g. behind ingest) neither spends the budget nor shrinks batches. Row loops (events, attachments, the SQLite embedding re-encode) check it between rows and stop early, always after at least one row, so a pass can't livelock; the cursor resumes at the first unprocessed row.
  - A single statement can't be interrupted, so each step's batch size adapts: cut in proportion when a slice overshoots the target, grown by 25% while under half of it. The bounds are a byte budget of 16 KB–2 MB for events and attachments (at least one row is always taken, over ≤ 200 keys), 1–200 rows for embeddings, 8–1,024 pages for vacuum and 50–250 rows per purge delete.
  - After a slice that worked for `t` ms the pass sleeps `max(250 ms, 9t)`, a ≤ 10% duty cycle on the main thread.
  - **WAL checkpoints** (SQLite, `state/walCheckpointer.ts`): SQLite's auto-checkpoint runs inside whichever commit crosses 1,000 WAL pages, so its page copy and fsync land on the main thread inside that commit. Each open SQLite store has **one** WAL checkpointer, created by `openDb` and owned by `db.ts` (the class is installed at boot with `installWalCheckpointer()`, so fleet-host's import graph never reaches worker code; without it, as on fleet-host or in tests that don't install it, SQLite keeps its own auto-checkpoint). Its users take **leases**: the leftover-WAL drain at open, and each retention run from its first slice (a run with nothing to do takes none, via `acquireWalCheckpointer`).
    - While a worker runs, the main connection has `wal_autocheckpoint = 0` and `journal_size_limit = 16 MB` (the rewind threshold below, so the WAL file shrinks back to it after a peak). The checkpointer records the connection's values when its worker takes over and restores them when it hands back, the one restore point; the switch is all or nothing (a pragma that throws puts back what was read, and the error is never retried outside the store mutex). The first lease spawns the worker thread (its own better-sqlite3 connection, loaded like the query worker's); later leases share it; the last lease to close ends it. There is never a second checkpointing worker, so leases never contend for the checkpoint lock.
    - The worker runs one `wal_checkpoint(PASSIVE)` at a time: on request, after each retention slice, and every second. SQLite rewinds the WAL only when a write starts after a complete checkpoint, and each rewind syncs a new WAL header, so slices don't wait for checkpoints by default: once the WAL passes 4,000 frames (~16 MB), the next slice first runs one and waits for it (asynchronously, outside its timing), and its write rewinds the WAL. That wait is bounded by the hung-checkpoint hand-back, which releases every waiter itself without waiting for the worker to exit, since `terminate()` can't interrupt native I/O.
    - A checkpoint is **complete** only when `busy = 0`, both counts are ≥ 0 and `checkpointed ≥ log`; a result with the lock busy (`busy ≠ 0` or `-1` counts) is incomplete.
    - While it runs, **every** main-connection commit relies on the worker. So it hands checkpoints back (restores the connection and ends the worker; that worker's leases stay open but inactive until they close, and the next lease taken starts a fresh worker even while they do) when the WAL passes 10,000 frames (not while the drain runs: a leftover WAL starts larger), when 5 checkpoints in a row fail or are incomplete (a reader such as the MCP read source or a query-worker snapshot pins frames), when a checkpoint is outstanding for 5 s (60 s while the drain runs; stuck I/O), or when the worker dies, exits or doesn't come up within 5 s; each logs `wal-checkpointer` (warn, through `setStateWarnSink`) with its last frame counts. A retention run's end closes its lease without waiting for a checkpoint in flight; the worker is told to close its connection, and terminated only if a checkpoint is in flight.
    - **Lifecycle:** the worker is tracked from the moment it is spawned, before its connection opens. `closeDb` (the quit path included) detaches the store's checkpointer, hands back, and waits up to 2 s for every worker thread it ever spawned to exit before closing main's connection, so main's close is the last one and SQLite's close-time checkpoint removes the WAL. With no worker alive, `closeDb` closes without waiting. A `closeDb` that waited returns without touching the store if another close or a reopen replaced the handle meanwhile.
    - `synchronous` stays FULL; `write_ms` in the slice meta shows how much of a slice is commit fsync.
    - **Leftover WAL:** recovery counts every valid frame of a WAL left by the previous run (a crash, a kill, a quit past its 10 s bound, or a close while another read-write connection still had the file open, which skips SQLite's close-time checkpoint) as not checkpointed, so the first commit past 1,000 frames would copy all of it, with fsyncs, on the main thread; this was measured at 456 ms for a 113 MB / 28,684-frame WAL in a Linux container. So `openDb` reads `state.db-wal`'s size and, before anything (migrations included) writes, its frame count via `wal_checkpoint(NOOP)`. At or past the connection's `wal_autocheckpoint` it switches the connection over synchronously, then spawns the checkpointer's worker (a failed switch spawns none) and starts the **drain** on that lease (`stateLeftoverDrain()`): checkpoints of its own, each judged only by its own result, until two complete ones in a row show at most 256 frames written between them (at most 20 rounds; a failed, hung or incomplete round retries after 100 ms and restarts that comparison), so frames main writes meanwhile (startup ingest, the `app-start` row) are copied by the worker too; then the lease closes. A retention lease taken meanwhile shares the worker and keeps the connection switched over until it closes too. A checkpoint is crash-safe, so a run that dies mid-drain repeats it on the next open.
    - Each launch records exactly one `claude_fleet.db.open` row (always kept, any duration: the open's own time, plus `wal_bytes`, `wal_frames`, `handed_off`, `worker_ready` (whether the drain's worker came up; -1 when unknown or no hand-off), `first_write_ms` and `first_write_after_ms`: the synchronous time of the first statement that succeeded and committed a change, an `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`REPLACE` outside a transaction that changed rows (`RETURNING` included) or the `COMMIT` of a transaction that did, so a read-only transaction or a no-op `INSERT OR IGNORE` doesn't count, and its offset from the open). `onStateOpenStats` reports it once, after `initPerf`: at that first write, or 10 s after the open without one; never after the store closed. After a drain, `claude_fleet.db.wal_drain` (`ok`, `rounds`, `wal_log`, `wal_ckpt`, `wal_busy`, `error`). Both carry their own measured start and end.
  - A stopped pass (quit, store switch) resumes at the session it stopped in.
  - Each pass logs `retention-pass` (info) with counts; a failing step logs `retention-step` (warn) and the next step still runs.
- **Orphan sweep** (SQLite and the managed Postgres only: an external Postgres can be shared with other desktops or `fleet-host`, whose workspaces this desktop has no manifest for): a workspace id with rows in `sessions`, `events` or `embeddings` (found by a recursive `min()` skip-scan per table) is purged only when all of these hold: the manifest listing is non-empty, the id is ULID-shaped, no manifest lists it, and `<userData>/state/<id>` is absent.
- **One-time VACUUM** (`state/vacuumHelper.ts`). `auto_vacuum` can only change through a full VACUUM, which rewrites the file under the write lock, so it runs only after quit.
  - **Decision:** before `closeDb`, `prepareVacuum` decides. It needs SQLite, `auto_vacuum ≠ INCREMENTAL`, `retention.first_pass_at` set, no attempt in the last 24 h (`vacuum.last_attempt_at`, written here) and free disk ≥ 2.2 × (db + wal): VACUUM peaks at about db + 2 × (temp copy + WAL), and the helper's temp files go to `<userData>` (`SQLITE_TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`) so one `statfs` covers them. Otherwise it logs `vacuum-skipped`.
  - **Helper:** after the bounded quit has closed the DB, main writes `state.db.vacuum.lock` (`{ pid: null, startedAt }`; no lock, no helper), spawns a detached helper (`process.execPath` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 -e`, better-sqlite3 from `app.asar.unpacked`) and rewrites the lock with its pid. The helper is the app's own executable, so an NSIS update started while it runs sees the app as running (and may close it); a killed VACUUM rolls back, so that is safe. The helper runs `PRAGMA auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL; VACUUM; wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, appends `vacuum-done` / `vacuum-failed` to `error.log` and removes the lock.
  - **Next start:** a fresh lock naming a live pid (`vacuumHelperActive`) defers only the SQLite history services (open, state writes, retention, watcher, capture scheduler, embedding backfill); the window, IPC and the MCP server (a WAL reader) start as usual and history IPC rejects until then. The outcome is logged as `vacuum-wait`.
    - `waitForVacuumHelper` polls the lock for up to 120 s without blocking the loop, then kills the helper (a killed VACUUM rolls back) and polls up to 5 min (`VACUUM_EXIT_WAIT_MS`) for the pid to exit, since Windows releases its file locks only once the process is gone.
    - A helper still alive after that is `lingering` (warn): history stays down for the session (`history-start` warn), because a helper holding the write lock would make every write block main for `busy_timeout` and then fail.
    - A quit during the wait ends it (`aborted`) and skips the history start.
    - `openDb` sets `busy_timeout = 5000` so a lock still being released waits instead of throwing. A failed open (including a migration that times out on a lock) closes its connection and leaves no handle, so a later `openDb` retries from scratch.
    - A lock older than 15 min, or naming a dead pid, is removed and never acted on, since its pid may have been reused. With no live helper (no wait), a leftover lock (pid-less, dead pid, old or unreadable) is removed at startup (`clearStaleVacuumLock`), never killed. A spawn that fails before the helper exists removes its pid-less lock.
- **Incremental vacuum:** every 30 min (and after a pass), if `auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL` and the freelist exceeds 4,096 pages, `incremental_vacuum(N)` runs in throttled slices (N adapts between 8 and 1,024 pages, starting at 256) until the freelist is at 4,096 pages or 65,536 pages have been returned in this run. It runs through `withRawSqlite`, holding the store mutex: through the driver's `run()` the pragma frees only one page. Postgres relies on autovacuum.

**Attachment dedupe** (`state/attachmentCodec.ts`; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-09-attachment-dedupe-design.md`). Claude Code re-records the same `attachment` payloads (tool lists, instructions, prompt snapshots, skill listings) on every session and turn: on the host copy measured 2026-11-09, 661 MB of such payload held ~22 MB of distinct content. Many of those lines also carry `rendered`, the attachment's rendered text, which repeats with it (355 MB, 19 MB distinct). On SQLite, a `type = 'attachment'` line whose top-level `attachment` and `rendered` values together serialize to ≥ 1,024 bytes is stored once per distinct pair:
- **Stored form:** each moved value is replaced by `{"fleetRef":"<sha256>","part":<i>}` (part 0 `attachment`, part 1 `rendered`; still valid JSON), and `events.att_ref` = the hash. The blob body is the JSON array of the values' literal text, hashed and deflated into `event_blobs`. A line is deduped only when each `"<key>":` + `JSON.stringify(value)` occurs in it exactly once, it has no `"fleetRef":"` already, and the rebuild equals the original; otherwise it is stored as it is.
- **Writes:** ingest dedupes before the insert, and only a line not stored yet: the watcher replays every JSONL on each start, so an attachment line ≥ 1 KB first probes `(session_id, dedup_key)` and skips the dedupe on a hit (the INSERT still runs, for AUTOINCREMENT). It adds a reference only when the event landed: `UPDATE … SET refs = refs + 1`, and on a miss the deflate plus `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(hash) DO UPDATE` (a hit needs no deflate). `dedup_key` comes from the original line, so a re-read line hits `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and refs don't move. The triggers release a reference on every delete and on any `att_ref` change; no `events(att_ref)` index exists. Postgres has the same triggers (`events_att_ref_release()`) for parity.
- **Reads:** `rawLine()` stays the stored-form codec (retention strips and compresses the stored form and never expands it); `expandLine(stored, att_ref, body_z)` splices each part back (`spliceParts`, which the MCP query worker also runs). `eventsForSession` fetches each distinct blob of a page once and returns the original lines; `unembeddedTurnEvents` reads only user/assistant rows, which carry no `att_ref`. A missing or corrupt blob reads as the stored form.
- **Downgrade:** a build older than v17 opening the database shows the markers in place of attachment payloads; nothing is lost.
- **Backfill:** the retention pass's attachments step (SQLite only) walks `type = 'attachment' AND att_ref IS NULL` by id (via `idx_events_type`) up to the max attachment id at the step's start, in ≤ 200-row slices budgeted at up to ~2 MB of work (a deflated row counts 3× its stored size; the budget adapts as above), inflating a compressed row first; when the source row was compressed and the stub is ≥ 512 bytes, the stub is deflated back into `raw_z`. It resumes from `maintenance['attachments.deduped_through_id']`; a row that fails the checks is passed over. `retention-pass` logs the count.
- **Postgres** does not dedupe (TOAST compresses large values, and the MCP query role reads `raw_jsonl` through a temp view): `att_ref` and `event_blobs` exist for schema parity and stay empty.

**Title derivation.** The display title is `user_set_name ?? ai_title ?? first_user_message`. `ai_title` is **populated, not dormant**, from **two** last-write-wins sources folded into `sessions.ai_title` by `ingestBatch`: (1) Claude Code (≥2.1.x) writes `{"type":"ai-title","aiTitle":…,"sessionId":…}` lines into its own transcript natively (no hook, no statusline) — but only **once, early** (a stale one-shot); (2) the `summarize.sh` Stop hook emits a **rolling** `ai-title` line from each chapter summarization (§4 *Title refresh*), so a long session's title keeps up with what it's actually about instead of freezing on the first prompt (#170). Claude's native line rides on an **undocumented native transcript line type**, so it is the kind of thing a `claude` pin bump can silently break (§4 — bumping the pin is deliberate; re-verify the `ai-title` line still appears); the summarizer's rolling line does not depend on that and continues regardless. `first_user_message` is the COALESCE'd first value, but **synthetic command-wrapper messages are skipped** when deriving it (`src/main/userPromptText.ts`): a session started with a slash command (e.g. `/clear`) or a `!` local command emits a wrapper-only first `user` message (`<local-command-caveat>`/`<command-name>`/`<bash-input>`/…) that would otherwise lock in as a junk fallback title; the first *real* prompt wins instead. Also skipped: the **bare** local-command caveat (`Caveat: The messages below were generated by the user…` — older claude CLIs omit the wrapper tag, and the local/WSL launcher runs whatever claude the host has) and any `user` event with `isMeta: true`. Rows poisoned before these filters existed cannot heal through ingest (COALESCE + event dedup), so migration 12 repairs them once — recompute from stored events, NULL when the session has no real prompt. `tests/ai-title.spec.ts` pins both behaviors against the real watcher + DB.

**`session-summary` JSONL event type.** The runner's `Stop` hook (`summarize.sh`) writes `{"type":"session-summary","summary":"…","tags":[…],"sessionId":"…","model":"…","fromEventTs":"…","toEventTs":"…"}` into the **sidecar** `<uuid>.fleet.jsonl` (never the live transcript). The watcher picks up sidecars via the `*.fleet.jsonl` naming convention and routes each `session-summary` event to `ingestBatch`, which inserts into `session_summaries` (append-only, dedup by `source_max_event_id`) and, per tag, `session_tags` (union across chapters, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). The chapter is then scheduled for embedding the same as a turn. Vectors are rebuilt from stored JSONL on DB drop + replay — the same idempotency guarantee as `events`.

**Local embeddings.** Turn text, session summaries and claims are embedded by `bge-small-en-v1.5` q8 (384-d, L2-normalized; `EMBED_MODEL_ID` `Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5@q8`) on the host, through one `EmbedFn` (`embed(texts) → Float32Array[]`) built by `embeddings.ts:makeEmbedder`. Indexing failures degrade silently (per-turn errors logged, search never blocks ingest). fleet-host uses the same sidecar binary through `EmbedderClient`, with no in-process fallback and no model download (§7 *Remote host*, *Embedder*).

- **Sidecar (default).** `embedderClient.ts` spawns `fleet-embedder --model-dir <dir>` lazily on the first `embed()` call (lookup: `FLEET_EMBEDDER_BIN`, then `resources/embedder/` packaged or `embedder/target/release/` in dev). The spawn runs in a `worker_threads` worker (`embedderSpawnWorker.ts`, an `eval` worker, so it needs no bundle entry or asar handling; 16 MB old-generation heap limit) that owns the child and relays its stdin/stdout/stderr bytes over the worker port as transferred buffers; the main thread keeps the framing, request and restart logic. `child_process.spawn` blocks its thread until the process exists, which took 5.7 s on Windows for the freshly installed exe (#566), so it must not run on main. `EmbedderClient` takes the spawn strategy as an option (default: direct spawn on the calling thread). Each spawner reports at most one error, labelled `spawn failed` (the child never existed), `child error` (a runtime error; the child is SIGKILLed) or `relay worker crashed`, then exactly one exit; the client treats all of them as a child exit. On POSIX only the thread that spawned a child can reap it, so the worker outlives its child: an exception in the worker kills the child and exits only after its `close`. If the worker dies outright (out of memory, `process.exit`), main SIGKILLs the child by pid before reporting, unless the worker already reported it reaped (a reaped pid may be reused); that child stays a zombie until the app exits. The worker is `unref`'d and never delays quit. `EmbedderClient.dispose()` closes stdin, sends SIGTERM after 2 s and SIGKILL after 4 s, for either spawner. Protocol on stdio: frames of a u32 little-endian length plus UTF-8 JSON (≤ 64 MiB). The child sends `{"type":"ready","protocol":1,…}` once the model loads (or `{"type":"fatal","error"}` and exits 1); requests are `{"id","texts"}`; replies are `{"id","n","dim","vectors":<base64 n×384 f32 LE>}` or `{"id","error"}`, in order. One request is in flight at a time. It exits 0 when stdin closes, so it dies with the app. A per-request `error` rejects only that call. A `ready` whose `protocol` differs from the host's `EMBEDDER_PROTOCOL` stops the client at once (no restarts) and selects the fallback, logging both versions.
- **Failure handling.** An exit, a missing `ready` within 120 s, a request over 120 s (the child is killed) or a protocol error counts as a failure: the in-flight request is resent once to the next child, and restarts back off 1 s, 2 s, 4 s … ≤ 30 s. **3 failures within 5 minutes** trip the crash loop: the client stops, and `makeEmbedder` switches to the in-process fallback for the rest of the run (`embedder-fallback` logged once). The next app start tries the sidecar again. A missing binary, model files that can't be fetched, or `CLAUDE_FLEET_EMBEDDER=inprocess` also select the fallback. Child stderr is logged as `embedder-stderr` (≤ 20 lines/min).
- **Model files.** The sidecar reads `tokenizer.json` and `onnx/model_quantized.onnx` from the transformers.js cache layout, `<userData>/models/Xenova/bge-small-en-v1.5/`, and never downloads. `embedderModelFiles.ts:ensureModelFiles` fetches any missing file from `huggingface.co/…/resolve/main/` (temp file + rename) before the first spawn, aborting after 10 min total or 60 s without bytes. A failed fetch rejects that embed call and, for 5 minutes, every call after it (`embedder-model-files` logged); then the next call retries. It never selects the fallback by itself.
- **Inference contract.** Both backends compute transformers.js `feature-extraction` with `pooling: 'mean', normalize: true` on **one text at a time, unpadded**: special tokens added, ids truncated to 512 *after* that (a long text loses its trailing `[SEP]`, as in transformers.js), `token_type_ids` zero, attention-masked mean, L2. Padding is avoided because the q8 model quantizes activations with one dynamic scale per tensor, padded positions included, so in a padded batch a text's vector depends on its neighbours. `embedderParity.test.ts` (CI `unit-run`) requires cosine ≥ 0.999 per text between the sidecar and the in-process embedder over a fixed 40-text corpus; onnxruntime 1.22 measures 1.000000, and 1.24 drifted to 0.996, which is why `ort` is pinned. **Existing rows** were written by earlier builds that embedded padded batches of 8, which differ from the unbatched vector of the same text by up to ~0.03 cosine (mean ~0.005). That noise predates the sidecar and is left in place: `EMBED_MODEL_ID` is unchanged and nothing is re-indexed; new rows are batch-independent.
- **In-process fallback (one release).** `embeddingsInProcess.ts` runs `@huggingface/transformers` on the native `onnxruntime-node` backend, one text per pipeline call, loaded only when the fallback is selected. `onnxruntime-node` is a cross-build native module: prebuilts are fetched by `install-app-deps` for the target Electron ABI; it is declared in `asarUnpack` alongside `better-sqlite3`/`keytar`/`node-pty`. The transitive `sharp` dep must ship and be asarUnpacked (see §4 *Native modules* for the rationale).
- **Startup indexing** (`embedBackfill.ts:runEmbedBackfill`, the `embed-backfill` service step) starts 30 s after the step runs, so boot and the initial JSONL ingest go first; watcher-triggered indexing (`ingest` events) is skipped for the first 30 s after the embedder is created, and those sessions are picked up by the backfill. The backfill purges vectors under other model ids only when `<userData>/models/.embed-model-id` differs from `<storeId> <EMBED_MODEL_ID>` (one file, rewritten after each successful purge; `storeId` is a 16-hex hash of the SQLite path, or of the Postgres host, port, database, schema and connection string, so no credential is written; switching stores A → B → A purges again, which deletes nothing new), and the purge deletes 500 rows per statement with a macrotask yield between (on SQLite each statement is synchronous). It then lists only the sessions with pending turns in one query (`sessionsWithPendingTurns`: sessions whose newest user/assistant event, found through `idx_events_session_type` in a `MATERIALIZED` CTE so it is computed once per session, has no `turn` embedding for the model, probed through `idx_emb_ref_event`; ordered like `listSessions`). That equals a per-session pending-turns read because `indexSessionTurns` embeds a session's turns oldest-first: the only possible holes below an embedded turn are placeholder rows lost to a failure between its two inserts, which carry no text and are never searched. It yields before each listed session's `indexSessionTurns` and before `indexSessionSummaries`; a failure in one session is logged (`index-backfill`) and the rest continue.

**Why these design choices:**
- **Unique `(session_id, dedup_key)`** makes ingestion idempotent. Re-tailing a JSONL from byte 0 (a lost or stale saved offset, a shrink) produces no duplicates: heavy events use their `uuid` as the key; light events without a `uuid` use a SHA-256 hash of the raw line. Insert uses `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.
- **`raw_jsonl` stored verbatim** so the DB is rebuildable and so new extract columns can be backfilled from existing rows without re-tailing.
- **Subagent JSONLs** (`<session-id>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl`) are ingested at `depth: 2`, attributed to the parent session id (reusing the `sidecar` no-new-session/no-mirror path so subagent turns contribute to cost but do not spawn spurious session rows or durable-mirror files).

**Watcher behavior:**
- One `JsonlWatcher` instance per main process. Started on `app.whenReady` (after `listWorkspaceManifests`), stopped on `before-quit`.
- Each workspace is registered with `registerWorkspace(id)`, which `mkdir -p`s `<state>/<id>/.claude/projects/-workspace` and adds it to the watch set (chokidar or `RecursiveDirWatcher`, see §4). The mkdir is non-obvious but load-bearing (recursive `fs.watch` throws on a missing dir, too): chokidar v5 silently drops paths that don't exist at `add()` time (its docs imply it queues missing paths and watches them when they appear, but in practice the create-detection misses files claude later writes there). Symptom when omitted: any workspace whose claude first-run happens AFTER `registerWorkspace` never gets its JSONLs ingested — the observability pane stays empty for that workspace forever.
- **Transcript offsets** (#539). Each file's byte offset lives in memory and is saved to `transcript_offsets`, together with the length and sha1 of the up-to-4096 bytes ending at the offset (`TAIL_BYTES`). `start()` loads every saved row without blocking registration; `process()` awaits the load, which retries up to 3 times and falls back to an empty map (a full re-read). On a file's first sighting the watcher re-reads those tail bytes and resumes from the saved offset only if the file is at least that long and the hash still matches. A file rewritten in place while the app was closed fails the check and is read from 0. Birth time is not used, because NTFS tunneling keeps it across delete + recreate and the 9P share reports ctime. After each batch that advances the offset, the row is marked dirty. Dirty rows are upserted and dropped rows deleted in one serialized chain every 2 s (`OFFSET_FLUSH_MS`) and on `stop()`, after the queue drains. Rows are dropped on unlink, for every transcript under a dir on `unregisterWorkspace`/`unregisterLocalWorkspace` (so a flush can't re-save a workspace being purged), and by a sweep 5 min after start (`OFFSET_SWEEP_MS`) for saved paths that no longer exist, such as transcripts claude deleted while the app was closed. So a launch reads only what was appended since the last run. A crash loses at most 2 s of offsets, which costs only a deduped re-read. `deleteSession` and `purgeWorkspace` also drop the matching rows. The table is part of the SQLite → Postgres copy (it describes the copied events) and is not in the MCP `query` snapshot.
- On add/change: read from offset to EOF, find the last `\n`, ingest the complete lines as one batch (`ingestBatch`), and advance the offset past the newline only after the batch resolves. A trailing partial line waits for the next event. chokidar drops a file's events that land within 5 ms of the previous one (it does not defer them), so a write in that window, such as the write half of a truncate-and-rewrite, gets no event. Each add/change therefore also arms a per-file 100 ms trailing re-check (`SETTLE_RECHECK_MS`), re-armed by later events and cancelled on unlink, unregister and `stop()`.
- **Ordering.** Each file has a serial queue (`keyedSerialQueue`, `src/main/state/serialQueue.ts`), so a file's batches never overlap and always apply in file order. The queue tail is not dropped on unlink or unregister: a re-added file waits for its in-flight batch. `stop()` drains every queued batch before `closeDb()` runs.
- **Async write path.** Every `state.db` write goes through one Kysely handle over the connection, whose mutex serializes all writes and transactions:
  - **Ingest:** `ingestBatch` applies `INGEST_TX_LINES` (500) lines per transaction and yields a macrotask between chunks. The per-line semantics are unchanged: deduped event insert; rollups only when it landed; metadata even for duplicates; anchors only for new rows. The per-line sessions upsert is folded once per chunk (`foldSessionTouch`), and reproduces SQLite's `MAX(x, NULL) = NULL` rule (#475).
  - **Mappings:** `recordBrokerSessionMapping` / `learnBrokerSessionMapping` run their check-then-act as one transaction. They mutate the in-memory `pendingClaudeIds` guard inside it, after the last await, and restore it if the transaction rejects. Ingest promotes a parked remap inside its chunk transaction.
  - **Close:** `closeDb()` is async and waits on the mutex before closing.
  - **Reads** (summaries, lists, cost, lookups, anchors, embedding backlog) are async on the same handle. On the SQLite driver a query does no I/O, so a read or read transaction runs to completion inside one microtask drain and never yields to an ingest mid-read. The live summary push (`observability:summary`) awaits the summary before sending; a failed push is logged as `observability-broadcast` (warn).
- Compaction (file shrinks below the stored offset) resets the offset to 0; `dedup_key` ensures already-ingested rows aren't duplicated.
- **Sidecar convention (`*.fleet.jsonl`).** Files matching `<uuid>.fleet.jsonl` in a watched directory are host-written data sidecars (Stop-hook chapter summaries) rather than live claude transcripts. `parseTranscriptFilename` recognizes them by the `.fleet.jsonl` suffix and returns `{ sessionId: <uuid>, sidecar: true }`. Sidecar ingestion: (a) never fires the `'new-session'` event (cannot touch the pending-attach fallback path — a sidecar is not a new claude session); (b) skips the new-primary-file guard (sidecars are created out-of-band and may appear after the session is indexed); (c) ingests events normally — `session-summary` lines insert into `session_summaries` + `session_tags` and are scheduled for embedding. Sidecar files are never mirrored by the durable transcript mirror; they are DB data, not user transcript history.
- Mock mode (`CLAUDE_FLEET_MOCK=1`) skips watcher + DB entirely — no real JSONLs to read.

### Inbox store (`inbox.db`, #407)
`<userData>/inbox.db` is **authoritative, not a cache**, unlike `state.db`, so it is never dropped or rebuilt. better-sqlite3, WAL, `user_version` migrations; before migrating an *existing* file the store copies it to `inbox.db.bak-v<old>-<epochMs>`. It opens at startup in both mock and real mode (`InboxStore.open`, `src/main/inbox/inboxDb.ts`). Migrations still run synchronously on the raw connection at open; the stores then use one async **Kysely** handle over that connection (`InboxStore.kysely`, `src/main/store/kysely.ts`). Kysely's SQLite driver serializes every query behind a single-connection mutex and a transaction holds it until commit, so multi-statement writes (`post`, `update`, automation create/update, `markInterrupted`, rule/downtime upserts) stay atomic against every other store call. Code inside a transaction uses the transaction handle only. In-memory concurrency guards that used to be implicit in synchronous code are explicit: automation runs claim their automation id after the store.get call and before startRun inserts the run (one running + at most one queued per automation), `tick()` in the notification service is serialized, proposal decisions take a per-proposal guard, and `AutomationStore.update` accepts an `expectedVersion` (proposal apply/revert pass it, and reject a stale base). A todo `post`'s dedupe-or-reopen check and write is one transaction. `AutomationStore.setNextRun`/`setLastScheduled` take an optional guard (`WHERE id=? AND version=? AND enabled=1 [AND next_run_at=?]`) and return whether a row actually changed, so the scheduler's `tick()` — which reads a snapshot of all automations, then writes per automation — only commits a reschedule or `last_scheduled_at` bump if the automation is unchanged and still enabled; a stale tick (edited, disabled, or given a run-chosen next fire since the snapshot) is silently skipped rather than overwritten. `AutomationService.start()` refuses a non-manual trigger (schedule, event, rescheduled) on a disabled automation by throwing `DisabledError`, which the tick and the event-debounce path both catch and drop quietly — including an event-triggered run whose automation was disabled during its debounce window. A step failure within a run becomes a failed run row plus a notification; onError fires only on ENGINE failures (store writes, failedStart, debounce path), as track() wraps every execute call (including manual runs). `before-quit` calls the idempotent `stop()` each of `registerNotifyIpc`/`registerAutomationIpc` returns — clearing their tick/prune intervals, flipping a `stopped` guard, and cancelling pending input-wait/event-debounce timers (#457) — before `inboxStore.close()`, so no new tick or timer fires against the closed driver (a tick already in flight may still hit it; its error is caught and logged); in-flight runs are left to `recover()` on the next start rather than awaited. **No N+1 (#456):** per-query overhead through async Kysely (~18µs) runs higher than sync better-sqlite3 (~14µs), so per-row query loops are batched — `InboxStore.list`'s tags and `AutomationStore.runs`/`runsWithStatus`'s steps are each fetched with one `entity_id`/`run_id IN (...)` query per ≤500-id chunk (`chunked()` in `store/kysely.ts`) and grouped in JS, and `notifications:effectiveMany` reads `rules()`/`getDefaultLevel()` once per call (`effectiveForMany`) rather than once per item.
On the Postgres store the inbox tables live in the same schema as `state.db`'s, from a baseline equal to `MIGRATIONS` 1–5 (`store/migrations/inboxPg.ts`, track `inbox`), with the same Postgres type mapping. Each later SQLite migration has a Postgres step of the same version (`INBOX_PG_STEPS`: 6–8, the knowledge-claims tables below), expand-only, so a store already at 5 migrates in place; `schemaParity.test.ts` pins the result to the SQLite schema at `INBOX_SCHEMA_VERSION` (FTS5 shadow tables excluded). There is no file backup: the migration is one transaction, and backups are the database operator's.

```sql
CREATE TABLE todos (id TEXT PRIMARY KEY /* ULID */, title TEXT NOT NULL, body TEXT,
  status TEXT NOT NULL /* open|done|dismissed */, priority TEXT NOT NULL /* low|normal|high|urgent */,
  source_kind TEXT NOT NULL /* user|workspace|automation */, source_id TEXT, source_run_id TEXT, via_workspace TEXT,
  dedupe_key TEXT, seen_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1, recurred INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  workspace_id TEXT /* "jump to" link */, session_id TEXT,
  created_at INTEGER NOT NULL, updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL, first_seen_at INTEGER NOT NULL, last_seen_at INTEGER NOT NULL, closed_at INTEGER);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX todos_dedupe ON todos (source_kind, IFNULL(source_id,''), dedupe_key) WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL;
CREATE TABLE tags (entity_kind TEXT, entity_id TEXT, tag TEXT, PRIMARY KEY (entity_kind, entity_id, tag));  -- shared by todos (and later automations)
CREATE TABLE todo_events (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, todo_id TEXT, ts INTEGER, kind TEXT,
  actor_kind TEXT, actor_id TEXT, run_id TEXT, detail TEXT /* JSON */);  -- created|seen|reopened|status|priority|title|tag_add|tag_remove|body_append
```
- **`source` is required, immutable and host-stamped** (`user` from IPC, `workspace:<id>` from the MCP listener identity).
- **Dedupe:** a post whose `(source, key)` already exists updates that row: `seen_count+1`, `last_seen_at`, `priority = max(old, new)`, tags unioned (≤10), body replaced when a new body is given, title kept, plus a `seen` event. If the row is `done` **or** `dismissed`, it **reopens** (`status=open`, `recurred=1`, `closed_at=NULL`, plus a `reopened` event). There is no per-item mute; noise control is notification rules (a later phase).
- **Tags:** lowercased, spaces become `-`, `[a-z0-9._-]{1,40}`, at most 10 per item.
- **Schema v2 (notifications):** `notifications(id, ts, kind post|todo|input-wait|post-rejected|digest|test, title, body, priority, source_kind, source_id, todo_id, workspace_id, dedupe_key, tags JSON, delivered, held_reason 'rule:<id>'|'default-level'|'pause'|'downtime:<id>', channel os|in-app, cleared_at, digested)`; `notification_rules(id, scope_kind key|source|tag|global, scope_value, level all|high|urgent|off, snooze_until, …, UNIQUE(scope_kind, scope_value))`, where scope values are `key` → `<user | kind:id>:<dedupe key>`, `source` → `user` | `workspace:<id>`, and `tag` → the tag; `downtimes(id, name, enabled, days_mask bit0=Mon…bit6=Sun, start_min, end_min, bypass none|urgent|urgent+input)`; `notify_state(k, v)` holding `pause` `{until|null, letUrgent, setAt}`, `defaultLevel` (default `all`) and `quietSince`.
- **Notification policy** (`resolve`): `test`/`digest` always deliver. Otherwise the most specific rule wins (key > source > tag > the default level; among several tag rules the strictest), where an unexpired snooze reads as `off` and `levelAllows(level, priority)` gates (`high` = high+). Then quiet: an active pause and an active downtime are resolved by **most recent explicit action** (`pause.setAt` vs the downtime window's start). A pause holds unless `letUrgent` and the item is urgent; a downtime holds unless its bypass admits it (`urgent`, or `urgent+input` which also admits `input-wait`). Downtime windows use host local time; end ≤ start wraps past midnight and belongs to its start day; start = end is all day.
- **What raises a notification:** `notify` (MCP); `post_todo{notify:true}`; any todo created or reopened at `urgent`; a workspace waiting on input for 20 s that you aren't viewing (deduped per waiting episode by key `input-wait:<id>`, cleared when the wait ends); a refused post (once per workspace per hour); a digest when a quiet period ends with ≥1 held item ("Notifications resumed: N held", one summary, never a replay).
- **Schema v3 (automations):** `automations(id, name UNIQUE, enabled, version, definition JSON, created_by_kind/id, origin, created_at, updated_at, last_scheduled_at)`; `automation_versions(automation_id, version, definition, created_at, created_by_*)` (every saved version); `automation_state(automation_id, state JSON)` (the last `{"state":…}` a script printed; Phase 7); `runs(id, automation_id, version, trigger JSON {kind schedule|manual|catch-up, by?, cron?}, args JSON, status queued|running|succeeded|nothing-to-do|failed|timed-out|skipped|cancelled|interrupted, started_at, ended_at, error)`; `step_runs(run_id, step_id, kind, status, workspace_id, broker_session_id, started_at, ended_at, error, detail JSON {via attached|headless})`. Automation tags use the shared `tags` table (`entity_kind='automation'`). Runs older than 90 days are pruned at startup. Todos gain `via_workspace` / `source_run_id` from automation attribution.
- **Schema v4 (proposals):** `proposals(id, kind new|change|batch, proposer_kind/id, rationale, status pending|approved|partial|rejected|superseded|auto-approved, items JSON, note, created_at, decided_at)`. Each item is `{automationId, name, baseVersion, before, after, lowRisk, reasons[], flags[], decision pending|accepted|declined|auto-applied|reverted}`. Never pruned (audit trail). `InboxStore.counts().approvals` = pending proposals.
- **Schema v5 (reschedule, #437):** `automations.next_run_at` / `next_run_by` (a next fire a run chose, and that run's id; replaces the cron until it fires) and `runs.reschedule` JSON `{requestedAt, appliedAt|null, clamped, by, reason?}`. Run triggers gain kind `rescheduled`.
- **Schema v6 (knowledge claims, #483):** `claims(id /* ULID */, text, kind decision|fact|gotcha|root-cause|rejected, topics JSON array (1-3 slugs), refs JSON array?, visibility fleet|private DEFAULT fleet, status current|superseded|retracted DEFAULT current, superseded_by, retract_reason, workspace_id /* authoring, host-stamped */, confirmations INTEGER DEFAULT 1 /* bumped on a near-duplicate re-record */, created_at, updated_at)`, indexed on `(status, visibility, workspace_id)`; `claim_sources(id AUTOINCREMENT, claim_id, workspace_id, session_id /* NULL unless via=capture, see §3 session-attribution note above */, via agent|capture, capture_run, ts)` — one row per record/confirm event, provenance trail; `claim_vectors(claim_id PRIMARY KEY, model_id, vec BLOB)` — same embedding model id as `embeddings` (transcript search), so query and claim vectors are directly comparable, and are recomputed from `text` after a model change (`ClaimService.backfillVectors`); `claims_fts` an FTS5 virtual table (`claim_id UNINDEXED, text, topics`) for BM25 keyword search, fused with vector cosine by reciprocal rank in `search_claims` — on the Postgres store `claims_fts` is a plain side table with the same columns, searched with `to_tsvector('simple', …) @@` an OR of per-token `plainto_tsquery('simple', …)` ranked by `ts_rank` (`ClaimStore.ftsIds`, which branches on `InboxStore.dialect`), and `claim_vectors.vec` is `BYTEA`; `capture_runs(id /* ULID, doubles as the run token */, workspace_id, session_id /* the live session forked */, fork_session_id, trigger ctx-<pct> (one per configured context band, e.g. ctx-85) | turns | search, status running|done|failed|timed-out, claims_recorded DEFAULT 0, usage JSON?, error?, started_at, ended_at?)`, indexed on `(session_id, started_at)` and `(status)` — `CaptureRunStore.failRunning` marks any `running` row `failed` at app start (interrupted by restart). All five tables are reachable only through the async Kysely handle (`ClaimStore`/`CaptureRunStore` in `src/main/claims/`); claims are never bind-mounted or exposed via the `query` MCP tool's snapshot.
- **Schema v7 (#483 §5a):** `capture_settings_log(id ULID, workspace_id, ts, actor user|workspace, "before" JSON, "after" JSON, reason?, applied 0|1, proposal_id?)` indexed `(workspace_id, ts)` — append-only; form edits log as `user` with no reason, MCP changes as `workspace` with their reason, approvals as `user` with the proposal id. `proposals.payload` (JSON `CaptureSettingsChange` for `capture-settings` proposals); because SQLite can't alter the v4 `CHECK (kind IN …)`, v7 rebuilds `proposals` (copy, drop, rename, recreate `proposals_status`) with the kind CHECK widened to admit `capture-settings`. `capture_runs.user_turns` (the session's user-turn count when the run started; `recaptureAfterTurns` is measured from it) and index `idx_capture_runs_started` (the daily-cap count, `CaptureRunStore.countSince(startOfLocalDay(now))`).
- **Schema v8 (#483 final review):** `capture_runs.visibility` (the workspace's effective `captureVisibility` when the run was admitted; `record_claim` with that run's `capture_run` token forces `visibility='private'` when it is `private`, whatever the fork asked for) and `capture_runs.compactions` (the session's compaction count at admit; seeds the ingest policy's fired context bands after an app restart, §5 *Knowledge capture*). NULL on rows from before v8.
- **Manifest `inbox` block** (host-private, `WorkspaceSpec.inbox`): `{ canPost?: boolean (default true), maxPostsPerHour?: number (default 60, capped 10 000), canReadAll?: boolean (default false), trusted?: boolean (default false; container workspaces only), exposeSkills?: boolean (default true) }`. `exposeSkills` gates whether `list_workspace_skills`/`get_workspace_skill` (§11 *Fleet-state MCP*) may see this workspace's skills at all; the form only ever writes `false` (opt-out). The manifest also gains `role?: 'automations'` (sanitized; carried through `workspaceMerge`). It is sanitized by `sanitizeInboxConfig` in the `readWorkspaceManifest` allowlist constructor, carried through `workspaceMerge.ts`, and edited in the Edit-workspace modal ("Inbox & automations": can post + posts per hour + an "Expose skills to automations" checkbox, default on). Only non-default values are written.
- **Manifest `knowledgeCapture` settings** (#483 §5a, `WorkspaceSpec.knowledgeCapture?: CaptureSettings`): an object; absent ⇒ all defaults. Fields (default, range): `enabled` (true), `onSearchHit` (true), `searchTopSessions` (3, 1–5), `recaptureAfterTurns` (30, 10–500), `contextBands` ([0.85], 0–4 values each 0.50–0.95, deduplicated and sorted), `turnsEvery` (0 = off, or 10–500), `model` (`"session"` or a model id matching `^claude-[a-z0-9.\-\[\]]{1,60}$`), `transcriptRetentionDays` (365, 30–3650), `captureVisibility` (`fleet` | `private`). The parser is an allowlist and every value is clamped on read (`readWorkspaceManifest`) and on write (`writeWorkspaceManifest`), via the pure shared module `src/preload/captureSettings.ts`. A legacy boolean `false` (task 10) reads as `{ ...defaults, enabled: false }`; `true` reads as absent. The global `config.json` `knowledgeCapture` switch must also be on. Carried through `workspaceMerge.ts` like `inbox`. Edited in the Edit-workspace modal's **Knowledge capture** disclosure (edit mode only; one control per field; `captureSettingsView.ts` maps the string inputs and clamps; defaults are submitted as absent). `formInitial.ts` maps the field, so an edit never resets it. `transcriptRetentionDays` becomes Claude Code's `cleanupPeriodDays` for the app's own launches (see *Docker container shape* / *Local backend*).

### Vault layout
Secret env-var values live in `<userData>/secrets.enc`: a single JSON object `{ "<workspaceId>": { "<envVarName>": "<value>" } }`, serialized, encrypted with `safeStorage.encryptString`, and stored base64. One file holds the whole fleet's secrets (no per-account keychain entries, no separate index — listing a workspace's keys is just the object keys of its bag). `vault.ts` keeps the decrypted store cached in memory and serializes writes through a lock.

Why a file rather than `keytar`: keytar requires a Secret Service daemon on Linux that's absent on bare WSL, so secrets silently failed there. `safeStorage` encrypts with the OS keyring when available; on Linux with no keyring, `vault.ts` calls `setUsePlainTextEncryption(true)` once so encrypt/decrypt still work (base64 plaintext — see §9 for the trade), making the file usable everywhere (see §4 *Stack*). Secrets written under the old keytar scheme are not migrated — users re-enter API keys once.

The old `__profiles__:*` keytar shape (global per-profile API keys) is gone. The startup migration in `src/main/migration.ts` still runs a best-effort purge of legacy keytar entries (lazy `import('keytar')`, no-op if libsecret is absent), so pre-existing installs don't leave stale credentials in the OS keychain.

Values are read from the renderer only on explicit `vault:getSecret`; during normal operation, the main process consumes them directly via `resolveEnv` when constructing the container env (not round-tripped through the UI).

The vault also holds **endpoint API keys**: scope `endpoint:<id>`, key `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` — the same file, the same safeStorage envelope, a different namespace. `deleteEndpoint(id)` purges the whole `endpoint:<id>` scope from the vault. The endpoint id is a UUID (not a ULID — `endpoints.ts` uses `crypto.randomUUID()`). See *Model endpoint registry* below for the non-secret part.

The vault also holds **loadout-source tokens** (#444):
- **Scope and key:** scope `loadout-source:<normalised base>` (e.g. `loadout-source:ghcr.io/acme/loadouts`), key `TOKEN`. The same file, safeStorage envelope and lock as the other scopes.
- **Purge:** `removeSource(base)` and `setSourceCredential(base, null)` purge the whole scope.
- **No read path:** unlike workspace and endpoint scopes, the generic `vault:*` IPC refuses any `loadout-source:` scope (`vaultScopes.assertRendererVaultScope`) and fails closed on a non-string scope, key or value (an array or `String` object survives structured clone and would otherwise be coerced to the real string). The renderer can write a source token only through `loadouts:addSource` / `loadouts:setSourceCredential`, and can never read one back.
- **Reserved workspace ids.** `loadout-source:`, `endpoint:`, `identity:` and `store:` (`vaultScopes.RESERVED_VAULT_PREFIXES`) are also reserved as workspace-id prefixes, since a workspace id doubles as its own vault scope: `workspace:create` and `workspace:writeManifest` reject a renderer-supplied id containing `:` or starting with either prefix (`assertRendererWorkspaceId`); `vault.resolveEnv` refuses a reserved scope as a backstop via the shared `vaultScopes.assertNotReservedVaultScope` helper. The automation runner's script-step secret lookup (`automationIpc.ts`'s `secret` dependency) applies the same guard before calling `getSecret` (#444 final review M6), so a reserved scope can never be read out through a script step either.

The vault also holds the **signed-in GitHub identity** (remote-workspaces (b)):
- **Scope and keys:** scope `identity:github` (`vaultScopes.GITHUB_IDENTITY_SCOPE`), keys `TOKEN` (the OAuth token) and `PROFILE` (JSON `{ identity, scopes, sso, verifiedAt }`). The same file, envelope and lock as the other scopes. PROFILE is written before TOKEN; a PROFILE that fails validation (wrong shape, a machine identity, a non-GitHub provider) reads as signed out.
- **No read path:** `identity:` is host-only like `loadout-source:`. The generic `vault:*` IPC refuses it (`assertRendererVaultScope`), and it's a reserved workspace-id prefix (`RESERVED_VAULT_PREFIXES`).
- **Degraded vault:** when `isVaultAvailable()` is false, the sign-in is held in main-process memory for the run (`AccountStatus.persisted: false`).
- **Purge:** sign-out and a 401 on refresh delete the whole scope.

The vault also holds the **managed Postgres password** (async store part 4b):
- **Scope and key:** scope `store:managed` (`vaultScopes.MANAGED_STORE_SCOPE`), key `APP_PASSWORD`: the `fleet_app` role's password, minted on first start.
- **No read path:** `store:` is host-only like `identity:`, and a reserved workspace-id prefix.
- **Lost vault:** the next start mints a new password and the bootstrap sets it (the superuser needs none), so the data survives.

### Model endpoint registry (on disk)
`<userData>/endpoints.json` is a JSON array of `ModelEndpoint` objects — the app-level list of Anthropic-format endpoints workspaces with `authMode 'endpoint'` point claude-code at. Only non-secret fields live here; the API key (if any) lives in the vault as described above. The file is read-through-cache in memory (`endpoints.ts`) and written atomically on every `endpoints:save` / `endpoints:delete` / `endpoints:setApiKey` call.

```ts
interface ModelEndpoint {
  id: string;            // UUID (crypto.randomUUID)
  name: string;          // user label, free-form
  baseUrl: string;       // Anthropic-format base URL; no trailing slash; claude-code appends /v1/messages itself
  modelId: string;       // model id passed as ANTHROPIC_MODEL
  smallFastModelId?: string; // haiku-class model; defaults to modelId when absent
  contextLength?: number; // display metadata only — the server owns the real limit
  hasApiKey: boolean;    // true iff a key is stored in the vault (the key itself is never serialised here)
  notes?: string;
}
```

Writes are validated before persisting (`name`, `baseUrl` with http/https prefix, `modelId` all required). The registry tolerates a missing or malformed file by returning `[]`. There is no migration path for the old per-profile keytar shape — `endpoints.json` is new in #250.

### Loadout library (#16-followup)
A **loadout** is a reusable, machine-parseable bundle of Claude config the user installs into a workspace on demand. Authored loadouts live at `<userData>/loadouts/<id>/`, each a folder with a `loadout.md` (**YAML frontmatter + markdown body** — the Claude-Code idiom, so a Claude instance parses it natively):
- **Frontmatter** = `title`, `version` (semver), `description` (the relevance signal), `tags`, `dependencies { loadouts: [{id, version?}], tools: [{cmd, version?}] }`, `scripts[] {label, run|file, unless?}`, `prompts[] {label, send|file}`. **Body** = install-instructions prose.
- **Convention-mapped files**: anything under `skills/ commands/ agents/ rules/ output-styles/` plus a root `CLAUDE.md` is part of the bundle — no need to list files.
- **Automations (#407 Phase 8)**: `automations/<folder>/automation.yaml` (plus script files beside it) ships an automation. At install it is parsed, `$self` (in `prompt.workspace`, `script.runIn`, `event.workspace`, `manual.allowFrom`) binds to the install target, `run: ./file.sh` is inlined from the folder (no `..`, ≤20 000 chars), and it is validated like the editor (`loadoutAutomations.ts`). It is created with `origin {loadoutId, version, workspaceId, folder, installedVersion}`; a second install target gets its own copy (`<name>-<workspace>`). **First install:** each valid automation is ON unless unticked, missing a declared secret where it runs (then OFF, "add the secret … to turn it on"), or it has a host step, which isn't installed unless the review's "Allow this loadout's automations to run scripts on this computer" is ticked. **Reinstall/Update ↑:** an automation unchanged since install (`version == installedVersion`) takes the new definition (enabled state kept); one you edited is kept (and reported); new ones arrive OFF. Automations dropped from a newer version are left alone. **Uninstall** removes that install's automations (run history kept). `installedLoadouts[].automations` lists their names. (`loadoutInstaller.ts`, wired through `setLoadoutAutomationHooks` in `loadouts.ts`.)
