**Apply model** (drives install + uninstall): **① drop** — convention files copied into the workspace's project root `.claude/` (for a container that's `<fleetRoot>/<id>/.claude/`, claude's cwd); **② merge** — `CLAUDE.md` appended as a marked `<!-- loadout:<id> start/end -->` block; `settings.json` blended into `.claude/settings.json` at **top-level-key** granularity (a key is added only if absent — collisions skipped + reported, no deep merge into an existing key in v1); named servers from `.mcp.json` merged into the workspace's root `.mcp.json`; hooks (from a dedicated `hooks.json` and/or `settings.json`'s `hooks`) appended **per-event** into `settings.hooks`; **③ run** — `scripts` run in the container at install via `docker exec` (`runInWorkspace`), each with an optional `unless` skip-guard (exit 0 ⇒ skip) and an inline `run` or a `file`; per-script results (`ran`/`skipped`/`failed`) return in the install result and surface in the review on failure. Scripts have side effects and are **not** reverted on uninstall. (`prompts` sent to the reloaded session are next.) Install is **collision-safe** (existing files/keys/servers are skipped + reported, never clobbered) and **container-only** in v1 (a local workspace runs in the user's real repo). `WorkspaceSpec.installedLoadouts[]` records the exact dropped files **and** merges (CLAUDE.md flag, settings keys, mcp server names, hook entries by value) so **uninstall reverts precisely** — removing only what the loadout added and keeping anything edited after install.

The pure engine is `src/main/loadoutCore.ts` (parse + apply/revert; fs+yaml only, unit-tested); `src/main/loadouts.ts` is the electron-aware wrapper (resolve `loadoutsRoot`/`fleetPrivateDir`, manifest read/write, `ensureBuiltinLoadouts` seeds 2 starters on first run).

**Run layer — reload (the "in-place `--resume`").** A loadout's files/merges land in the workspace's `.claude/`, but claude only reads its config **at session start** — so a freshly-installed loadout has no effect on a *running* session until claude restarts. Rather than make the user restart (the old destructive `stop`/`start` path loses the conversation and isn't reused here), the reload **closes and re-resumes the active session in place**: ① resolve the active tab's Claude session UUID (`sessions:resolveResumeTarget` — verified mappings only; unverified ⇒ the reload is skipped with an explicit toast rather than resuming a guess), ② `pty:closeSession` the live handle (broker CLOSE ⇒ claude exits, transcript flushed to `<uuid>.jsonl`, session dropped), ③ re-attach the **same** broker session id with `resumeOf=<uuid>` ⇒ the broker `CREATE`s `claude --resume <uuid>`, so the **same tab** picks the conversation back up — now with the loadout loaded. This close+resume is driven by a **per-session token map** in `TerminalPane`: `reloadTargets: Record<sessionId, token>`. Each `TerminalSession` is handed its own `reloadToken={reloadTargets[id] ?? null}`; when that number advances, the session runs the close+resume against itself. **Two producers feed the map**, both deferred until the target session is idle (busy/idle from the title-glyph `activityDetector`):

- **Loadout reload.** `LibraryPane`→`onInstalled(workspaceId)`→`App` (only if **autoReloadLoadouts** is on **and** the workspace is a running container) sets a per-workspace `reloadRequest`; the matching `TerminalPane` holds it pending and, once the **active session** is idle, bumps `reloadTargets[activeId]` (and fires `onReloadStarted` → the "Applying loadout…" toast).
- **Manual Refresh** (chip ⋮ menu, §6). `requestRefresh(s)` enqueues `s.id` into a `pendingRefresh: Set<sessionId>`; a drain effect bumps `reloadTargets[id]` for every queued id that is idle, not ended, and still present (the pure `readyToRefresh` helper, unit-tested). App shows the shared toast at click time. This targets **the specific tab** (even a background one), unlike the loadout path's active-only target — which is why the single target became a map.

If no resumable UUID exists (claude never started in that tab), it's a no-op — the files are already in place for the next start. The close+resume touches the broker session lifecycle and can't be exercised in mock/CI (needs a real `claude --resume`); the loadout path is verified by live container test, and the manual Refresh's queue/idle-gate logic is unit-tested (`readyToRefresh`) with an e2e covering the menu→toast wiring in mock mode.

**Shipped so far:** the format/parse, the **drop** + **CLAUDE.md marked-block** install/uninstall, manifest tracking, collision-safety, container-only, the IPC surface, and the **left-rail Library UI** — an accordion (Sessions + Library) with search + tag-filter, detail cards with per-workspace install/uninstall, and a review-before-install modal (files manifest + 🗀 Open folder). Plus the **merge layer** — `settings.json` (top-level keys), `.mcp.json` (named servers), and hooks blended in with precise tracked revert, surfaced in the review modal (a "Merges into config" list + a "Hooks · run on events" group). Plus **scripts** in the run layer — a loadout's setup commands run in the container at install (`docker exec`, `unless`-guarded), with results surfaced. Plus the **in-place `--resume` reload** — installing a loadout into a running workspace closes + re-resumes its active session so the loadout takes effect without losing the conversation, gated on an **auto-reload setting** (default on) firing only while claude is idle (see *Run layer — reload* above). Still **planned** (below): `prompts` sent to the reloaded session, versioning/dependency resolution + the Requirements preflight, the authoring modal, and the MCP discovery tools.

**Host-private boundary (invariant, §9).** The library at `<userData>/loadouts/` is in the host-private zone — **never bind-mounted into any container**, like the DB / vault / transcript mirrors. A workspace only ever sees the **installed snapshot** copied into its own `.claude/` + `CLAUDE.md`, never the library folder. A workspace's Claude discovers loadouts (to judge relevance and recommend) **only through the read-only MCP `list_loadouts`/`get_loadout` tools** — *mediated* metadata the main process chooses to expose, exactly the §9 rule (mediated, never a raw bind mount). Discovery ≠ filesystem visibility.

**Versioning, dependencies & sync (design; PR2+).**
- **semver.** `loadout.md` carries a `version` (`parseLoadout` → `LoadoutMeta.version`, a bare YAML number stringified, blank ⇒ absent; also on `LoadoutSummary`). `installLoadout` records it as `installedLoadouts[].version`, so each workspace knows what it has; unversioned loadouts and pre-existing records have none. *Not yet built:* a version-stamped install marker (`<!-- loadout:<id>@<version> start -->`) and the `semver` dep (comparison is `ociCore.compareVersions`).
- **Editing the source.** A loadout folder is filesystem-first and the app re-parses it on demand, so editing the source needs no "push" — the library reflects it immediately. Edit it via the **authoring modal** or **Open folder → your editor** (both first-class). A workspace's **Claude** authors a loadout by writing the folder **into its own workspace** (where it has write access); the host then **imports** it into the library — Claude never writes the host-private library directly (the host mediates).
- **Pushing an edit into installed workspaces is host-side**, reusing the install/uninstall engine: **Update** (when the source's semver > the workspace's installed version → revert old snapshot, install new — surfaced as "update available") and **Reinstall/Sync** (force re-apply the *current* source, for the author iteration loop where the version isn't bumped each save). Both run in the **main process** (it owns each workspace's private dir), keep collision-safety, **keep + flag** any file the user edited after install (never silent overwrite), and re-stamp the version marker. The container picks it up on its next session (same as install). Updates are **explicit/reviewed** (scripts re-run, files change), never automatic.
- **Both versions live on the host** — the library's `loadout.md` and the workspace's host-private manifest — so the container **never participates in version sync** and never sees the library.
- **Writes never go through the MCP server.** Edit/import/install/update/uninstall are mutations and run in the privileged **main process via IPC + the loadouts engine**. The MCP server stays **read-only** — its loadout role is **discovery only** (`list_loadouts`/`get_loadout`). A future *autonomous* install/update triggered by a workspace's Claude would be a deliberate, opt-in **write RPC** whose effect still executes in the main process, not the MCP server touching the filesystem.
- **Loadout dependencies** resolve **install-first** (topo-ordered, cycle-guarded). The library holds exactly **one** version of each loadout on disk, so a dependency `version` range is a *satisfies-check* against that single version (no multi-version solver) — an unsatisfiable range blocks with the exact mismatch.
- **Tool dependencies** are *declared expectations*, **preflight-checked** in the workspace (`command -v` + optional `--version` range) and surfaced in the review's **Requirements** section (✓ present / ✗ missing or wrong version). Since the runner image is **non-root** (no `apt`), runtimes are provided by the loadout's own **user-space bootstrap scripts** (`rustup`, `uv`, `nvm` into `$HOME`), guarded by `scripts[].unless` (e.g. `unless: command -v cargo`) so they run only when missing. System packages needing root are **not** auto-installed — the recommended escape hatch is a custom runner image (e.g. the **dev** variant above, which bakes build-essential/Python/Go/Rust). A missing **`required: true`** tool hard-blocks; otherwise install warns + proceeds (the review showed the risk).

Renderer: `LeftRail.tsx` (the accordion shell — Sessions section reuses `SessionsPane` in `embedded` mode + the new Library section), `LibraryPane.tsx` (search/tag-filter + cards + install/uninstall), `LoadoutReviewModal.tsx` (the review). **Card collapse (long-list ergonomics):** each loadout card is independently collapsible via a disclosure **chevron** in its title row (collapsed = title + action only; the body — description + tags — hides). The chevron toggles that one card (stops propagation, so the card-body click still opens the review modal); a header **Collapse all / Expand all** toggle (shown when >1 card is visible) acts on the currently-filtered set, its label following whether most visible cards are open. Cards default **expanded**; the set of collapsed ids persists to `localStorage` under `loadoutLibraryCollapsed` (a pure UI preference, like the rail-collapse state) — a loadout not in the set is expanded, so new loadouts appear open. Still forthcoming: the **Requirements** section in the review (tool preflight, with the merge/run layer), the authoring modal, and the read-only MCP discovery tools.

### Loadout library v2 — Phase 1: local catalog, favorites, browser modal

**Phase 1 is implemented.** Design doc + hi-fi mocks: [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-28-loadout-library-v2-design.md`](superpowers/specs/2026-06-28-loadout-library-v2-design.md). The pure OCI logic core (`src/main/ociCore.ts` — ref parsing, artifact-layer path sanitizer, index parsing, version compare) was implemented and unit-tested in an earlier step; Phase 1 wires the user-facing layer on top of the local-only catalog.

**IPC (Phase 1, all privileged main process):**
- `loadouts:catalog(workspaceId?)` → `CatalogEntry[]` — assembles the local library with per-workspace install state (`installed`, `installedVersion`, `updateAvailable`), `present` (folder exists on disk), and `favorited` (from `config.json`). Returns all entries when `workspaceId` is omitted (used by the browser modal, which shows the full catalog regardless of workspace selection).
- `loadouts:setFavorite(id, on)` → `string[]` — toggles one loadout's membership in `config.json`'s `favorites` set and returns the updated set. Favorites are global (user-level, not workspace-scoped).

**Favorites (global).** `config.json` gains a `favorites: string[]` field. The field is absent-tolerant (defaults to `[]`) and is written by `loadouts:setFavorite`. The catalog builder reads it and sets `favorited: boolean` per entry. There is one favorites set for the whole app; per-entry `installed`/`updateAvailable` state is relative to the selected workspace.

**Left-rail additions (Phase 1).** The library pane gains two affordances beside the existing Tags dropdown: a **`.fav-filter`** toggle button (☆/★) that, when active (`.on`), filters the card list to `favorited === true` only; and a **`.lib-browse`** "Browse all" button that opens the browser modal. Each expanded card gains a **`.lc-fav`** button (☆ Favorite / ★ Favorited) that calls `loadouts:setFavorite` and triggers a catalog reload.

**Browser modal (`LoadoutBrowserModal`).** Renders as `.modal.loadout-browser` (720 px wide, flex column). Layout: `.lb-head` (title + close); `.lb-body` grid (200 px `.lb-facets` sidebar + `.lb-results` list). The facets sidebar has a `.lb-search` input and a `.lb-tagcloud` of `.lb-tag` pills (each togglable, `.on` when active — tag filter requires *all* active tags), plus a `.lb-sources` section added in Phase 2 (see below). Each result row is `.lb-row` with `.lb-row-main` (title, tags, description) and `.lb-row-actions` (`.lc-fav` toggle + Install / Installed button).

**Styles (new classes, `styles.css`).** `.fav-filter`, `.fav-filter.on`, `.lc-fav`, `.lc-fav.on`, `.lib-browse`, `.modal.loadout-browser`, `.lb-head`, `.lb-body`, `.lb-facets`, `.lb-search`, `.lb-tagcloud`, `.lb-tag`, `.lb-tag.on`, `.lb-results`, `.lb-row`, `.lb-row-main`, `.lb-row-title`, `.lb-row-desc`, `.lb-row-actions`, `.lb-empty`. All use the existing token set (`--ok`, `--rule`, `--bg-3`, `--ink`, `--ink-2`, `--r-sm`, `--r-md`). Phase 2 adds: `.lb-sources`, `.lb-sources-head`, `.lb-source-row`, `.lb-source-name`, `.lb-source-remove`, `.lb-add-source`, `.lb-source-error`, `.btn.update`. Private sources (#444) add: `.lb-source-item`, `.lb-source-lock`, `.lb-source-cred-actions`, `.lb-cred`, `.lb-cred-head`, `.lb-cred-actions`.

### Loadout library v2 — Phase 2: remote OCI sources + update detection

**Phase 2 is implemented.** The paired index publisher shipped in `claude-fleet-loadouts` (the `publish-loadouts.yml` workflow emits `<source>/index:latest` with artifactType `application/vnd.claude-fleet.loadout-index.v1`). Per the workspace CLAUDE.md cross-repo contract, both sides landed in the same change.

**`src/main/ociClient.ts` — GHCR pull client.** A native, zero-dependency OCI pull module (`fetch` only, no `oras` binary), supporting both anonymous and credentialed pulls. The **token flow**: probe the manifest URL; if the registry returns HTTP 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm=…`, exchange the parsed realm/service/scope for a bearer token via the GHCR token endpoint — anonymously when no credential is supplied (the common case for the public default source), or via the Basic exchange described below when one is. The token is then attached as `Authorization: Bearer` for all subsequent requests (manifest + blobs). If the probe returns anything other than 401, no token is requested (the manifest is already publicly accessible or the registry allows unauthenticated pull). Key exports: `pullArtifact(ref, destDir, { cred? })` — fetches the manifest and streams each layer's blob into `destDir`, writing each file at its `org.opencontainers.image.title` annotation path via `safeLayerPath` (from `ociCore.ts`) to prevent directory traversal; `fetchAnnotations(ref, { cred? })` — returns the manifest's top-level `annotations` object without pulling any blobs (used for provenance/version checks). **Security invariants**: every layer title goes through `safeLayerPath` (throws on `../` or absolute paths — aborts the whole pull); each blob is checked against a 5 MiB `MAX_BLOB_BYTES` cap before writing (manifest `size` field + `Content-Length` + final buffer all checked); all requests carry a 30s `AbortSignal` timeout. Non-GHCR registries are rejected by `parseImageRef` before any network call.

**Private sources (#444).** `cred` is an optional `RegistryCredential { token, username? }` (`ociCore.ts`).

`registryToken(repository, manifestUrl, cred?)` replaces the former anonymous-only helper. It runs the same 401 → `WWW-Authenticate` → realm flow, but with `cred` it sends `Authorization: Basic base64(<username or "token">:<token>)` to the realm. It does so only when `ociCore.realmMayReceiveCredential(registryHost, realm)` holds: an `https:` realm on the registry's own host, or a `KNOWN_REALM_HOSTS` pairing (`ghcr.io`→`ghcr.io`, `registry-1.docker.io`→`auth.docker.io`). Any other realm is asked anonymously, so a hostile `WWW-Authenticate` can't redirect a source's token. The exact post-query token-exchange URL is checked (not just the advertised `realm` substring), and `fetch`'s default behavior of stripping `Authorization` on a cross-origin redirect is relied on for the GHCR blob → CDN hop — do not swap the fetch implementation or add an explicit `redirect` option without preserving that.

**Static-token fallback:** if the probe returns 401 with no realm, or the token exchange is answered 401/403, the credential is sent as a direct `Bearer <token>` on the manifest and blob requests. That header only ever goes to the registry host.

**Token shape + error sanitisation.** A token is validated against `isTokenShaped` (1–4096 bytes of `\x21-\x7e`) before it ever reaches a `Headers`/`fetch` call (`assertTokenShape`), because undici's own rejection of an invalid header value echoes the raw value in its thrown message. Any other transport throw (DNS failure, abort, a future bug) is caught and re-thrown naming only the stage and host, never the original message or `cause`. A non-OK token-exchange or manifest JSON body is never echoed into an error message.

**Errors:** `RegistryAuthError` carries `kind`:
- `needs-credential`: a 401/403 on the manifest, no credential was sent;
- `credential-rejected`: a 401/403 on the manifest, a credential was sent and refused; message `credential rejected (HTTP <n>)`;
- `malformed`: the credential itself isn't header-shaped (`assertTokenShape`) — thrown before any request, so there is no real HTTP status; message `credential is malformed` (#444 final review M2: this used to throw `credential-rejected` with a fake status 400, misrepresenting a shape problem as a registry decision). `withSourceAuth` (`loadoutSources.ts`) maps this to the same IPC contract as a pasted malformed token: `malformed-credential: <base>` — so an invalid `FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN*` surfaces the same way as a bad paste.

Messages never contain the token. `parseImageRef` still pins the registry to `ghcr.io`.

**`src/main/loadoutSources.ts` — sources, provenance, download.** Manages `<userData>/loadouts/sources.json` (shape: `{ sources: string[], provenance: Record<string, { source, version, downloadedAt }>, credentials: Record<base, { username?: string }> }`). `credentials` records only that a source has a vault token, plus its non-secret username — the token is never in this file, and a file without the key reads as `{}`. A fresh install (no `sources.json`) is seeded with `DEFAULT_SOURCES` (`ghcr.io/imioimi/claude-fleet-loadouts`) and the file is written, so the published library shows up without setup; a removed default stays removed, and an existing `sources.json` is never re-seeded. A corrupt or unreadable file fails closed (every read and write throws) instead of being treated as empty and overwritten. All mutations — including the paired vault + JSON writes a credential change makes — are serialised through one module-level promise-chain lock, and every write replaces the file atomically (temp file + rename). On read, every base is canonicalised (`normalizeBase`: trimmed, `oci://` stripped, trailing slash stripped, lower-cased) and de-duplicated (`sources` becomes a `Set`), so a hand-edited file with case or `oci://` variants of the same base collapses to one entry. `normalizeBase` also rejects (generic `invalid source`, naming nothing from the input) any base that still contains `@` or `://` after the `oci://` prefix is stripped — a base smuggling embedded credentials (`user:pass@host/repo`) or another scheme marker would otherwise reach `parseImageRef`, whose own error messages echo the invalid segment verbatim (#444 final review M4). Exports: `listSources()` → `string[]`; `listSourceInfo()` → `SourceInfo[]` (`{ base, hasCredential, username? }`, never the token); `addSource(base, cred?)` — validates the source is reachable by pulling and parsing its index, then appends to `sources[]`; `setSourceCredential(base, cred | null)` — sets/replaces/clears a configured source's token, then force-refreshes its index; `removeSource(base)` — purges the source's vault scope, then removes it from `sources[]`/`credentials` and clears the in-memory index cache; `browseSource(base, { refresh? })` — returns the cached index or pulls a fresh one; `allRemote()` — maps every configured source to its index (failed sources are skipped + logged, not fatal, so a temporarily unreachable source doesn't block the catalog); `download(source, id, version?)` — pulls the loadout artifact into `loadoutDir(id)` (i.e. `<userData>/loadouts/<id>/`) using the source's resolved credential, and writes provenance, recording the `version` from the pulled `loadout.md` (so a `latest` pull records e.g. `1.1.0`; falls back to the requested tag, then `latest`). The index ref is constructed as `<base>/index:latest`.

**Credential resolution (#444).** `credentialFor(base)` runs in the main process only and resolves, in order:
1. the vault token (`loadout-source:<base>` / `TOKEN`, with `credentials[base].username`);
2. `FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN_<SANITISED_BASE>` (base upper-cased, every non-alphanumeric → `_`, e.g. `FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN_GHCR_IO_ACME_LOADOUTS`);
3. for `ghcr.io/` bases other than `DEFAULT_SOURCES`, the signed-in GitHub token. `ipc.ts` registers `IdentityService.packagesToken` with `setGithubTokenProvider`, which returns the token only when its saved scopes include `read:packages` (or `write:packages`). The provider isn't called for the public default, which is always pulled anonymously, so a revoked or SSO-limited token can't break it;
4. `FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN`, for any source not in `DEFAULT_SOURCES`;
5. otherwise, anonymous.

There is no headless pin mechanism yet (`FLEET_LOADOUTS` isn't implemented). The env steps are its hook. `pullIndex` (so `browseSource`/`allRemote`) and `download` pull with the resolved credential. The resolved token is also stripped from a local (non-container) workspace's own env before `claude` spawns (`FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN` and any `FLEET_LOADOUT_TOKEN_*`), since it's host-only — the main process reads it to pull loadouts, but a workspace's claude must never inherit it.

**Pasted-token handling.** A credential submitted via IPC is trimmed and validated before anything is stored (`cleanCredential`): the token must be non-empty after trimming and header-shaped (`isTokenShaped` — 1–4096 bytes of `\x21-\x7e`); an optional username must be ≤256 chars with no `:` and no control characters. A failure here throws `malformed-credential: <base>` before any vault or `sources.json` write. Separately, inside `ociClient`, an empty/whitespace-only token on a pull is treated as "no credential" (falls through to anonymous) rather than malformed.

**Validate-then-commit.** `addSource(base, cred?)` stores `cred` in the vault (and `credentials[base]` in `sources.json`) *before* validating by pulling the index, then rolls back to the previous token/entry (or clears them, for a source that had none) on **any** validation failure — an auth rejection, a network error, a bad index, anything, not only an auth-specific one. `setSourceCredential` makes the same before/validate/rollback-on-any-failure round trip when replacing or clearing a configured source's token. The rollback re-reads the vault immediately before restoring and skips entirely if the token it's about to overwrite no longer matches what *this call* wrote (a concurrent credential change or `removeSource` already landed), so it never clobbers newer state. A failed rollback itself is logged (source base only, never a token).

Auth failures cross IPC as plain `Error` messages with a parseable prefix, because Electron carries only the message:
- `needs-credential: <base>`;
- `credential-rejected: <base> (HTTP <n>)`;
- `malformed-credential: <base>`.

The renderer parses them in `sourceErrors.ts`.

**Catalog remote merge (`src/main/loadoutCatalog.ts`).** `buildLoadoutCatalog(workspaceId?)` now calls `allRemote()` and passes the result to `assembleCatalog` (in `ociCore.ts`). The catalog merges local library entries with remote index entries: a remote loadout whose `id` is already present locally gets `present:true`, `sources:[…]`, and `updateAvailable` set when the remote version is higher than the installed version; a remote-only loadout gets `present:false`, `installed:false`. The `CatalogEntry` type carries `remoteVersion?: string` from `ociCore.ts`.

**Pull-if-needed install + collision-confirm.** `loadoutInstall.ensureAndInstall(workspaceId, id, opts?)` is the install entry point. When `opts.source` is supplied: if the loadout is absent locally (`present:false`), `download(source, id, version)` is called first to pull it into the library before `installLoadout` runs (pull-if-needed). If the loadout already exists locally and `opts.force` is not set, the handler returns `{ status: 'needs-confirm', reason: 'collision' }` — the renderer's `onInstall` prompts the user ("already exists locally — overwrite?") and, on confirm, re-calls with `force: true`. `force: true` overwrites the local copy. Returns `{ status: 'installed' }` on success.

**New IPC channels (Phase 2, all privileged main process):**
- `loadouts:listSources()` → `{ base, hasCredential, username? }[]`: the current sources from `sources.json`, with whether each has a vault token. It never returns the token. Main-process callers that want bare bases use `loadoutSources.listSources()` → `string[]`.
- `loadouts:addSource(base, cred?: { token, username? })` → `RemoteLoadout[]`: stores `cred` in the vault (when given), then validates and adds the source and returns its index. It rejects with:
  - `needs-credential: <base>` when the index answers 401/403 without a credential;
  - `credential-rejected: <base> (HTTP <n>)` when the credential is refused;
  - `malformed-credential: <base>` when the token/username shape is invalid (rejected before anything is stored).
- `loadouts:setSourceCredential(base, cred | null)` → `RemoteLoadout[]`: sets or replaces (with `null`, removes) a configured source's token, then force-refreshes its index. Same error contract. An unknown source rejects.
- `loadouts:removeSource(base)` → `void`: removes the source from `sources[]`, clears the cache, and purges the source's vault scope and `credentials` entry.
- `loadouts:refreshSource(base)` → `RemoteLoadout[]` — force-refreshes one source's index.
- `loadouts:install` (extended) — now accepts `opts?: { source?, version?, force?, automations?: string[] (folders to turn on), allowHost?: boolean }` to trigger pull-if-needed and collision-confirm, and (#407 Phase 8) to pick which shipped automations start ON; without opts, plain local install with the review's defaults.
- `loadouts:automations(workspaceId, loadoutId, allowHost?)` → `LoadoutAutomationPreview[]` `{folder, name, status new|update|unchanged|kept-edited|invalid, defaultOn, blocked, hostStep, missingSecrets, flags, errors}` for the review's **Automations · turn on with this install** list (ticks; host-step flags in red; a disabled tick with the reason when blocked) (#407 Phase 8).

**Browser modal additions (Phase 2).** The `.lb-facets` sidebar gains a `.lb-sources` section below the tag cloud. It renders one `.lb-source-row` per configured source (checkbox + `.lb-source-name` + `.lb-source-remove` ×-button); checking a source filters the results list to entries that come from that source (or are already present locally). A `.lb-add-source` row has a text input (placeholder `ghcr.io/owner/repo`) + `+ Add` button that calls `loadouts:addSource`; errors surface in `.lb-source-error`. The ×-button on each row calls `loadouts:removeSource`; it stops propagation and prevents default so it doesn't toggle the surrounding label's checkbox.

**Private sources in the modal (#444).**
- **Token prompt:** when `+ Add` fails with `needs-credential` — or `credential-rejected` on that same no-credential add (an automatic env token was already tried and refused; #444 final review M3) — a `.lb-cred` form appears with:
  - the text "This source is private. Add a token:";
  - a password field (`Source token`) and an optional `Source username (optional)` field;
  - `Save token` / `Cancel` buttons.
- **Save:** re-calls `loadouts:addSource(base, cred)`. The only client-side check is that the token field isn't empty (blocked before any call). A refused token shows `credential rejected (HTTP 401)` (or 403) in the form; a malformed one is rejected by main — `malformed-credential: <base>` — and shown as "That token contains spaces or invalid characters. Paste it again." (`sourceErrors.ts`'s `SOURCE_MALFORMED_MESSAGE`), also a round trip.
- **Rows with a token:** each `.lb-source-row` sits in a `.lb-source-item`. A source with a token shows a `.lb-source-lock` 🔒 badge and two actions:
  - `Change token…` opens the same form, which calls `loadouts:setSourceCredential`;
  - `Remove token` calls `setSourceCredential(base, null)`.

**Rail `Update ↑` affordance (Phase 2).** When a catalog entry has `installed:true` and `updateAvailable:true`, `LibraryPane.tsx` renders a `.btn.update` button beside the ✓ Installed badge. Clicking it calls `loadouts:install(workspaceId, id, { source, version: remoteVersion, force: true })` to pull the latest version and reinstall. `updateAvailable` compares the newest `remoteVersion` across sources with the workspace's `installedLoadouts[].version` (or, when no workspace is targeted or the record has none, the local library's `loadout.md` version); `buildLoadoutCatalog` passes both. The rail's **+ Install** on a remote-only entry (`present:false`) passes `{ source: sources[0], version: remoteVersion }` so it pulls first; `ensureAndInstall` with neither a local copy nor a source throws "isn't in the local library yet". The button is disabled when the workspace is not in a runnable state.

**Host-private invariant (§9).** All downloaded artifacts land exclusively in `<userData>/loadouts/<id>/` via `loadoutDir(id)` — the host-private library, never bind-mounted into any container. A workspace only ever sees the installed snapshot in its own `.claude/`. The `safeLayerPath` + `MAX_BLOB_BYTES` cap (5 MiB/layer) enforces this at the pull layer: traversal attempts abort the pull before writing anything; oversized blobs are rejected.

### Remote host (`fleet-host`)

`src/host/` (bundle entry `cli.ts`, wiring `main.ts`) serves one hosted workspace from a `runner-host` pod (§4 *Host images*). With a `db:` block it keeps its lease, workspace id and its own sessions' history in Postgres (*Host store*, *Ingest*), and the lease holder runs the workspace's automations (*Host automations*) and serves the fleet-state MCP to its sessions (*Host MCP*). Approvals, notifications and the desktop's automation UI for a remote workspace are §11 *Remote host: what remains*.

**Topology.** Twingate → internal ALB → Istio ingress gateway → sidecar → `:8080`. The ALB and Envoy append `X-Forwarded-For`, but its client entry is always a Twingate connector IP, so nothing keys on XFF and `trustedProxyHops` stays 0. Plain `http://` to the pod is allowed; there is no in-pod TLS.

**Env contract.**

| Var | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `FLEET_HOST_PORT` | `8080` | listen port (`0.0.0.0`) |
| `FLEET_HOST_CONFIG` | `/etc/fleet-host/config.yaml` | read-only ConfigMap mount |
| `FLEET_HOST_STATE` | `/home/fleet/.claude/fleet-host` | shared volume; holds `workspace-id.json` |
| `CLAUDE_FLEET_BROKER_SOCKET` | `/run/broker/broker.sock` | the in-pod broker |
| `FLEET_HOST_PROJECTS` | `~/.claude/projects` | transcripts the holder ingests |
| `FLEET_HOST_CRED_SOCKET` | `/run/fleet-host/cred.sock` | App credential socket |
| `FLEET_HOST_MCP_SOCKET` | `/run/fleet/mcp.sock`; `/run/fleet/mcp/mcp.sock` when the group below is set | the fleet-state MCP socket the holder serves (absolute, ≤ 107 bytes) |
| `FLEET_HOST_MCP_SOCKET_GROUP` | unset | unset: the socket is 0600 in a 0700 directory fleet-host creates. A numeric gid (container split): the directory must already exist (`prep`), and the socket is 0660 in that group (*Host MCP*) |
| `FLEET_HOST_EMBED_DIR` | `/usr/local/lib/fleet-host/embed` | the embedder sidecar and its `model/` (*Embedder*) |

**Config** (`config.ts`; unknown keys are refused at the top level and in `workspace` and `identity`):
- `workspace: { id?, name, description?, color?: { hue } }`. `name` is 1–64 trimmed characters with no control characters; `id` is a ULID; `hue` is in [0, 360).
- `identity: { provider: github, org? }`.
- `grants` and `machines` (GitHub team / App installation → tier), validated by `parseGrantConfig`. A person's tier is the highest matching grant; no match is 403.
- `db` (optional): §6 *Settings* `store`'s Postgres block without `kind`, parsed by `parseStoreConfig`. A `static` password or a password in `connectionString` is refused. `password: { kind: env, name }` and `env` (naming a connection-string variable) are accepted but are no safer: sessions inherit the container env and can read `/proc/<fleet-host>/environ` (§9), so use them only for tests and local setups. `password: { kind: aws-rds-iam, region }` is the only production form. On AWS: the cluster writer endpoint as `host` (IAM tokens are signed for it), `database: fleet`, `user: fleet_agent`; `ssl` defaults to `verify-full` with the image's RDS bundle. Absent: a process-local lease and no ingest (one replica only), with a startup warning.
- `github` (optional): `{ appId, installationId, keyFile, repositories?, permissions? }`. `appId`, `installationId` (positive integers) and `keyFile` (an absolute path to the App's PEM, mounted from a Secret, 0400, uid 1000) are set together or not at all; without them the App credential helper is off. `repositories` (1–500 names in the installation's account) is an allowlist; `permissions` (`{ <name>: read | write }`) is the default and ceiling for every token, else the built-in set (*GitHub App credentials*).
- `automations` (optional, ≤ 100): a list of `{ definition, enabled? }` (`enabled` defaults to true; names unique). The parser checks only this shape, since validating a definition needs the workspace id; the holder validates each one (*Host automations*). A definition that fails validation (a bad cron, a live step) is logged and skipped and the reload is still accepted. These are the only source of the host's automations.
- `search` (optional): `{ embedder: boolean }`, strict keys, default `{ embedder: true }`. Fixed for the process: a reload that changes it is refused with `search cannot change on reload; restart to apply`. With `embedder: false` the holder starts no sidecar, indexer or backfill, the host MCP has no `search_transcripts`, and `history:search` answers 503 `search unavailable` (*Embedder*).
- `trustedProxyHops` (integer 0–10, default 0). Above 0 the per-address limiter keys on the XFF entry that many hops from the right; here that is the Twingate connector, so a value above 0 logs a warning ("per-address limiting keys on the Twingate connector here; leave 0").

**Reload.**
- Mount the ConfigMap as a directory, not `subPath` (a `subPath` mount never updates). Kubelet propagation takes ~60–90 s; SIGHUP forces a reload.
- Triggers: a change to the file or the `..data` symlink in its directory (debounced 250 ms), and SIGHUP. The SIGHUP handler is installed first thing at start and stays through shutdown, so a SIGHUP never kills the process.
- Reloads are serialized. The first accepted config (`reload('start')`) is the baseline, and it is the same object passed to `resolveWorkspaceId`. A file that fails to parse, or whose `workspace.id` (including set ↔ unset), `db` or `github.appId`/`installationId`/`keyFile` differs from the running one, is refused before anything applies and the current config is kept. A deep-equal config is a no-op that keeps the verify cache.
- A refused reload leaves the file on disk unloadable, so the next fleet-host restart (a crash, a lease loss, a pod restart) fails at startup until the file is fixed.
- `github.repositories` and `github.permissions` apply on reload; the pools and the App key are built once at start. `automations` apply on reload: an accepted reload reconciles them on the holder (*Host automations*).
- An applied reload swaps one immutable `AuthContext` (provider, verify cache and negative cache built together by `buildAuthContext`), then re-verifies every open socket. Each request reads the context once, at its start.

**Workspace id.**
- With `db:`, the one-row `host_meta` is authoritative (`resolveHostWorkspaceId`). An empty table is seeded from config `workspace.id`, else `$FLEET_HOST_STATE/workspace-id.json`, else a new ULID, with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` then a re-read, so concurrent pods agree. Seeding refuses start when config and file name different ids, or when the file exists but can't be read; it never mints over an existing file.
- Once seeded, a configured `workspace.id` that differs from the row refuses start, naming both. A state file that is missing, unreadable or different is rewritten from the row (temp file, fsync, `rename`), with a warning when it held another id.
- Without `db:`, a configured `workspace.id` is authoritative; otherwise a ULID is minted once into the state file (written, fsynced, then `link()`ed so concurrent pods agree), and a state file holding a different id than the config refuses start.
- `fleet-host init` resolves the id the same way (opening the host pool when `db:` is set) and prints `{"workspaceId": …}`.

**Startup.** With `db:`, the process listens before the DB is open. Until the host pool is open, the workspace id resolved and one lease tick done, `/healthz` is 503 `{status:"starting", lease:"standby", db:"connecting"}`, and every authenticated route and upgrade answers 503 `{"error":"host starting"}` with `Retry-After: 5`, without verifying the token. An invalid `db:` block fails before listening. Without `db:`, start completes before listening.

**Lease and standby.** The lease is a `LeaseStore` seam (`lease.ts`). The holder renews every 10 s with a 30 s TTL; a store call is raced against the remaining validity. Until it holds the lease the process is **standby**: every authenticated route (`/whoami`, `/rpc`, `/attach`, `/events`) answers 503 `{"error":"standby"}`, and it runs no ingest and creates no broker sessions.
- With `db:` the lease is `host_lease(workspace_id PK, holder, epoch, expires_at)` (`pgLease.ts`). Expiry is judged on the DB's `clock_timestamp()` (not `now()`, the transaction start, which is stale after a row-lock wait); the caller's clock is ignored. `tryAcquire` takes a free, expired or own row and bumps `epoch`; `renew` and `release` match `holder` and `epoch`. Each runs in a transaction with `lock_timeout` = (TTL − renew) / 2.
- `tryAcquire` and `release` first take `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the row, which waits for in-flight fenced writes (*Host store*). `renew` is a non-key `UPDATE` and doesn't wait for them, so fenced writes can't starve it.
- Without `db:` it is `memoryLeaseStore()`, correct only for one replica.

**Health.** `/healthz` is 200 `{status:"ok", lease, db}` while the lease loop runs and, with `db:`, the last DB ping (every 5 s, 3 s timeout) succeeded within 15 s, holder or standby; otherwise 503 `{status:"unhealthy", …}`. `db` is `ok`, `down`, `connecting` or `none`. A DB outage makes the pod unready: use `/healthz` for readiness, not liveness. `/leader` is 200 only on the holder.

**Exit codes.** Losing the lease shuts down with exit 1 (`superseded`: renew refused, or a write fenced off). Exit 75 (`unreachable`: the lease couldn't be confirmed in time, or the holder's stores or startup after the host pool opened failed with a connection-level error) means the DB is down; `fleet-host-entry` doesn't count it toward its give-up (§4 *Host images*). A holder whose DB is unreachable for TTL − renew (20 s) loses the lease and exits 75; PTYs survive in the broker.

**Host store** (`db:`). Two pools on one schema (`fleet`; the schema and pgvector must exist, the store never creates them; `fleet-host bootstrap` does, *Bootstrap and migrate*):
- **Host pool** (`hostDb.ts`, max 2, holder and standby alike): the `host` migration track (`host_meta`, `host_lease`, `host_ingest_offsets`, beside the `state` and `inbox` tracks in `schema_migrations`), the lease and the health ping, on an unfenced Kysely handle. Opening retries with backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s), logging each failure with the IAM hint; SIGTERM aborts it.
- **Store pool** (`holder.ts`, holder only): opened after the lease is acquired. The `state` and `inbox` migrations run on an unfenced handle under `migratePostgres`'s advisory lock, with a holder check before and after; then `openPgStores(cfg, { state: true, afterBeginWrite: fence, migrate: false })` opens the fenced stores. Only data writes are fenced; a fenced migration would stall every takeover for its whole run.
- **The fence** (`leaseFence`): the first statement of every read-write transaction and around every bare write (data-modifying CTEs included) is `SELECT holder, epoch, expires_at > clock_timestamp() AS live FROM host_lease WHERE workspace_id = $1 FOR KEY SHARE`. Another holder, another epoch, an expired or missing row, or no local epoch throws `LeaseLostError` (the transaction rolls back) and, once per fence, calls `LeaseManager.markLost()`. `FOR KEY SHARE` conflicts with a takeover's `FOR UPDATE`, so a takeover waits for in-flight fenced writes and every write after it sees the new epoch; it doesn't conflict with renew.
- **Session settings** on both pools: `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 10 s and `statement_timeout` 30 s (migrations set `statement_timeout = 0` locally), TCP keepalive after 10 s. A standby takes over from a partitioned holder within about TTL + 10 s. A server-ended connection is reported on the pool's `'error'` listener, not thrown uncaught.
- All host SQL goes through `postgresKysely` handles: a bare `pool.query()` skips the per-connection `SET search_path`. `fleet-host bootstrap`'s admin `pg.Client` is the exception: it touches only catalogs and DDL.

**Bootstrap and migrate** (`dbCli.ts`; one-shot commands for pre-rollout Jobs, both reading the same `db:` from `$FLEET_HOST_CONFIG`; no `github:` needed). JSON log lines on stdout; exit 0 ok, 1 failed, 2 usage. Any other argument to `fleet-host` (besides `init`) is a usage error, so a typo never starts a host inside a Job.
- `fleet-host bootstrap` runs as an admin (the RDS master) and never runs migrations, logs in as the app role, or stores the admin credential. Exactly one admin source: `--admin-secret-arn ARN` (Secrets Manager `SecretString` JSON via the AWS SDK default chain; fields `--admin-user-field`/`--admin-password-field`, default `username`/`password`; region `--region`, else the ARN's), `--admin-password-file PATH`, `--admin-password-env NAME`, or `--admin-iam` (an RDS IAM token for the admin; region `--region`, else `db.password.region`, else `AWS_REGION`; refused when `db:` has ssl `disable`). Secret ARNs may contain `!` (RDS-managed `rds!cluster-…` secrets). `--admin-user` is required for the last three and overrides the secret's user field. `--maintenance-db` (default `postgres`) is where the database is created. Host, port, database, app role and TLS come from `db:` under its usual rules (verify-full with the image's RDS bundle by default; the connection-string forms are parsed for them).
- An admin user equal to `db.user` is refused, before connecting and again by `current_user` after (on PG ≤15 the steps would otherwise strip the master's CREATEDB/CREATEROLE). The maintenance-DB session holds `pg_advisory_lock` until the target steps finish, so concurrent bootstraps serialize.
- Steps (`bootstrap.ts`), each a catalog check that applies only when it fails; every check must pass again afterwards: (1) on the maintenance DB, `CREATE DATABASE`; then on the target DB (2) the app role `LOGIN NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE` (created, or altered back; an app role that is a superuser, has BYPASSRLS or REPLICATION, or is a member of `rds_superuser` when that exists is refused); (3) `GRANT rds_iam` when `db.password.kind` is `aws-rds-iam` (refused if `rds_iam` doesn't exist); (4) `ALTER ROLE … SET search_path = <schema>, public`; (5) `vector` ≥ 0.8.0 in `public` (created, or `ALTER EXTENSION vector UPDATE`); (6) the schema, created with or altered to the app role as owner; (7) `CONNECT` on the database and (8) `USAGE` on `public` when the role lacks them; (9) `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC` when the role can create in `public`. Then, on PG ≥ 16 with a schema of at most 32 characters and a valid `db.queryUser` (else a logged warning and the steps are skipped), the `query` roles (*Host MCP*): `<schema>_mcp_reader` and `<schema>_mcp_raw` (NOLOGIN, NOCREATEDB, NOCREATEROLE); the login `<q>` = `db.queryUser` (`LOGIN NOINHERIT CONNECTION LIMIT 4`, created without a password) and its NOLOGIN `<q>_raw`; `GRANT rds_iam TO <q>` when `rds_iam` exists; the edges reader → `<q>` (INHERIT TRUE), reader and raw → `<q>_raw`, `<q>_raw` → `<q>` (INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE); a revoke of every other direct membership of those four roles (so `<q>` is never a member of the app role, directly or through reader); and `CONNECT` for `<q>`. `<q>` must differ from the app role and the admin; an elevated role (superuser, BYPASSRLS, REPLICATION) is refused. The app role gets no membership in or ADMIN on these roles. Before altering or granting a role (PG16: the ADMIN option; before 16, CREATEROLE suffices) or re-owning a schema (the admin must act as its owner), the admin's rights are checked and a missing one is a `BootstrapError` naming the role or the current owner, not a raw 42501. Each change is logged as `bootstrap: <change>`; the last line is `bootstrap complete` with `changed: [...]`, or `bootstrap complete: already in place, nothing changed`.
- The app role gets a password only when bootstrap creates it and `db.password` isn't `aws-rds-iam`: the `db.password` env var, the connection string's password, or `--app-password-file`. It is sent as a SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier computed client-side (printable ASCII only), since DDL can reach server logs. An existing role's password is never changed.
- Under PG16 a CREATEROLE admin (the RDS master) holds only ADMIN on roles it creates, not the SET that `CREATE SCHEMA … AUTHORIZATION` / `ALTER SCHEMA … OWNER` need; that step grants it `WITH INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE` and revokes it in the same transaction.
- Secrets: errors name sources and fields, never values (a non-JSON secret is reported without `JSON.parse`'s quoting; `parseArgs` errors are reduced to their code). Every output line is scrubbed of the admin password, the raw secret, minted IAM tokens and the app password, raw and JSON-escaped, on top of `redactSecrets`.
- `fleet-host migrate` runs as the app role (no admin access) exactly what a host runs at start: `openHostDb` (the `host` track) then `migratePgStores` (`inbox`, the pgvector check, `state`), unfenced under `migratePostgres`'s advisory lock, then prints `migrate complete` with each track's version. A failure exits 1, which blocks the rollout; host-start migration stays as the fallback. It runs before the new Rollout, while the previous release still holds the lease and serves, so every migration must stay compatible with the previous release (expand/contract): no drops or renames of anything it uses, and no long table-rewriting steps (they hold locks the live holder waits on).
- **Shutdown** with `db:`: halt → stop the holder (MCP socket, automations, ingest, then its stores) → release the lease → close the server → close the credential socket → end the host pool. Bounds: the stores and the host pool 5 s each (`STORE_CLOSE_MS`; `store close timed out` is logged), the lease 4 s (2 s for an in-flight tick, 2 s for the release), the server 1 s and the credential socket 2 s (`CRED_CLOSE_GRACE_MS`; connections still open are cut). Worst case about 17 s, inside the 30 s grace.

**Ingest** (`hostIngest.ts`, holder only, after the stores open). Every `*.jsonl` under `$FLEET_HOST_PROJECTS/*/` and `*/<session>/subagents/` is ingested with `ingestBatch` under the hosted workspace id; subagent files go under the parent session. Changes come from a recursive `fs.watch` (writers are local, so inotify fires on EFS too) plus a full rescan every 60 s; a per-file serial queue keeps each file's batches in order.
- **Offsets** persist in `host_ingest_offsets(path, byte_offset, ino, updated_at)`, upserted by `ingestBatch`'s `inTx` hook as the last statement of the chunk's fenced transaction, so rows and offset commit or roll back together and a stale holder can't advance either. A restart resumes from them instead of re-reading shared storage.
- **Chunks** are at most `INGEST_TX_LINES` (500) lines and 1 MiB per transaction, with `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '5s'` (`atStart` hook); a chunk that hits it rolls back and is retried on the next change or rescan. Reads are 8 MiB per step.
- A line over 64 MiB is skipped and logged (`transcript line over the size cap; skipped`); the offset moves past its newline.
- A file that shrank, or whose inode changed (a rewrite by rename), is re-read from 0; dedup keys keep rows unique.
- Every line is redacted before it is stored (*Logging*). On Postgres a NUL becomes U+FFFD.
- No transcript mirror, no tab mapping and no summaries on the host. Turns are embedded by the holder's indexer (`hostIndexer.ts`, *Embedder*): a backfill at holder start, then the session of each ingested chunk (`onIngested`), serially, through the fenced state store, only while the embedder is ready.
- After each chunk commits (never inside its transaction), the holder bumps a 2 s trailing hint (`trailingHint`, `SUMMARY_HINT_MS`) that publishes `observability:summary` `{ workspaceId }` (no data) to `/events`.

**Holder start and stop** (`holder.ts`). Start: the store pool and fenced stores → the embedder and indexer (when `search.embedder`; *Embedder*) → ingest → the index backfill (not awaited; a failure logs `index backfill failed`) → the automation runtime (`recover()`, then reconcile `automations`) → the MCP socket. Stop: MCP socket → automations → indexer (drops the queue, waits at most 2 s, `INDEXER_STOP_GRACE_MS`, for the pass in flight, then takes no more work) → embedder (stdin closed; the sidecar exits on EOF) → ingest → offsets handle → inbox store → state store → pool. The MCP comes last so no session call lands on a half-built runtime, and closes first so none lands on a stopped one. A failure in any step before the MCP stops what started and fails the holder (the lease is given up). The MCP start is the exception: a failure (a non-socket at the path, a bad directory) is logged (`mcp start failed; retrying`) and retried with backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s), never ending the holder; each attempt builds a fresh `pgReadSource`, because a failed `startHostMcp` closes the one it was given.

**Host MCP** (`hostMcp.ts`, `socketDir.ts`; holder only). The fleet-state tools (§11 *In-container SQLite access via MCP*) come from `mcpCore.ts`, the Electron-free core the desktop's `mcpServer.ts` also re-exports (*fleet-state MCP core* below). A standby has no socket; a session's bridge reconnects with backoff until the holder serves it.
- **Socket.** Single container (`FLEET_HOST_MCP_SOCKET_GROUP` unset): fleet-host creates the directory 0700 (tightening an existing one), refuses any non-socket at the path (a file, a symlink), unlinks a stale socket and creates it 0600; sessions share fleet-host's uid, so they connect. Container split (a gid): the socket lives in `prep`'s `mcp/` directory, which must be a real directory owned by fleet-host's uid, in that group, with group r-x and no group write and no access for others (`mode & 077 = 050`; prep makes it 2750), else start fails naming `fleet-host-entry prep`. Sessions in the group can connect but can't create anything there, so they can't plant a file, symlink or directory at the socket path; fleet-host removes whatever is there and creates the socket 0660 in the group. After `listen`, the path is `lstat`ed before and after the chmod/chown and must be a socket owned by fleet-host's uid, so a link is never followed.
- **Caller and scope.** Every connection is the hosted workspace (the `host_meta` id), fixed by fleet-host and never read from the wire; the read scope is exactly that workspace (the host config grants people tiers, not workspaces read access). Published partitions and cross-host committee grants are later.
- **Tools** (`HOST_MCP_TOOLS`, 14): `list_sessions`, `get_session`, `get_cost`, `list_events`, `session_summary`, `query` (on the holder's state store); `post_todo`, `update_todo`, `list_todos`; `list_automations`, `get_automation`, `list_runs`, `run_automation`, `schedule_next`. With `search.embedder` (the default) `hostMcpTools(true)` adds `search_transcripts` (15): it embeds the query in the holder's sidecar (`setQueryEmbedder`; cleared when the MCP closes) and scans through the read source's state handle (`pgSearchEmbeddings`, scope `{hosted}`), not the `query` login; an unavailable sidecar answers `embedder sidecar unavailable: <reason>`. Everything else answers `Unknown tool` in `tools/call` and is absent from `tools/list` (so does `search_transcripts` with `search.embedder: false`): `list_errors` (the host logs to stdout), `notify` and the proposal tools ((g)), `mark_useful` ((e)), the committee, perf, `plan_usage`, skills, claims, capture-settings, `expose_port`, `get_config` and hook tools. `initialize` sends no `instructions` (the claims rule needs both claims tools; the port line needs `expose_port`).
  - **`query`** runs under part 4a's isolation (`openPgMcpReadSource(…, { preprovisioned })` on the holder's fenced state handle; §9 *MCP `query` on Postgres*): caller = the hosted workspace, scope = `{hosted}`. It signs in as the login `fleet-host bootstrap` pre-provisions, `db.queryUser` (default `<db.user>_query`; host-only key matching `[a-z_][a-z0-9_]{0,58}`, which the desktop's `config.json` refuses), with an RDS IAM token minted for that login; the app role stays `NOCREATEROLE` and creates no role. At open, and on each call until it succeeds, it checks that the reader, raw, query and query-raw roles exist, then runs `ensureIsolation` as the app role (its `CREATE ROLE … IF NOT EXISTS` is then a no-op). Every call rewrites `mcp_grants` and first checks confinement: a recursive walk of `pg_auth_members` from the login must reach only its own four roles and `rds_iam`, none elevated. A missing role, a failed confinement check, or a refused login (no role, NOLOGIN, no `rds-db:connect` yet) answers `query is unavailable on this Postgres store: <reason>`, naming `fleet-host bootstrap` where re-running it fixes it; the other tools work. A non-IAM host (`db.password` kind `env`, or a connection string) has no password source for the login, so `query` is always unavailable there. All calls share the one login (`CONNECTION LIMIT 4`, a pool of 1), so they run one at a time; past 8 queued a call answers busy. Until the container split, sessions can reach the app role itself (shared uid and pod identity), so this is defense in depth; it becomes a boundary with the split and matters once (e) adds publish partitions.
- **Caps.** The core's: request lines ≤ 1 MiB, ≤ 16 requests in flight per connection, tool errors clipped to 4 KiB, the 10 s stall breadcrumb. The host adds at most 64 open connections; past that a new connection is closed at once, so one session can hold all 64 and deny MCP to the workspace's other sessions. Refusals are logged at `warn`, the first at once and then at most one line a minute carrying a `refused` count.
- **Logging.** Core log rows go through the host logger (redacted, *Logging*) at their own level; `mcp-conn` and `mcp-conn-closed` are `debug`, so a session's reconnect loop can't flood stdout. Tool arguments are never logged beyond the core's 300-character excerpt.
- **One instance per process.** The core's handler state is per process, so `startHostMcp` refuses a second start (`already serving`) until the first closes or fails. It owns the read source it is given: `close()` and a failed start both close it.
- **Sessions get the tools through `--mcp-config`.** `hostMcpArgs(socket)` is one argument, `--mcp-config=<json>`, naming server `claude-fleet-state` (the prefix `INBOX_TOOLS` uses), `command: node`, `args: [/usr/local/lib/fleet-host/mcp-bridge.cjs]`, `env: { CLAUDE_FLEET_MCP_UNIX: <socket>, CLAUDE_FLEET_MCP_TCP: "" }`. `--mcp-config` is variadic in claude's CLI: in the space form a later positional is read as another config file, the `=` form takes exactly one value (verified on the pinned 2.1.281). With `db:`, `AttachHub` appends it to every viewer `CREATE` after the frame gate (a viewer still can't pass its own `--mcp-config`), and the automation runtime to every fresh step. `~/.claude.json` on the shared volume isn't touched. Whether this inline `claude-fleet-state` or one of the same name in `~/.claude.json` wins is not verified (`claude mcp list` ignores `--mcp-config`); it is on the host checklist.

**Embedder** (`embedder.ts`, `hostIndexer.ts`, `embedCheck.ts`; holder only). Text is embedded on the host by the same `fleet-embedder` sidecar as the desktop (§7 *Local embeddings*), so host and desktop vectors share `EMBED_MODEL_ID` and merge by score (§6 *Sessions*, `history:search`).
- **Process.** `startHostEmbedder` wraps `EmbedderClient` directly (same protocol, timeouts, backoff and crash-loop rule as the desktop) with its default direct spawner (no relay worker: fleet-host has no UI thread to protect), spawning `$FLEET_HOST_EMBED_DIR/fleet-embedder --model-dir $FLEET_HOST_EMBED_DIR/model` lazily on the first `embed()`, which the backfill makes happen at holder start. The sidecar gets an empty environment: fleet-host's carries store settings and AWS Pod Identity variables the sidecar doesn't need. It doesn't use `embeddings.ts`'s selector, which imports Electron, downloads missing files from the Hub and falls back to transformers.js; `importGraph.test.ts` keeps `embeddings.ts` and `embedderModelFiles.ts` out of the host bundle. Inference never runs on fleet-host's event loop.
- **States.** `ready`; `missing` (the binary or a weight file is absent at start: logged once, never spawned); `failed` (a crash loop, 3 failures in 5 min); `closed` (holder stop). There is no fallback and no download. When not `ready`: `search_transcripts` answers `embedder sidecar unavailable: <reason>`, the indexer takes no work, and `history:search` keeps serving the vectors already stored (they stay valid for the pinned model). The next holder (a lease takeover or a pod restart) tries again.
- **Indexer** (`startHostIndexer`). Turns only (the host has no summarizer). Backfill: `deleteEmbeddingsForOtherModels(EMBED_MODEL_ID)` (chunked, yielding), `sessionsWithPendingTurns(EMBED_MODEL_ID)` (the sessions whose newest turn has no embedding for the model), then one queued pass per session. After that, `onIngested(sessionId)` queues that session; the queue coalesces per session and one pass (`indexSessionTurnsWith`, `transcriptIndexCore.ts`) runs at a time. A failed pass is logged (`transcript index failed`) and the queue continues. Writes go through the fenced state store, so a superseded holder's insert fails and ends the process like any other write. HNSW stays deferred (§7 *JSONL→SQLite cache*, *Search*; §11 *Remote host: what remains*).
- **Off-switch.** `search: { embedder: false }` (*Config*) starts no sidecar, indexer or backfill, drops `search_transcripts`, and makes `history:search` answer 503 `search unavailable` before it reads its arguments; the desktop lists such a host as unavailable, reason `disabled` ("search off").
- **`fleet-host embed-check [reference]`** runs the build-time check (§4 *Host images*) against `$FLEET_HOST_EMBED_DIR/reference.json` by default; exit 0 with `"ok":true`, else 1. It can be re-run in a pod.
- **Memory.** The sidecar peaked at 102 MiB RSS (VmHWM) embedding the 40-text corpus at ≤ 1000 characters, batch 8 (x64, 2026-11-09); `embed-check` prints `peakRssKiB` on every build. The reference deployment's memory request goes up by 256 Mi, on the host pod.
- **Placement.** The sidecar is a child of fleet-host and runs only where the holder runs. Under the host/workspace split (§11) that is the host pod; the workspace pod has the same files but never starts fleet-host, and `search_transcripts` reaches the host pod over the MCP's :8082 with the same handler and scope. In the single-container image the sidecar shares the session container (§9).
- **Why a sidecar in the image.** Rejected: the desktop indexing the host's sessions (two writers for one workspace); a separate embedding Deployment (another workload and Service for one model, plus a network hop per batch); transformers.js in the image (about 150–250 MB on disk and the in-process stall the desktop removed). `history:search` takes a vector, not text, so a `read` viewer can't make the host spend model CPU.

**Host automations** (`hostAutomations.ts`, `hostDefinition.ts`; holder only). The desktop's automation, proposal and inbox services (§6 *Automations*, §8 *Automations*) on the holder's fenced inbox store, so every write they make checks the lease; a write after a takeover throws `LeaseLostError`, which ends the process.
- **Definitions come only from `automations:`.** On start and on every accepted reload, `reconcile` makes the DB match the config: `$self` (in `prompt.workspace`, `event.workspace`, `manual.allowFrom`, `script.runIn`) becomes the hosted workspace id, each definition is validated (below) and matched by its validated name. A new one is created in one write with its `enabled` state and origin `{ source: 'host-config' }`; a changed one gets a new version only when its normalized YAML differs; `enabled` follows the config; a host-config row no longer listed is removed. A same-name row with no origin is adopted as host-config (never deleted); a same-name row with another origin is skipped with an error log; every other enabled row not from the config is disabled with a warning. Reconciles are serialized like `tick`. Each entry runs on its own: a `LeaseLostError` ends the reconcile, any other failure is logged (`host-config automation failed`) and the rest continue.
- **Validation and refusals** (`ResolveCtx.check`, `AutomationDeps.checkDefinition` = `remoteHostRefusals`, run only on an otherwise valid definition): only the hosted workspace resolves, by id or name, so other workspaces in steps, `event.workspace` or `allowFrom` fail as `unknown workspace`; script steps (`steps[i].script: script steps are not available on a remote host yet`; there is no way yet to run one in the workspace container once the split lands, so `runIn: host` goes with them), `prompt.mode: live` (a multi-person host has no single workspace session to type into, and no busy detector) and `session.idle` / `workspace.started` events (no detector) are refused. Error paths match the validator's (`triggers[i].event.on: …`, `steps[i].prompt.mode: …`).
- **Run-time gates.** `start()` re-runs `checkDefinition` on the stored definition and `AutomationDeps.mayRun(record)`, which on the host refuses any row whose origin isn't `host-config`. A refused manual or MCP start throws `<name> can't run here: …`; a refused due schedule or event start records no run and is logged. The runtime's `post` (live steps) and `execScript` also throw. The desktop passes neither hook.
- **Fresh steps.** `BrokerOps.create('auto-<runId>-<stepId>', 120, 40, ['-p', …options, '--mcp-config=…', '--session-id', <uuid>, '--', <prompt>])`: the prompt follows `--`, so a prompt starting with `-` stays text (verified on 2.1.281: without `--` a dash-led word is `unknown option`), and a prompt over 120 KiB fails the step before anything starts. Right before the `CREATE`, an empty write transaction runs the lease fence. A takeover after that check and before the `CREATE` still starts at most one session: that run's next write fails, the next holder's `recover()` marks the run `interrupted`, and the session runs until claude exits. `freshState` reads `BrokerOps.list()`; `closeFresh` attaches and closes, best-effort (a viewer may hold the session).
- **Workspace deps:** `workspaceState` is `running` for the hosted workspace and `null` otherwise; `ensureRunning` is a no-op; `busy` is false; the inbox `manifest` resolves only the hosted workspace, so another caller's post is refused. No secrets are read; no notifications are sent (run failures and notices are logged; delivery through desktop policy is (g)).
- **MCP.** The hosted workspace's sessions are privileged for the automation tools (every automation on the host is its own). `propose_automation`, `propose_automation_change` and `get_proposal` are refused with `on a remote host, automations come from the host config` (and not served). The proposal service still exists with `trusted: false`, for (g).
- **Ticks.** `recover()` at start, a 30 s tick, and `stop()` that clears the tick, cancels pending event debounces, unhooks the MCP handlers and waits at most 2 s for in-flight runs; a run still in flight then fails its next write and the next holder marks it `interrupted`.
- **Events:** `automations:changed` and `proposals:changed` (`null`) and `inbox:changed` (the counts) go to `/events`. `startHolder` returns `automations: { automations, proposals, inbox }` (the service objects the desktop's IPC handlers call), so (g) registers `automations:*`, `proposals:*` and `inbox:*` as RPC channels; none are registered yet.

**fleet-state MCP core** (`mcpCore.ts`). Protocol (`initialize`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`), dispatch, caps, every tool but the three perf tools, the handler seams (`set*Handlers`, `setReadScopeResolver`, …), the read-source state and `serveMcpConnection(sock, callerId)`. It imports only pure modules (`mcpLines`, `store/readSource`, `pricing`, `clipText`, `vectors`, `claims/searchHits`), so the host bundle loads it with no Electron or OpenTelemetry. `setMcpLogSink(fn)` receives the rows (the desktop passes `errorLog.logError`); `setExposedTools(names | null)` limits `tools/list` and `tools/call` (the desktop never calls it); `initialize`'s `instructions` include the claims rule only when `record_claim` and `search_claims` are served and the port line only when `expose_port` is. The desktop's `mcpServer.ts` keeps the per-workspace Unix listeners, the Windows TCP listener and tokens, the health status, the SQLite default and the perf tools (spliced after `query`; a missing `query` throws at load), and ends with `export * from './mcpCore.js'`. `src/main/mcpServer.contract.test.ts` pins the desktop's `initialize` and `tools/list` bytes.

**GitHub App credentials** (`appToken.ts`, `credServer.ts`, `credCli.ts`, `credServe.ts`). On only when `github.appId`, `installationId` and `keyFile` are set. It is not a boundary against a session while sessions share fleet-host's uid (§9).
- The key is read once into a `KeyObject`, never into env, logs or errors (errors name `github.keyFile`, not its contents or path). Each GitHub call signs an RS256 App JWT (`iss` = App id, `iat` = now − 60 s, `exp` = `iat` + 540 s). The installation's account login is fetched once.
- **Every token is scoped to repositories.** A request names 1–100 repositories (and optionally `owner`, which must equal the installation's account, and `permissions`); an unscoped request is refused. `github.repositories`, when set, refuses anything outside it (403) without calling GitHub.
- **Permissions are a ceiling.** `github.permissions`, else `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` (`contents`, `pull_requests`, `issues`: write; `checks`, `actions`, `statuses`, `metadata`: read), is the default and the ceiling: a requested set must be a subset (write implies read), or 403 without calling GitHub. The minter always sends explicit `permissions` to `POST /app/installations/{id}/access_tokens` and hands out the token only when GitHub's repository list equals the request and its permissions are within the request plus `metadata: read`.
- Tokens are cached per scope until 5 min before `expires_at`; concurrent identical requests share one call; at most 64 cached and 4 mints in flight. A 429, or a 403 with `retry-after` or `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0`, opens a refusal window (1–60 s) answered 503 without calling GitHub. A 404 is a 502 naming `github.installationId`.
- **Socket:** HTTP on `FLEET_HOST_CRED_SOCKET` (0600, in a 0700 directory): `POST /token` with a JSON body ≤ 8 KiB → `{ token, expiresAt }` or `{ error }` with 400/403/404/413/500/502/503 (404 for any other method or path; 500 `internal error` for an unexpected failure). A request must arrive within 20 s (`requestTimeout`). The log records repositories, never a token.
- **`fleet-host-cred`** (the second bundle entry): `git-credential get` answers git's protocol for the one `owner/repo` in `path` (`https://github.com` only); `gh-token -- <args>` resolves the repo from `-R`/`--repo`, `GH_REPO`, a URL or `gh api repos/<o>/<r>/…` argument (text-flag values ignored), or the cwd's remotes (none if any remote isn't on github.com; else `upstream`, `github`, `origin`, then the first), and gives no token when gh would talk to another host; `serve` runs the socket standalone, reloading config like the host. Both token-printing commands refuse when stdout is a TTY, and there is no subcommand that prints a token for a person. Without a resolvable repo, git and `gh` get nothing and fall through (`gh search` and org-wide `gh api` run unauthenticated).

**Routes.**

| Route | Auth | Tier | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /healthz`, `GET /leader` | none | — | probes |
| `GET /whoami` | Bearer | any grant | `{ workspace: { id, name, description, color }, user, tier }` |
| `POST /rpc/<channel>` | Bearer | per channel | body `{"args":[…]}` (JSON, ≤ 64 KiB; at most 8 in flight per identity, more get 429 with `Retry-After: 1`) → `{"ok":true,"result":…}` or `{"ok":false,"error":"…"}`; 404 unlisted channel, 403 tier, 400 args, 413 body, 415 content type, 500 redacted `internal error` |
| `GET /attach` (WS) | Bearer on upgrade | per frame | binary broker frames |
| `GET /events` (WS) | Bearer on upgrade | `read` | server push `{channel, payload}` |

RPC channels: `sessions:live` (`read`, broker `LIST` → `{ id, alive, mine }[]`, `mine` = the caller is the session's `CLOSE` owner, `owners.get(id) === ownerKey(caller)`), `ports:list` (`read`), `ports:kill` (`steer`), `history:sessions` (`read`; args `[{ after?: { lastActiveAt, id }, limit? ≤ 200 }]` → `{ rows, next }`, a keyset page of the hosted workspace's sessions ordered `COALESCE(last_active_at, 0) DESC, id COLLATE "C"`, with `eventCount`/`usd` from `session_costs` and the latest chapter's tags; text clipped to 500 chars, tags to 20 × 64), `history:summary` (`read`; args `[sessionId?]` → `WorkspaceSummary | null`, null for a session outside the workspace) and `history:search` (`read`; args `[{ vector: number[384], modelId, limit?: 1–50 (default 10), kind?: 'turn' | 'summary' }]` → `{ modelId, hits: { sessionId, workspaceId, kind, ts, text, score }[] }`; `modelId` must equal `EMBED_MODEL_ID`, else 400 `model mismatch: host uses <id>`; every element must be finite and the norm within 1e-3 of 1; an exact pgvector scan over `{hosted}` in a `readTransaction` on the holder's state handle; text capped at 1000 characters; 503 `search unavailable` when `search.embedder` is false, checked before the arguments). All three use the holder's `HistoryReader` (`historyRead.ts`), whose SQL always names the `host_meta` workspace id; arguments are parsed strictly and never widen it (a `workspaceId` in the args is ignored). Without a holder (no `db:`, or stores not open) they answer 503 `history unavailable` (`RpcUnavailableError`). An older host without `history:search` answers 404, which the desktop lists as `protocol`. Unlisted channels (vault, filesystem, config, image, Docker) don't exist on the host.

**Verify limits** (`server.ts` `gate()`, in order): halted → 503 `shutting down`; standby → 503; a missing or malformed bearer → 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` (not counted); a token the caches already answer (`AuthContext.peek`: verified for 5 min, or rejected for 30 s in a 1000-entry SHA-256-keyed LRU) is answered without any limit; a cache miss then passes the per-address limiter (only when `trustedProxyHops > 0`), an in-flight cap of 16 GitHub verifications (503, `Retry-After: 1`) and a global budget of 30 non-OK verifications per minute (429 with `Retry-After`) before it reaches GitHub. Every non-OK result spends the budget (401, 403 refusals including `forbidden`, `machine-unknown` and no grant, 502) except a network error (503). A token GitHub verified OK in the last 24 h (a 10k-entry LRU of SHA-256 hashes, kept across reloads, refreshed by re-verify, dropped on a 401 or 403) skips the per-address limiter and the budget, but not the in-flight cap. The trade-off: one client spending the budget can lock out only callers whose tokens the host hasn't seen accept recently (new sign-ins, rotated tokens), and a stolen known-good token can drive GitHub calls bounded only by the in-flight cap and the 5-min verify cache. Network failures and machine refusals are never negatively cached.

**Connections.**
- At most 8 concurrent `/attach` + `/events` sockets per identity (`<provider>:<id>`); more get 429 with `Retry-After: 5`.
- `requestTimeout` 30 s, `headersTimeout` 10 s (per request), `keepAliveTimeout` 65 s (above the 60 s idle timeout of the ALB and Envoy, so the proxy closes idle connections first).
- Every WebSocket is pinged every 25 s (`PING_MS`) and terminated after 2 unanswered pings, which keeps idle sockets inside the ALB's 60 s idle timeout.
- A WebSocket protocol violation (an oversize message, a set RSV bit, a bad opcode, invalid UTF-8) is logged (`websocket error`) and terminates only that socket.
- A socket whose send buffer exceeds 8 MiB is closed 1013 (`viewer too slow`). Revoking a socket stops its frames at once, unsubscribes or disposes it, sends the close, and terminates it after 1 s.
- **Re-verify:** every open socket is re-checked every 60 s and after every applied reload. A lowered tier applies to the next frame; a rejected token closes 4401; a removed grant or refused machine closes 4403. While GitHub is unreachable (502/503) a socket is kept until it has gone unverified for more than 5 min, then closed 4401.

**`/attach`.**
- Each WebSocket binary message carries whole broker frames only; a message that ends mid-frame closes the socket 1002 (`partial frame`), so the host never reassembles a frame across messages. Channel numbers are per viewer connection.
- The host holds one upstream broker attachment per session (the broker allows one writer) and fans `OUTPUT` to every viewer. A newcomer gets a 64 KiB replay trimmed with the broker's `trimToSafeStart` rule. The last viewer leaving detaches the upstream and leaves the session alive. A second `ATTACH` of the same session on one connection is refused.
- Frame tiers: `ATTACH`, `HISTORY`, `LIST`, `LISTPORTS` need `read`; `INPUT`, `RESIZE`, `KILLPORT`, `DIAL` need `steer`; `CREATE`, `CLOSE` need `session`. `read` input is dropped. Server-only frame types close the socket 1008.
- `CREATE` args are rebuilt by the host: only optional `--settings <hooks>`, then `--resume <uuid>` or `--session-id <uuid>`; anything else is refused. With `db:` the host then appends its `--mcp-config=…` (*Host MCP*).
- `CLOSE` ownership: an in-memory map `broker session id → <provider>:<account id>`, set on a successful `CREATE`. Only the owner, at `session` tier, may close; sessions the process didn't create can't be closed by anyone. Owners are dropped when their session exits, and a 60 s timer (while holder) prunes owners of sessions the broker no longer lists as alive.
- `DIAL` passes the `steer` gate, then is refused ("port forwarding is not available on remote hosts").
- `KILLPORT` (and `ports:kill`, which answers 403) refuses the host's own port and any port the broker doesn't attribute to a session. Gaps: a TOCTOU window between `LISTPORTS` and `KILLPORT`, and a port owned by a session's root process itself kills that session (§11 *Remote host: what remains*).
- PTY size follows the most recent `steer` viewer's `RESIZE`, clamped to 1–1000.
- Per viewer: at most 256 frames and 8 MiB of payload pending (more closes 1008); control frames (all but `INPUT` and `RESIZE`) pass a token bucket of 20 burst, 10/s, answering `rate limited` beyond it.

**`/events`.** `sessions:changed` and `ports:changed` come from a broker poll every 2 s that runs only while the process holds the lease and something subscribes; the holder's automation runtime publishes `automations:changed`, `proposals:changed` and `inbox:changed`. The other desktop event channels are allowlisted for later publishers. Every frame is redacted (*Logging*) before it is sent.

**Shutdown and crashes.**
- SIGTERM/SIGINT: halt (authenticated routes and upgrades answer 503; every socket revoked with 1001) → close the holder's stores (bounded, *Host store*) → release the lease (bounded) → finish `close()` (`hub.shutdown()` detaches every upstream, bounded at 5 s; sessions stay alive) → exit 0. All of it fits the 30 s k8s grace.
- An uncaught exception or unhandled rejection is logged once per arrival with its redacted stack. The first one halts the endpoint (sockets revoked 1011), races the lease release against 2 s, and exits 1 without draining; the entry script restarts fleet-host.

**Logging.** One JSON line per event on stdout; one per request (`method`, `path`, `status`, `login`, `ms`; `/healthz` is not logged). Request headers are never logged, and every string, nested value and the final line are redacted (bearer, Basic, `gh*_`, `github_pat_`, `sk-ant-`, PEM private keys, URL credentials).
- **Redaction** (`redactSecrets`, `main/identity/redact.ts`): a token must not follow an alphanumeric, except one that ends a JSON escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\b`, `\f`) or an ANSI CSI code (raw or `\u001b`-escaped). Token classes exclude `"`, so a redacted JSON line stays valid.
- **Structural first:** ingest parses each line, redacts every nested string and re-serializes only when something changed (a clean line keeps its bytes and dedup hash); `/events` serializes with a string-redacting replacer. Both then redact the serialized text as a backstop. Decoding first catches tokens behind escapes the text pass can't see (e.g. BEL ending an OSC 8 link).

## 8. User flows

### Startup
1. Main creates the window, registers IPC handlers.
2. Before any IPC traffic, main runs `runStartupMigration()` from `src/main/migration.ts`. Four idempotent passes:
