   1. Legacy name-keyed state dirs get a fresh ULID and the dir is renamed in place (with the manifest rewritten to the new shape — `authMode='oauth'`, empty `env`, etc.).
   2. Any keytar entry under `service=claude-fleet` whose account doesn't fit the new `<id>:<key>` / `__secrets__:<id>` shape is deleted (`__profiles__:*` purge).
   3. **OAuth credentials consolidation** (Phase 3, #57): every workspace's per-workspace `.claude/.credentials.json` that's a *real non-empty file* (not a symlink, not empty) is folded into the shared file at `<userData>/claude-shared/.credentials.json`. If the shared file doesn't exist (or is empty) and at least one workspace has real credentials, the most-recently-modified one is promoted to the shared path; the others are backed up as `.credentials.json.old`. Every per-workspace path becomes a symlink to the shared file so the host-side filesystem reflects reality.
   4. **Fleet folders**: create `<fleetRoot>/shared` and each workspace's `<fleetRoot>/<id>` private folder, and rewrite any manifest whose `workspaceRoot` predates the fleet-root model to the derived private path.
3. Renderer mounts; on first render it calls `workspace:ping`. A false result does not block the app: a slim banner ("Docker daemon unreachable — container workspaces are shown from last-known state…") renders under the strip, container workspaces show per the degraded merge described above, local workspaces are fully usable (including creation — the create form's Container option is disabled with a hint while the daemon is down, and the form defaults to Local). Recovery is poll-driven: `workspace:ping` is called every 5s; when it returns true, `backendReady` flips to true (dropping the banner and re-enabling the daemon status dot), and the same `refresh()` call re-fetches the workspace list so real states replace the last-known-state entries.
4. Renderer calls `workspace:list` and polls every 5s thereafter to pick up state changes from outside the app. The list includes live workspaces (running/stopped) and deleted workspaces (manifest on disk, no container). The renderer keys selection by the ULID `id`; `containerId` is present only for live workspaces and is what stop/pause/remove/attach take.
5. **Store choice.** Before the inbox opens, main reads `config.json` `store` (§6 *Settings*).
   - **SQLite** (the default) opens `inbox.db` and, when the JSONL watcher runs, `state.db`, as before.
   - **SQLite with `inbox.db.on-postgres` present** (Continue without settings, a hand-edited config.json) doesn't open the inbox: the inbox IPC stays unregistered, and the status `detail` says to choose Postgres or switch back from a Postgres start.
   - **Boot functions:** the store-backed services start in named functions called in the original order on SQLite: `startStateWrites`, `startClaims`, `startWatcher`, `startCaptureScheduler`, `registerLocalDirs`, `startEmbedBackfill`, `startInboxServices` (notify, automation and inbox IPC, capture settings) and `startPresenceServices`. Inbox IPC registers inside `recordHandlers` so it can be removed and registered again.
   - **Postgres** (managed or external) doesn't hold the launch. The window, workspaces, terminals and the MCP server start at once; the MCP history tools answer "unavailable" until the store is ready. `StoreSupervisor` (`store/storeSupervisor.ts`) is built before IPC registration and run after the window exists. It:
     1. **opens** the store: `ensureManagedPostgres` (managed; its steps show as `starting` detail), then `openPgStores` with the `state`, `inbox` and `import` migrations. The inbox opens with the inbox write gate and the state store with the history write gate as `afterBeginWrite`, so every bare write on Postgres is wrapped in `begin`/`commit`. A failure is `down` with its reason (`store-open-failed`), closes what opened, and retries after 1 s, doubling to 30 s.
     2. **copies** on the first switch (§7 *First-switch copy*), reporting `importing` progress; its notice becomes `status.notice`.
     3. **readies** the services as `ServiceSteps` (`store/serviceSteps.ts`): named, idempotent steps in an inbox group (claims, inbox services, presence) and a history group (`openDbPostgres`, which rehydrates pending mappings from the copied rows, state writes, the MCP read source with `query` isolation, the watcher, local dirs, the embedding backfill), with the capture scheduler held back until claims and the watcher are up. A failed step stops the rest of its group only; the status is `ready` with `detail` `Inbox unavailable: …` / `History unavailable: …` (logged once per message as `store-ready-failed`), and every good ping retries until it succeeds. Then `inbox:changed` is broadcast.
     4. **watches** with `SELECT 1` every 5 s (3 s timeout). A failure is `down` (`store-unreachable`, once per outage); for the managed store it also runs `ensureManagedPostgres` to restart a stopped container. The next good ping is `ready` again: the watcher re-reads every known transcript from its offset (`requeueAll`; a failed batch never advanced it) and `inbox:changed` is broadcast. pg's pool reconnects on its own because the port is fixed.
     - Bring-up attempts, ping-and-repair and the switch back run under one lock (`exclusive`). `stop()` cancels a queued attempt and closes a bring-up that finishes after it without readying it.
   - **While not ready** the store banner shows (§6 *Store*). Store-backed IPC is unregistered (inbox) or rejects (`db not opened`); it never falls back to SQLite, which would split the data. Pool errors are logged as `store-pool-error`; an `aws-rds-iam` auth failure (28P01/28000) carries a hint that names no secret. An isolation failure is logged as `mcp-query-isolation`; `query` then reports the reason and every other tool works.
   - **An invalid `store` block** stops the launch with a Quit-only error dialog naming the key to remove.
   - **Mock mode** reads config.json (for the unreadable-JSON dialog) and then uses SQLite.
   - **Quit** (#485): `before-quit` holds the quit once (`preventDefault`), stops the supervisor, waits up to 5 s for `exclusive`, runs the existing teardown, `closeDb`, the pool's `end()` and `stopManagedPostgres`, all bounded to 10 s (`QUIT_BOUND_MS`), then `app.exit(0)`. A second quit passes straight through. `docker stop -t 10` can be cut off by the bound, which WAL makes safe. On SQLite, when `prepareVacuum` says so, the one-time VACUUM helper is spawned just before `app.exit` (§7 *Retention*); the next start waits for it before `openDb`.
   - **A `config.json` that isn't valid JSON** raises a dialog with **Quit** (default) and **Continue without settings**. The dialog says that if the file configured Postgres, continuing uses the local SQLite store instead. Continuing boots on SQLite with in-memory default settings and leaves the file untouched. A sticky "Settings can't be saved" toast names the file until it's fixed and the app restarted. The choice logic is `store/startupPrompt.ts`.

### Create a workspace
1. User clicks the **`+`** button in the top strip (adds a workspace — new or resumed).
2. `<WorkspaceModal>` opens. Two tabs at top: **Saved** (count badge) + **New**, underlined-tab style. Default tab is Saved when at least one non-running workspace exists, else New.
3. The Saved tab lists every stopped / deleted workspace (the "cold" fleet; running/paused are in the strip, #21) — sorted with a Variant-B label-filter search at the top: text input matches name + description (substring, case-insensitive), and a **Labels** dropdown opens a checkbox list of fleet-wide labels with usage counts. Selected labels filter the list as OR (any-match); active filters surface as removable pills above the list with an `N of M` count on the right. Each row shows a color identity bar, name, description, state badge, and last-used relative time. Clicking a row's header animates it open (CSS `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr`, 320ms cubic-bezier; chevron rotates 180°; form contents fade-and-slide in over 220ms with 80ms delay) and renders `WorkspaceForm` inline in `mode='edit'` with the persisted spec pre-filled. The expanded row's footer is **Delete (danger)** on the far left, then `Cancel · Clone · Resume` on the right.
4. **Resume** (primary in the expanded form) — `App.handleResume`:
   - Writes any newly-typed secret env values via `vault:setSecret(id, key, value)`. Pre-existing secrets the user didn't touch are left alone.
   - Calls `workspace:writeManifest(spec)` so renderer-visible edits (description, labels, color, name) take effect immediately even if the container needs a restart for env/image changes.
   - Calls `workspace:start(id)`. If a container exists (state was stopped/paused/running with no label match), it's started. If null (state was deleted, container gone), the renderer falls through to the create flow with the **same id reused** so state-dir + vault history stay attached.
5. **Clone** in the expanded form opens the modal in Clone mode — `App.openCloneFrom` strips the source's id, auto-suggests `<source>-N` (incrementing N from 2 until unused), clears the color so a fresh hue is picked, and the modal's New tab takes the foreground pre-filled with the source's spec. Extra mounts are copied as-is (re-validated on create). The user can edit before clicking **Create & start**. Source workspace is unchanged.
6. **Delete** in the expanded form (or chip ⋮ menu) opens `DeleteWorkspaceModal` — a confirm modal with explicit warning text. On confirm: `workspace:stop` (if live) → `workspace:remove(containerId, { deleteState: true })` (wipes state dir + manifest) → `vault:deleteAllForWorkspace(id)` (purges every secret).
7. **Cancel** in the expanded form collapses the row without applying changes.
6. The **New** tab is the Create form. The user fills:
   - **Type** radio: Container (default — isolated Docker runner), Local, or Remote (see *Remote kind* below).
   - For Container, an **Image** input appears with the inline image-library picker beneath it (default = most-recently-used library entry, or the bundled runner). A magnifying-glass button right of the input opens **`AdvancedImageSearchModal`** — a focused search surface mirroring the Saved-tab Variant-B shape (text input matches ref + digest, **Tags** dropdown filters by the `:tag` segment with OR semantics + pills). Each row in the list also surfaces which workspaces currently use the image (stopped consumers called out in warning color), so pinning a specific build is informed.
   - **Name** with pet-name placeholder and a **color swatch** to the left (popover with 14 OKLCH preset hues + Random). The swatch is dashed when no hue has been picked — `WorkspaceTabStrip` falls back to a name-hash of the same palette in that case so the chip still gets a stable distinct color.
   - **Description** textarea (optional).
   - **Labels** chip input with `<datalist>` autocomplete drawn from every other workspace's `labels[]`. Used for filtering in the Saved-tab list (PR 2 surface).
   - **Subfolder in /workspace** (optional) — a working subdir inside the container's private folder. (There is no host-path picker: the private + shared folders come from the app-level fleet root, configured in Settings.)
   - **Model** combobox: Claude (first and default); one entry per registry endpoint showing `name` and `modelId · baseUrl`; a final "＋ Add endpoint…" entry that deep-links to Settings → Model Endpoints. Sits directly above Auth.
   - **Auth** row (contextual): when Model = Claude, shows the OAuth (default) / API key radios — API key is disabled until `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in the env list below. When Model = endpoint, the radios are replaced by a passive note naming the endpoint's stored key. `authMode` and `endpointId` are derived in the renderer (`modelPicker.ts`) from the Model selection; the manifest and IPC wire shape are unchanged. A manifest `endpointId` missing from the registry renders "(deleted endpoint)" in the note and blocks submit until the user re-picks a model.
   - **Env vars** disclosure: KV editor with a per-row `secret` toggle. Plain rows land in `manifest.env.plain`; secret rows ship to `vault:setSecret(<newId>, key, value)` and only their *key names* are listed in `manifest.env.secretKeys`. The secret toggle is forced off when `vault:available` returns false. Edit mode shows pre-existing secret keys with a "•••••" placeholder; the user can replace by typing or leave untouched and the keychain entry stays as-is. Every secret row has an **eye button** (show/hide). On a value typed in this form it just flips the input between password and text. On a pre-existing (saved) secret it fetches the value via `vault:getSecret(id, key)` and shows it in an amber-outlined input with a countdown ring ("Hides in Ns"); it re-masks after **30 s** (`REVEAL_SECONDS` in `secretReveal.ts`), on a second click, on any edit to the row (key rename or removal), or when the form unmounts. Revealing alone is not an edit (the revealed text is held apart from the row's `value`, so Save writes nothing and no restart banner fires); typing over a revealed value replaces the secret and keeps the new text visible. A null result shows "Not in the vault. Type a value to set it, or remove the row."; a fetch failure shows the error and leaves the value hidden. In a Clone-prefilled form (no workspace id — the keys belong to the source) the eye is disabled on pre-existing rows.
   - **Extra mounts** disclosure (Container only), between Env vars and Resource caps: summary reads `(none)` or `N folders` (`· M read-only` when any row is read-only). Each row is host folder + **Browse…** (native directory picker, `window.api.dialog.pickDirectory`) → container path + **read-only** checkbox + **×** remove, plus a **+ Add mount** button. Host-path placeholder is platform-aware (`C:\path\to\folder` on win32, `/home/you/folder` elsewhere); the container-path placeholder is the suggested `/mnt/<basename>` once a host path is typed, else `/mnt/folder`. Validation (shared rules in `src/preload/extraMounts.ts`, so the form and the main-process check agree) runs per row as the user types — an invalid or duplicate container path shows an inline error under that row — and again on submit, which re-opens the disclosure and blocks if any row still errors. A mounts change counts as container-level for the restart-to-apply banner (`EditWorkspaceModal.tsx:containerLevelChanged`).
   - **Resource caps** disclosure (Container only): CPUs + Memory MB. Blank → no Docker limit.
7. **Create & start** submits. The renderer:
   1. Validates name (1–80 chars, no control characters, no name clash against the existing fleet) and env-var keys (`[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]+`, no duplicates).
   2. Mints a fresh ULID via the `ulid` package (in `WorkspaceModal.handleCreate`, before the form's payload reaches App).
   3. For each secret env row, calls `vault:setSecret(id, key, value)`. Failures are warned but don't block the create (the container will start with the secret resolving to `""`).
   4. Calls `workspace:ensureImage(channelId, submit.image)` (pulls the selected image with progress in the modal), then `workspace:create` with the payload (id, name, description, labels, color, workspaceSubdir, kind, image, authMode, endpointId, env: { plain, secretKeys }, resources, mounts) — no host path for `workspaceRoot` itself; `mounts` is the one place the user does pick host paths, validated server-side (§6 *Workspace*).
8. Main creates the workspace's private folder (`<fleetRoot>/<id>`) and the shared folder (`<fleetRoot>/shared`), creates the container (`docker create cf-<id>` with the `com.claude-fleet.id` label, the private folder bound at `/workspace` and shared at `/shared`, env resolved through `vault.resolveEnv`), writes the manifest, records the image in the library, and returns the `Workspace`.
9. The top strip refreshes; the new workspace appears and is auto-selected.

**Remote kind.** In create mode the Type radio also offers **Remote** ("a fleet-host someone deployed"), which swaps the form for `RemoteWorkspaceForm`. Signed out, it shows the Account sign-in panel (`AccountSettings onStatus`) and switches to the URL field once signed in. **Check host** calls `remote:probe`; main may show its send-token dialog (§9). A good probe shows the host's name, description, "Signed in as @login · your tier: <badge>", and the plain-http warning when set. **Add workspace** calls `remote:add`, closes the modal and selects the new chip. When the id is already bookmarked at another URL (`existingUrl`, or an add answered `moved`), the button reads **Move here** and re-sends with `sameHostNewAddress: true`. `declined` and `moved` are neutral notes that keep the last good probe on screen; every other error is red (`remoteView.probeErrorText`: unreachable adds the network/VPN hint; `sso` carries the GitHub authorise link). The form's logic is the pure `remoteView.ts` (`applyProbe`, `remoteFormView`).

### Attach a terminal
1. User selects a workspace in the top strip (only live workspaces appear there).
2. `<TerminalPane>` mounts. It reads the workspace's persisted `sessions.json` via `sessions:read(workspaceId)`. If the inventory is non-empty the saved tabs are restored (including which one was active); otherwise a single auto-created `main` tab is inserted and persisted right away. The pane manages a tab strip above the terminal body and one `<TerminalSession>` per tab stacked in the body — only the active tab is `visibility: visible`, the rest stay mounted so their PTYs and scrollback are preserved across tab switches.
3. Each `<TerminalSession>` creates an `xterm` `Terminal` (DOM renderer by default — see *Terminal renderer* below — so it does native per-glyph CSS font fallback), fits to its host div, calls `pty:attach(containerId, cols, rows)` → gets a `sessionId`. **Terminal fonts:** `TERMINAL_FONT_FAMILY` is a monospace-first chain with symbol/emoji fallbacks. Because the Linux/WSLg fontconfig set has no glyph for some characters Claude Code's TUI emits — the permission-mode media triangles (`⏵⏵ accept edits on`, U+23F5) and the tool-result tree connector (`⎿`, U+23BF) — two subsetted symbol fonts are **bundled** via `@font-face` (styles.css, Miscellaneous-Technical block only, ~3–4KB each): `Noto Sans Symbols 2` (crisp, preferred) and `Unifont` (last-resort catch-all for glyphs even Noto lacks, e.g. `⎿`). Both are **inlined as data: URIs** (`electron.vite.config.ts` forces `assetsInlineLimit` for `.woff2`) — the renderer is served over `file://`, where an emitted-as-a-file `@font-face` woff2 fails to load. After `term.open` the session forces `document.fonts.load(...)` for both families and refreshes so the glyphs don't flash as tofu on first paint. (Unifont's source `OS/2` carries an out-of-spec unicode-range bit that must be zeroed before subsetting, or Chrome's OTS sanitizer rejects the woff2.) It registers `onData` (writes chunks into xterm) and `onEnd` (shows the session-ended overlay). `term.onData` forwards to `pty:input(sessionId, data)`. A `ResizeObserver` re-fits and calls `pty:resize` on host div resize. The end-state overlay has two variants: a **natural** card ("claude session ended — Start new session") when `pty:end` fires after a successful attach, and an **attach-error** card surfacing the error message verbatim plus a `docker pull` hint when `pty:attach` itself throws (most often: stale runner image missing the broker, broker socket unreachable). The attach-error variant exists because the overlay is `position: absolute` over `.terminal-host` — without it, any error text written into xterm would be hidden behind the modal.
4. Clicking the **+** in the tab strip creates a new session. The first session is named `main`; subsequent sessions are `session 2`, `session 3`, … via a counter that doesn't decrement on close (so names stay stable). Each tab carries a small status dot showing two states today: **live** (PTY attached, normal-color dot) and **ended** (PTY exited, grey dot). Lifecycle is driven by the existing `pty:end` signal each `TerminalSession` already observes; `TerminalSession` reports state changes via an `onLifecycleChange(sessionId, 'live' | 'ended')` callback the parent `TerminalPane` aggregates into a `Set<string>` of ended tab ids. The dot flips back to live on the next "Start new session" click. Clicking a tab switches the active session. The **×** on a tab closes it; closing the last session auto-creates a fresh `main` so the strip is never empty. Every change is persisted to `sessions.json` immediately so a sudden quit doesn't lose tabs. Richer per-tab states (`idle`, `needs-input`) land when the observability watcher + permission-request log expose the relevant signals.
5. On unmount (workspace removed, or app close): each `<TerminalSession>` unsubscribes listeners, calls `pty:detach`, disposes the terminal.

**Remote panes** (`TerminalPane remoteTier`, rules in `remoteView.paneRules`; `null` tier = treated as `read` until the host answers). Tabs stay per desktop (`sessions.json`).
- **New session** (`+`) appears only for the `session` tier. Below it, an empty inventory opens no default tab and closing the last tab leaves none (creating one would fail); the pane says "No sessions open here. Use Join… to open one that is running on the host." If the tier was unknown at load and turns out `session`, the default `main` tab opens then.
- **Join…** lists the host's live sessions (`remote:liveSessions`) that aren't already tabs; picking one opens it as a tab. A listing failure shows its error.
- **Watch-only** (`read`): the tab is tagged "watching"; `TerminalSession readOnly` drops keystrokes, clipboard paste and the Ctrl+L repaint nudge, and never sends `pty:resize` (the local fit still runs). The host drops `read` input anyway.
- **Tab close** detaches (§6 `pty:closeSessionByBroker`). **End session…** in the tab ⋮ menu appears only for the `session` tier on a session the host reports as `mine` (the menu waits up to 1 s for `remote:liveSessions`, then opens with the last known `mine`; clicks while it waits are ignored); it confirms in a `ModalBackdrop` ("This stops the session on the host for everyone watching it.", Cancel focused), then `pty:endSessionByBroker`.
- The mirror toggle, Refresh and Auto rename are hidden (no local transcript).

### Remote host unreachable
A remote chip whose host isn't answering is dimmed (dashed, static red dot, sub-line "host unreachable") and inert except for its ⋮ menu; it never moves to the Saved list (`fleetTemperature.isWarm` is always true for remote). Its pane reads "Host unreachable — <name> (<url>) isn't answering. Its sessions reappear when it's back." A slim banner under the top strip names every such host ("*review-bot* unreachable: its sessions and automations are hidden until it's back"; several names → "their … they're back"), with its own grid track (`.app.remote-down`, stacking with the Docker banner). A bookmark not yet probed this run (`status: 'checking host'`) is neutral instead: grey dot, "checking host…", pane "Checking host…", and not in the banner. Remote dots don't pulse.

**Bare LF must not gain a carriage return.** xterm's `convertEol` is **off**, and must stay off. It makes a bare LF behave as CRLF — correct for line-oriented output piped into a terminal, wrong for a full-screen TUI. Claude's Ink renderer positions absolutely and then relies on LF meaning "down one row, same column"; an injected carriage return moves the cursor to column 0, so a frame that positions to column 3, erases to end of line and emits LF no longer writes where it intended, stranding the previous frame's leading characters in the first columns. That is the root cause of #268's stray `Th`/`Co`/`Tw` fragments, and why they survived every renderer change: the damage happens in the **parser**, before anything is painted — which is also why xterm's buffer and the rendered DOM always agreed, and why disabling hardware acceleration, switching to the canvas renderer, and the scrollback-reflow fix all made no difference. A real PTY already applies ONLCR on the slave side, so a bare LF needing a CR has one before it reaches us; the option only ever added a second. Pinned by `terminalOptions.test.ts` (LF preserves the cursor column; an Ink-style position→erase→LF→write frame keeps its indentation).

**Terminal renderer.** Resolved per pane at mount as **workspace override → app default → `dom`** (`CLAUDE_FLEET_TERMINAL_RENDERER` wins over both, for tests). The app default is `config.json`'s `terminalRenderer` (Settings → Behavior); the override is `WorkspaceSpec.terminalRenderer` (workspace form, "Default (from Settings)" ⇒ absent ⇒ inherit). Scoped per workspace because the artifact it works around is reported on **local** workspaces specifically, while the DOM renderer is otherwise preferable for its font fallback — so this is not a fleet-wide switch. Both the manifest parser and the create path sanitize the value: the parser is a strict allowlist and the create path builds the spec field by field, so an unlisted field is silently dropped rather than persisted. Changing it affects terminals opened afterwards, not live ones — changing it affects terminals opened afterwards, not live ones. `dom` is the historical default and the only renderer doing native per-glyph CSS font fallback, which is what the bundled Noto Sans Symbols 2 / Unifont subsets rely on for `⏵` (U+23F5) and `⎿` (U+23BF). It is also the renderer most exposed to Chromium's scroll/paint invalidation: the grid is thousands of absolutely-positioned spans inside a scrolling container, and narrow stale strips can survive a scroll, showing a previous frame's characters pinned to screen rows while the content moves past them (#268 — the buffer and the DOM are both correct in that state, which is why `term.refresh()` in #328 did not help and why disabling hardware acceleration does not either; Chromium tiles in software compositing too). `canvas` and `webgl` paint the whole grid as one element, where that class of artifact cannot occur. **Fallback is part of the contract:** the addon is loaded *after* `term.open()` (it needs the renderer wired) and is guarded — WebGL is unavailable in headless Linux/WSLg and a driver can refuse or lose a context at any time, so failure leaves a working DOM-rendered terminal and logs `terminal-renderer-failed` rather than blanking the pane; `WebglAddon.onContextLoss` disposes for the same reason. An unrecognised value resolves to `dom` on both read and write. Verified by `tests/terminal-renderer.spec.ts`, which asserts on the rendered output (canvas layers vs. DOM rows) rather than the config value — the regression that matters is the addon silently not attaching while the setting claims it did.

**Always-mounted TerminalPanes.** App.tsx renders one `<TerminalPane>` per live workspace (state ≠ `deleted`), all permanently mounted; the one matching `selectedId` has `visible={true}` and the rest are CSS-hidden (`visibility: hidden` to preserve xterm layout dimensions; `pointer-events: none` so hidden panes don't intercept clicks on the visible one stacked at the same coords). Keying is by workspace name (not containerId) so the pane survives container stop+start — sessions.json is per-workspace, not per-container. **Workspace switches are a CSS toggle, not a remount**: xterm scrollback, broker connections, and PTY state persist across selection changes; only an actual workspace removal triggers teardown. This is the architectural fix for a long tail of timing-sensitive bugs (HISTORY frame dropped on re-attach, xterm `Viewport.syncScrollArea` crash on remount, broker `EventEmitter` race on attach) — all of those were second-order effects of the previous tear-down-on-switch model.

**Paused state.** When the selected workspace's state is `paused`, the terminal pane renders a modal card centered in the session-stack ("workspace paused" + Resume button) while the underlying `TerminalSession`s stay mounted but are dimmed (~40% opacity + greyscale + pointer-events disabled). The session tab strip and accent band stay live so the user can see which tabs exist and which workspace they're looking at. The chip in the workspace ribbon also shows a small ⏸ glyph and an amber status dot. The Resume button calls `workspace:start(name)`, which `docker unpause`s the container; the next `workspace:list` poll picks up the running state and the overlay disappears. **`TerminalSession` must not initiate an ATTACH while the workspace is paused**: `docker pause` freezes the broker (PID 1), but a Unix-socket *connect* still succeeds (the kernel parks it in the listen backlog), so an ATTACH sent to a frozen broker never gets its `ATTACHED` reply and the RPC hangs the full `RPC_TIMEOUT_MS` (30s) before failing `ATTACHED timed out`. The pane therefore passes `paused` into `TerminalSession`, which sets up xterm but **skips the network attach** while paused; the attach effect depends on `paused`, so when Resume flips it false the effect re-runs and attaches cleanly (the broker replays its ring buffer). This is what makes reopen-while-paused safe — without it, the always-mounted pane would auto-attach into the frozen broker on app start. Workspace-ribbon chips for other workspaces remain interactive so the user can switch to another workspace without resuming.

**Stopped state.** A `stopped` workspace is **not** in the top strip and has no main-pane (the always-mounted panes are running/paused only, #21) — it lives in the workspace modal's Saved list. Restart is the Saved-row **Resume** (writes the manifest, then checks the image via `workspace:resumeImageStale(id)` — which pulls the manifest's ref — and if the stopped container is now running a stale image it **recreates** via `applyContainerEdit` so the resume runs the newer image; otherwise `workspace:start(id)` → `docker start`. Either way, on the next `workspace:list` it flips to `running`, rejoins the strip, and gets auto-selected; sessions mount fresh from `sessions.json`). This is why a `:latest` workspace picks up a republished runner on its next resume, and why editing the image field on a stopped workspace takes effect — a stopped container's image is fixed at create time, so a plain `docker start` can't change it. Only stopped containers auto-recreate on a newer image; running/paused ones (with live/suspended sessions) refresh only via the explicit edit → restart-to-apply banner. (An earlier iteration, #17, surfaced a "Start" overlay on a stopped chip *in the strip*; #21 superseded it by moving stopped out of the strip entirely.) The "+ new tab" button is disabled while paused. **In-memory continuity (#18):** because the broker owns every PTY inside the container (not the host), a paused workspace survives a full app quit — on reopen + Resume the host re-attaches to the same live `claude` processes with their in-memory context intact, not fresh re-spawns. The reattach is reliable across both broker failure modes: the frozen-broker ATTACH hang is avoided by the paused attach-gate above, and the unpause-reap race is covered by the host-side `already attached` retry (§6). A `stopped` (not paused) container is genuinely down — its PTYs are gone and Resume mounts fresh sessions from `sessions.json`.

Each `pty:attach` runs `claude` fresh inside the container via `docker exec` — it is *not* the container's main process. The container's main process is `sleep infinity`, kept alive by `tini`. Multiple sessions in the same workspace are independent `docker exec claude` processes side by side.

**Copy and paste**: the terminal pane has selection-aware key bindings. Ctrl+C copies when a selection exists and falls through as SIGINT when not; Ctrl+V pastes. Ctrl+Shift+C / Ctrl+Shift+V are unconditional copy / paste (terminal-convention alternates). Right-click opens a native context menu (Copy / Paste / Select All) via `menu:showTerminalContextMenu`. Clipboard reads and writes route through `clipboard:read` / `clipboard:write` in main, not `navigator.clipboard`. **OSC 52 (set clipboard) is honored, write-only**: `TerminalSession` registers an xterm OSC 52 handler (`osc52.ts:decodeOsc52`) that writes the decoded text via `clipboard:write`. This is how claude's *own* copy (fullscreen-mode selection + Ctrl+C, `/copy`) reaches the host inside a container, where claude finds no native clipboard tool and falls back to OSC 52 — unhandled, those copies silently vanished (WSL-local sessions never hit this; claude reaches the Windows clipboard via interop). A `?` read query is ignored so nothing in a container can read the host clipboard; payloads over 1 MB of base64 or with invalid UTF-8 are dropped. On a remote workspace's tab OSC 52 is swallowed and never written (`osc52ClipboardText`, `TerminalSession remote`): another viewer of a shared session, or the host, could otherwise set this desktop's clipboard, including during history replay. The terminal's `wordSeparator` is tuned to whitespace + brackets + quotes only, so double-clicking a URL selects the whole URL (URL-safe characters like `/`, `?`, `&`, `=`, `.`, `:` stay inside the word).

**Clickable links**: `term.registerLinkProvider` walks back to the first non-wrapped row, forward through `isWrapped` continuations, concatenates the rows, and matches URLs against the joined text — so a URL that soft-wraps across multiple rows is registered as a single link spanning all of them. Activation calls `window.open`, which `setWindowOpenHandler` routes through `shell.openExternal`. OSC 8 hyperlinks (escape-sequence links claude emits, e.g. MCP auth URLs) bypass the provider and go through the `linkHandler` terminal option, which likewise calls `window.open(uri)` — never xterm's default handler, whose blocking `confirm()` + `window.open()`-then-assign-href only ever surfaced `about:blank` to main (Windows then offered to install an "About" app from the Store).

### Manage per-workspace env vars
The global Profiles modal is gone. Per-workspace env vars (both plain and secret) live in the Env-vars disclosure inside `WorkspaceForm` — the same component renders for new workspaces in the New tab, for non-running workspaces in the Saved tab's inline expand-edit form, and for **running** workspaces in `EditWorkspaceModal` (opened via the chip ⋮ menu). Underneath, the renderer calls `vault:setSecret(workspaceId, key, value)` / `vault:deleteSecret(workspaceId, key)` to mutate keychain values, and the manifest's `env.secretKeys` array tracks which keys exist for that workspace so the editor can surface them without reading a value (the only read is the user-initiated eye-button reveal described in §8 *Create a workspace*).

### Chip ⋮ menu (live workspaces)
Each chip in the workspace strip has a `⋮` trigger that opens a contextual menu. The menu's contents depend on the workspace's state:
- **Pause / Stop** (running), **Resume** (paused), or **Start** (stopped) — single-action lifecycle controls.
- **Edit…** — opens `EditWorkspaceModal`, a single-purpose modal wrapping `WorkspaceForm` in edit mode with the workspace's spec pre-filled. On Save, `App.handleEditSave` persists secrets + calls `workspace:writeManifest`, then compares pre-edit vs post-edit specs (`containerLevelChanged` in `EditWorkspaceModal.tsx`). Render-only changes (`name`, `description`, `labels`, `color`) take effect immediately. Container-level changes (`env`, `image`, `authMode`, `resources`) flip the **restart-to-apply banner** in the workspace's `TerminalPane` (see below).
- **Clone…** — opens the workspace modal in Clone mode pre-filled from this workspace (same `App.openCloneFrom` flow as the Saved-tab Clone footer button).
- **Close…** — opens `CloseWorkspaceModal` (preserves state dir).
- **Delete…** — opens `DeleteWorkspaceModal` (purges state dir + every keychain entry under the workspace's id). Distinct from Close — Close keeps the workspace in the Saved list; Delete makes it disappear.

A **remote** chip shows a cloud glyph (tooltip "Remote · <url>") and a neutral tier badge (`read`/`steer`/`session`, `?` until known; tooltip from `tierTitle`). Its status dot follows `/healthz`. Its ⋮ menu — kept while unreachable — has only **Rename locally…** (`RenameRemoteModal` → `remote:rename`; the host keeps its name), **Copy host URL** and **Forget…**, which confirms in a `ModalBackdrop` ("Forget <name>? Removes the bookmark and this computer's tab list. The host and its sessions are untouched.", Cancel focused, danger Forget) then calls `workspace:remove`. No Pause, Stop, Edit or Clone: the deployment owns those.

### Restart-to-apply banner
`TerminalPane` renders a dismissable banner above the session tab strip when `EditWorkspaceModal` saves changes to any container-level field on a running or paused workspace:

> Changes apply on next start. **[Restart now]** ×

- **Restart now**: **recreates** the container from the saved manifest — `workspace:getManifest(id)` → `workspace:ensureImage` (pull the possibly-new image) → `workspace:stop(containerId)` → `workspace:remove(containerId, {deleteState:false, id})` → `workspace:create(spec)`, reusing the same ULID so the private state dir + vault history stay attached. A plain `stop`→`start` would reuse the existing container and silently *not* apply the image/env/resource change (the container's spec is fixed at create time), so it must be recreated. The orchestration lives in `applyContainerEdit` (`src/renderer/src/workspaceLifecycle.ts`, unit-tested with an injected api); on success the banner clears and the workspace is re-focused, on failure the banner stays up for a retry. Recreating ends the current PTYs/Claude sessions (they start fresh in the new container); the host-side `/workspace` files persist and remount.
- **×** (dismiss): banner clears without recreating.
- The banner is keyed by workspace id in App.tsx's `restartBannerIds` Set; the corresponding TerminalPane reads `restartBanner` as a prop and renders accordingly. The Set survives chip switches — selecting another workspace and coming back still shows the banner until dismissed.

### Close a workspace
1. User selects a workspace, then clicks **Close…** in the main-pane header.
2. `<CloseWorkspaceModal>` opens, showing the workspace name and current status. A single checkbox — "Also delete the state directory" — is unchecked by default (Keep is the spec default; recreating with the same name inherits prior Claude state and keeps the workspace in the past list).
3. Action buttons depend on current state:
   - **Running**: `Stop only` (calls `workspace:stop` → state goes to `stopped`), `Pause` (calls `workspace:pause` → state goes to `paused`, processes frozen via cgroups, recoverable), and `Stop & remove` (calls `workspace:stop` then `workspace:remove(id, { deleteState })`).
   - **Paused**: `Resume` (calls `workspace:start` → unpauses), and `Stop & remove` (forces SIGKILL via `remove --force`, so pause state doesn't block removal).
   - **Exited / stopped**: only `Remove` (calls `workspace:remove(id, { deleteState })`).
4. On success, the modal closes, the selection clears, and the top strip refreshes. With `deleteState=false` the workspace transitions to "deleted" state (still in the past list, recoverable via restart). With `deleteState=true` it's fully purged. Failures surface inline in the modal.

### Browse & resume a past session
The left rail (`SessionsPane`, the 280px column) is the Sessions table. It mirrors the ObservabilityPane's scope control: **This workspace** (the selected one) or **All** (every live workspace). A search box filters by title + workspace name (and tag text); the list refetches on scope/selection change, on every `observability:summary` push (throttled, so just-active sessions surface live), and after the pane's own rename/delete actions.

**Open / Recent grouping.** The list splits into two labeled groups — `Open · N` above, `Recent` below — rendered only when at least one session qualifies. A session is **open** when a mounted workspace's terminal tab has a live (non-ended) PTY and that tab's broker session maps to the claude session UUID via `broker_sessions`. Openness is derived entirely in the renderer: `TerminalPane` emits the current live (non-ended) broker-session-id set via `onLiveIdsChange`; App.tsx collects these per-workspace, resolves each broker id to a claude UUID via `observability.summaryForBrokerSession(…)` (the same trust model and re-resolution path used for the busy pulse), and builds the `openSessions` map passed down to `SessionsPane`. **Every group is ordered newest-first at 30-minute resolution** (`orderByRecencyBucket` in `sessionsView.ts`: `lastActiveAt` floored to a 30-min bucket, descending; ties by `startedAt` descending, then id), not raw last-active: a busy session writing transcripts moves up at most once per bucket boundary instead of on every write, so the rows stay put while you work. **Automation runs** (sessions whose `broker_sessions` mapping comes from an `auto-*` broker id — `listSessions` derives `headless`) are pulled out of Recent into their own `Automation runs · N` group at the bottom (`splitHeadless`), **collapsed by default** and auto-expanded while a search/tag filter is active; the display-prefs limits apply to it as they do to Recent. Without this, every run lands a fresh transcript at the top of Recent and shoves the user's own sessions down. Transcripts from runs that predate the mapping stay in Recent. **Knowledge-capture forks** (#483 — `SessionListItem.capture`, `sessions:list` stamping `isCaptureFork(id)` from the in-memory registry `captureForks.ts`) are pulled out the same way into their own `Knowledge capture · N` group below Automation runs (`splitCapture` in `sessionsView.ts`), **collapsed by default**, auto-expanded under the same search/tag condition, under the same display-prefs limits, and excluded from the "All · N" badge entirely (background bookkeeping runs, not sessions a user browses). Open rows get a green left edge + tinted background; the row for the **current tab** (the selected workspace's active tab, resolved to a claude UUID by App as `currentSessionId`) is emphasized with a full green outline, a thicker edge, stronger tint, a bold title and `aria-current`. Rows FLIP-animate only when they actually move — between groups as PTYs start and end, or when a session crosses into a newer recency bucket. Openness is not persisted — it self-heals on every observability push that updates the broker→claude mapping. Non-goals: no persistent open state, no main-process open derivation, no `#` search grammar.

**Clicking an open session** selects its workspace and activates the already-open tab instead of opening a new resume tab: App calls `setActivateRequest({ workspaceId, brokerSessionId, token })` (token is a monotone counter so the same target can be re-activated); `TerminalPane` consumes via `activateRequest`/`onActivateConsumed` (token-guarded, mirrors `resumeRequest`/`onResumeConsumed`) and calls `setActiveId(brokerSessionId)`.

Each row shows the display title, and — in **All** scope — a workspace color dot + name, plus relative last-active time and USD cost. The meta line also shows up to 2 **tag chips** (Library `.tag` style) from the session's latest tagged summary chapter; clicking a chip toggles it as a filter. A **Tags ▾** checkbox-menu (one checkbox per distinct tag with occurrence counts, green `.pill` chips for active filters, `N of M` session count) sits below the search field; text search also matches tag text; multi-selected tags are OR-combined. Tags are `[]` when no summary chapter exists yet. Hover (or focus) reveals a single **`⋮` trigger** per row, which opens a portaled `.ws-chip-menu` dropdown (viewport-coordinate anchor, closes on outside click / Escape / scroll / resize — the same pattern as the workspace-chip and session-tab menus; the three former per-row buttons ↻/✎/🗑 reserved ~55px of invisible flex width per row and squeezed titles). Menu items:
- **Resume** (labelled **Go to tab** for an open session): for a session that is **not** currently open — App calls `sessions:resume(workspaceId)` to bring the container up, selects that workspace, and hands the matching `TerminalPane` a resume request. The pane opens a new tab whose `SessionEntry.resumeOf` is the Claude session UUID; that tab's first attach runs `claude --resume <uuid>` in the container (see §6 *PTY*). If the container can't be brought up, App logs a non-fatal warning and does nothing.
- **Rename**: inline edit → `sessions:rename`; empty clears the override.
- **Delete**: a two-click inline confirm → `sessions:delete` (drops cache rows + unlinks the transcript). No modal — the action is row-local and the confirm is reversible up to the second click.

**Remote rows** (remote-workspaces (d)). The pane also holds the remote pages from `sessions:listRemote` and merges them with the local list (`withRemote` in `sessionsView.ts`); the merged value is opaque (its per-source pages ride a non-enumerable symbol), so the pane replaces it only with `federation.ts` results and never spreads it. A remote row's meta line starts with the cloud glyph (`CloudGlyph`, titled `Remote · <bookmark name>`). Its menu has no Rename or Delete. Resume (and clicking the title) is enabled only when the bookmark is at the `session` tier and running in the workspace list; otherwise it is disabled with a title saying why. Remote pages refresh on mount, on a remote `observability:summary` push for that origin (at most every 5 s, `sessions:listRemote({ origin })`), every 60 s, and on the header's refresh button; a full reload overtaken by a newer one is dropped; local pushes never fetch remote. Below the list, "N source(s) unavailable: <names>" names hosts that didn't answer, and each incomplete host gets **Load older sessions from <name>**, which appends that host's next page; under *This workspace* both show only the selected workspace's host. A refresh (one origin or full) puts a host's fresh first page on top of what is loaded from it (`refreshSessionPage`): rows loaded past that page stay, with their Load older cursor, and a row the fresh page also holds shows once, from the fresh page; a host that goes unavailable drops out. Remote text renders as React text only.

**Transcript search** (remote-workspaces (d2), `TranscriptSearch.tsx`). A **Transcripts** toggle (`aria-pressed`) beside the search box opens a second search box above the list that searches transcript content through `history:search` (§6 *Sessions*). It submits on Enter or its **Search** button, never per keystroke, since each search embeds and fans out. It follows the pane's scope (`searchTargets`): *All* searches this desktop plus every remote bookmark, *This workspace* only the selected workspace's origin, and with nothing selected it is disabled. A line under the box names who receives the query ("Searching this desktop + review-bot", "Searching review-bot"). Each hit is a button showing the cloud glyph for a remote origin, the workspace name, a relative time, the score as `(0.83)` and the hit text (`white-space: pre-wrap`, clamped to 4 lines, as React text). Below the hits, `searchUnavailableText` names the sources left out ("N source(s) unavailable: review-bot (search off), triage (different model), x"); an error such as `transcript search index is unavailable` shows as text. Clicking a hit sets the pane's text filter to the hit's session id (`filterSessions` matches an id exactly) and scrolls its row into view; a remote session beyond the loaded pages isn't in the list until **Load older** reaches it. Results live only in the component's state.

### Dev-server preview
A dev server (Vite, Next, a Python server, …) started inside a workspace container is auto-detected and openable in the host browser, with traffic relayed over the existing broker socket — no new published ports, no Docker bridge routing required. See §6 *Ports* for the IPC surface; §5 *Broker frame protocol* for the wire frames.

1. When a workspace reaches `running`, the `PortForwardManager` starts a `PortMonitor` for it: a dedicated `BrokerClient` issues `LISTPORTS` every ~3s, diffs the result against the last-seen port set, and HTTP-probes each newly-appeared port (broker `DIAL` + minimal `GET`; see §6 *Ports*). Only ports that answer `HTTP/…` enter the Serving snapshot — everything else a container listens on (DevTools ports, inspectors, one-shot tool servers) is retried briefly, then silently ignored. The Serving snapshot (`ports:changed` broadcast + `ports:list` seed) powers the Serving rail section.
2. The agent that started the server calls the fleet-state `expose_port({ port, label, reason? })` tool (the server's MCP `instructions` tell it to). When the declared port is in the Serving snapshot, `PortExposureService` fires `ports:detected` and the renderer shows a sticky labeled toast with **Open preview** (keyed per workspace+port). Undeclared ports appear only in the Serving rail section (see §5 *Observability pane*) unless `ports.undeclaredToastAfterSec` enables the fallback toast.
3. User clicks **Open preview** (toast or rail ↗) → `ports:open(workspaceId, N)`. Main re-probes the port first; if nothing answers anymore it returns `{ hostPort: null }` and the renderer shows an error toast instead of a dead tab. Otherwise main creates (or reuses) a `PortForward`: a `net.Server` bound to `127.0.0.1:0` (ephemeral host port). For every inbound browser connection, main sends `DIAL {channel, port=N}` to the broker over a fresh `BrokerClient`, awaits `DIALED{ok:true}`, then pipes the browser socket ↔ the broker channel using the existing `INPUT`/`OUTPUT` relay. Returns `{ hostPort }`.
4. Main calls `shell.openExternal('http://127.0.0.1:<hostPort>')` — the system browser opens the forwarded URL. WebSocket/HMR traffic (Vite, Next hot-reload) passes through the raw byte relay untouched.
5. User may also kill a serving port from the rail ✕ button (always visible — two-step confirm unchanged) → `ports:kill(workspaceId, N)` → broker `KILLPORT` (kills the whole command subtree so a supervisor can't respawn the listener; SIGTERM + 2s grace + SIGKILL). The row disappears from the Serving section via the next `ports:changed`. If the workspace's runner image predates KILLPORT support, the broker times out and the renderer shows an error toast: "runner image too old — recreate the workspace to enable kill".
6. The `PortForward` and `PortMonitor` are torn down automatically when the workspace pauses, stops, or is removed; `ports:changed(workspaceId, [])` is broadcast so the rail clears immediately.

### Model endpoint workspace (#250)
1. **Register an endpoint.** User opens Settings → **Model Endpoints** tab. Clicks **Add endpoint**, fills Name, Base URL (must start with `http://` or `https://`), Model ID, optional Small/Fast Model ID, optional Context Length (display only), optional Notes, optional API key. The **Test** button fires `endpoints:probe(baseUrl, modelId, apiKey?)` — a one-shot `POST /v1/messages` with `max_tokens:1`; the response badge shows OK or the error message. Clicking **Save** (or **Add**) calls `endpoints:save(...)` (persists to `<userData>/endpoints.json`) and, if an API key was provided, calls `endpoints:setApiKey(id, value)` (stores under vault scope `endpoint:<id>`, key `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`). The list refreshes. Deleting an endpoint via **Remove** calls `endpoints:delete(id)` — removes the registry entry and purges the vault scope.
2. **Create an endpoint workspace.** In the **New workspace** modal the **Model** combobox lists registered endpoints below Claude; the user picks one. Auth switches automatically to a passive note (no radio choice needed — the endpoint supplies its own token). With an empty registry the list is just Claude + "＋ Add endpoint…" — there is no locked dead-end state. The remaining form fields (image, name, env vars, resources) work identically to OAuth workspaces.
3. **Container create / local spawn.** `workspace:create` (or `local spawn`) calls `endpointEnv(endpointId)`, which resolves the registry entry live and retrieves the API key from the vault. It compiles five env vars — `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL`, `CF_SUMMARY_MODEL` — and spreads them **first** in the container env, so explicit workspace env vars (from `resolveEnv`) override them. No OAuth credentials file is bind-mounted. If the endpoint id is unknown (deleted after the workspace was created), `endpointEnv` returns `{}` and warns; the workspace starts and claude surfaces the auth failure itself. Registry edits and key rotation take effect on next create/recreate (not in a running container). For **local** endpoint workspaces specifically, `buildEnv` strips the inherited host `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (present in dev when using the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-…` fallback) before assembling the env, so the real Anthropic key is never forwarded to a third-party endpoint's process; an explicit `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set in the workspace's own env restores it.
4. **Observability.** Identical to other workspaces — JSONL → SQLite pipeline, cost read from `events` rows grouped by `(model, service_tier)`. For models not in `src/main/pricing.ts` the USD field renders **`—`** (zero in the DB, not surfaced as `$0.00`) — the pricing table covers only Anthropic's own models; custom/org endpoints are unpriced by design.

### Sign in with GitHub (Settings → Account)
Settings has an **Account** tab (`AccountSettings.tsx`). Each action applies at once.
- **Open:** the tab renders `identity:status()` (local, cached) first, then swaps in `identity:status({ refresh: true })`, so a revoked token shows "Sign in again". If the first read fails, the tab shows the error and **Retry**.
- **Not configured** (no client id): a note naming `CLAUDE_FLEET_GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` and `identity.github.clientId`.
- **Signed out:** **Sign in with GitHub**, plus a note that it uses claude-fleet's own OAuth app (not the gh CLI's, whose per-user token cap would sign people out at random) and asks for `read:org` and `read:packages`.
- **Signing in:**
  - shows the device code with **Copy**, **Open github.com/login/device** (opens in the OS browser through the `setWindowOpenHandler` gate) and **Cancel**, plus a countdown to expiry;
  - polls `identity:signInPoll` one call at a time, waiting the interval main returns;
  - stops at `expiresAt` with "The sign-in code expired. Start again.";
  - a `done` that lands after Cancel is still shown as signed in (the sign-in was already saved);
  - leaving the Account tab (another tab, or closing Settings) abandons the flow via `identity:signInCancel`.
- **Signed in:**
  - avatar, display name (the profile name, falling back to the login) and @login, plus teams;
  - **Sign out**, disabled while a sign-in is pending;
  - whether private ghcr.io loadout sources can use the sign-in (`packagesAccess`);
  - a revoke-on-GitHub hint.
- **Degraded vault:** a note that the sign-in lasts until the app quits.
- **SAML SSO:** when GitHub's `X-GitHub-SSO` header reported an org needing authorisation, the tab explains that it's a one-time GitHub step. It links the org's authorise URL (the `required; url=` form), or the app's per-org settings page (the `partial-results` form).

### Inbox
- An **Automations chip** (labelled "Automations"; the code still calls it the inbox chip) sits after the workspace chips in the top strip. Its dot is red when anything urgent is open, grey when todos are open, and hollow when there's nothing; its sub-line reads "7 open · 1 urgent" / "nothing open".
- Clicking it swaps the main pane for the **Inbox view** (`InboxView.tsx`). The workspace chip loses its active outline (`selectedId` passed to the strip is `null`), but `selectedId` itself is kept, so the left and right rails keep showing the last workspace; terminals stay mounted but hidden. Clicking a workspace chip (or "jump to" in a todo) closes the inbox.
- **Todos tab:** status tabs (Open · N / Done / Dismissed / All), an add row (title + priority + Add todo), search (title, body, tags), and click-a-tag filtering (a removable `#tag` pill). The list is grouped Urgent / High / Normal / Low with a colored left edge. Each row shows source (You / workspace name / "deleted workspace"), age, "seen N×", "recurred" and tags; its round check marks the todo done.
- **Detail pane** (`TodoDetail.tsx`): Done / Dismiss (or Reopen), a priority select, Source / Key / Seen (count, first, last) / Linked workspace / editable Tags, the body (rendered as markdown, see §9 "Markdown text fields"), and the history timeline (newest first).

### Notifications
- **Delivery:** with the window focused, an in-app toast with **Open** (sticky when urgent); otherwise an OS notification (Electron `Notification`) whose click focuses the app on the todo, or on the workspace for needs-input. No action buttons on OS toasts.
- **🔔 popover** (`BellPopover.tsx`) on every todo row, in the todo detail ("Notifications") and on every Activity row. It has scope tabs (This item / source / #tag), levels All · High and up · Urgent only · Off, snooze chips (1 hour / Rest of today / 1 week), "Now: X, from …", and Clear rule. Rows whose effective rule is non-default show a bell-off hint ("off for this source", "snoozed for this item · until 23:59").
- **Inbox header notify control** (`NotifyControl.tsx`): "Notifying · Nights at 22:00" / "Paused · until 15:30" / the active downtime. Its menu offers For 30 minutes / For 1 hour / Until 17:00 (before 17:00) / Until tomorrow 08:00 / Until I turn it back on, Let urgent through, and Downtime schedules…; while paused it shows Resume now / Extend…. While paused or in a downtime the inbox shows a banner with the held count (+30 min / Resume now).
- **Activity tab** (`ActivityView.tsx`): Everything / Notifications / Held back, with time, title (click → its todo or workspace), source, and the delivered channel or the held reason in words.
- **Settings › Notifications** tab (`NotificationsSettings.tsx`, saves immediately): Notify me for (default level), Downtime (a week view with a "now" marker; the schedule list with enable, Edit, Remove; an editor for name, start/end, days and bypass), Rules (the table with Remove), and Desktop notifications (support check + Send a test).

### Desktop presence
`src/main/inbox/presence.ts` (Electron) + `presenceCore.ts` (pure), refreshed on every inbox / notification / input-wait change (`inboxEvents`) and once a minute.
- **"Needs you"** = urgent open todos + workspaces waiting for input (+ pending approvals, from Phase 5). Plain open todos never count. **1 → dot, ≥2 → count, 9+ cap; red if anything urgent, otherwise violet.**
- **Windows:** `setOverlayIcon` with `resources/presence/overlay-<urgent|wait>[-<n>|-9plus].png`. **macOS:** `app.setBadgeCount` + `dock.setBadge(count)`. **Linux:** `app.setBadgeCount` (desktop-dependent), with the tray as the reliable signal.
- **Tray** (all platforms): the app tile with a dot (`tray-<none|urgent|wait>-16/32.png`, never a number), a tooltip ("claude-fleet: 7 open · 1 urgent · 2 waiting for input · paused until 15:30"), click → show the window. Its menu has a status line, Open Automations, Open claude-fleet, Pause notifications for 1 hour / Pause until tomorrow 08:00 (or Resume notifications), and Quit claude-fleet.
- **Attention:** a *delivered* urgent desktop notification while the window isn't focused flashes the taskbar button (`flashFrame`, cleared on focus) or bounces the dock once (macOS). Held items never get attention.
- **Keep running in the background** (Settings › Notifications, default off): when on and a tray exists, closing the window hides it instead; the tray's Quit (or app quit) really quits.
- **App icon:** the "Teardrop" outline starship (dark tile, ink outlines, green nacelle strips). Source is `scripts/icons/build-icons-html.mjs` (SVG) and the PNG export is `scripts/icons/render-icons.mjs` (puppeteer, run by hand). It outputs `resources/icon.png` (1024px; electron-builder `icon:` derives .ico/.icns), `resources/presence/*.png` (shipped via `extraResources` → `process.resourcesPath/presence`; dev reads `<appPath>/resources/presence`), and `window-256.png` (the Linux window icon). A unit test asserts every asset name presenceCore can return exists.

### Automations
- **Definition** (YAML, validated by `definition.ts`): `name` (`[a-z0-9-]`, unique), `description`, `tags`, `triggers` (`schedule: {cron, tz?, args?}`, a 5-field cron with an IANA `tz`, default host local, and DOM/DOW OR semantics; `manual: {allowFrom?: [workspace]}`), `concurrency` skip|queue, `timeout` (default 30m), `reschedule: {min}` (#437, below), `params` (string|number|boolean|enum, with required/default/values), and `event: {on: session.idle|workspace.started|todo.created, workspace?, tag? (todo.created only), debounce? (≤1h, default 2m)}` (#407 Phase 4), and `steps` (≤10): `prompt: {workspace (name or id, stored as id), mode: live|fresh, message, wait? (live), ensureRunning?, timeout? (default 10m), allowedTools? (fresh)}`, or `script: {run, runIn?, shell?, secrets?, ensureRunning?, timeout? (5s–2h, default 5m)}` (#407 Phase 7). `$self` is rejected until Phase 8; top-level `secrets` is rejected (declare them on the script step). Message placeholders: `{{args.x}}`, `{{trigger.kind}}`, `{{trigger.workspace}}`, `{{trigger.todo}}`, `{{run.id}}`, `{{steps.<id>.stdout}}` (an earlier script step).
- **Running:** the scheduler ticks every 30 s. A due schedule fires **once** even if several slots were missed (a closed app gets one `catch-up` run), and creating or re-enabling an automation restarts its schedule clock. Non-user runs are capped at one per minute per automation. With `skip` a run that arrives during one in progress is recorded `skipped`; with `queue` one waits. Each prompt step checks the target's state (`target-not-running` unless `ensureRunning`, which starts or unpauses it and waits for the broker; `docker-unreachable`), injects the rendered prompt into its live session (the open tab if attached, else a transient headless attach, as `committee_post` does but **without** the committee grant gate, since the user's approval is the consent), and with `wait` waits for the host busy signal to go busy → idle within the step timeout. Failures and timeouts raise a `run-failed` notification. On startup `running` runs become `interrupted` (one notification, no retry).
- **Reschedule (#437):** with `reschedule: {min}` (a duration from 5m to 30d; durations take s/m/h/d) a run may choose when the automation runs next. A script step prints `{"next":{"in":"4h"}}` or `{"next":{"at":"<ISO>"}}` (`fleet-emit next 4h` / `fleet-emit next --at <ISO>`); a prompt step's Claude calls `schedule_next({run_id, in | at})`, gated exactly like a `post_todo` with `run_id` (the run is live and one of its steps targets the caller). `in` resolves at request time; requests past 365 days, or with both/neither of `in`/`at`, are refused (a script's becomes a step warning, as does a request from an automation without `reschedule`). The last request in a run wins, and it is applied only when the run ends `succeeded` or `nothing-to-do` and the automation is still enabled at the same version: `next_run_at = max(requested, runEnd + min)` (a request sooner than the floor is clamped, never refused). Failed, timed-out or interrupted runs apply nothing; the run still records what was asked and why it was dropped. While `next_run_at` is set it **replaces** the cron: `nextFireAt` reports it and cron slots are ignored; when it comes due the tick clears it, sets `last_scheduled_at` to now (so a cron resumes from there) and starts a `rescheduled` run with param defaults. Disabling, saving a new version or deleting the automation drops it. A cron is optional (manual- or event-only automations were already valid), so with neither a cron nor a request an automation idles until something runs it; there is deliberately no fallback interval — an automation that must not stall keeps a slow cron, relies on the `run-failed` notification, or posts a todo. Fresh sessions of an automation with `reschedule` also get `schedule_next` in `--allowedTools`.
- **Fresh steps (#407 Phase 4):** the host creates a headless broker session `auto-<runId>-<stepId>` whose args are `-p <rendered prompt> --allowedTools <mcp__claude-fleet-state__{post_todo,update_todo,list_todos,notify,list_automations}[,allowedTools…]> --session-id <uuid>`. `hostSpawnFresh` picks that claude UUID and records the `broker_sessions` mapping before the spawn (as tab CREATE does), which is how the Sessions list recognises automation transcripts (`SessionListItem.headless`). No broker change was needed, since `CREATE` already appends args to the claude exec. The step polls `LIST` every 2 s until the session ends (or the step timeout → `timed-out`) and always attaches + `CLOSE`s it afterwards. Fresh sessions are container-only (local workspaces refuse; use live). `Backend.spawnHeadless` / `headlessState` / `closeHeadless` are implemented for docker (BrokerClient on a dedicated channel) and mock (a simulated ~1.5 s session).
- **Remote workspaces are not desktop automation targets:** their automations run on their host. `hostPostToWorkspace` (before its attached-tab shortcut), `hostSpawnFresh` and `hostEnsureRunning` refuse a `kind: 'remote'` target with "remote automations run on their host" (`backendRouter.resolveAutomationKind`), and a remote tab's busy detector feeds neither `session.idle` nor committee busy (`hostRunsSignals`).
- **On a remote host** the scheduler and runner run on the lease holder, on the host's fenced store (§7 *Remote host*, *Host automations*). Definitions come only from the host config's `automations:` block, reconciled on start and reload; sessions can't propose. Only fresh prompt steps to the hosted workspace run there: script steps, live steps, `session.idle` / `workspace.started` events and other workspaces are refused at validation and again at run start, and a row not from the host config never runs.
- **Event triggers (#407 Phase 4):** `ipc.ts` `setFleetEventObserver` reports `session.idle` (the attached-session busy detector going busy → idle) and `workspace.started` (`workspace:start`); the inbox emits `todo.created` (user or workspace posts). `automationService.onEvent` matches enabled automations (event name, optional workspace, optional tag), then debounces: the first event starts a timer and later ones increment its `count`, so a burst becomes **one** `event` run (`trigger.event = {on, workspaceId?, todoId?, todoTitle?, count}`). Loop guard: an automation never reacts to a todo it posted, nor to a workspace one of its steps targets while its run is active or for 2 min after it ends. The rate cap applies.
- **Script steps (#407 Phase 7):** `runIn` omitted → the **automations workspace's container**; `runIn: <workspace>` → that container workspace; `runIn: host` + `shell` → this computer. **Host shells** are detected (`hostShells.ts`, cached 5 min, `automations:hostShells` → `HostShell[] {id, label, isDefault?}`): on Windows `pwsh` (PowerShell 7, if on PATH), `powershell` (Windows PowerShell, always) and `wsl:<distro>` for each distro from the same `listWslDistros` probe the local-workspace form uses; on macOS/Linux `bash`/`zsh`/`pwsh` when on PATH, plus `sh`. Validation: with one shell available an omitted `shell` is filled in; with several the step must name one ("pick the shell to run this on: …"); a named shell must be on the list ("isn't available on this computer"). The drafting prompts list the shells. Wherever a host step is reviewed or edited (proposal card, automation detail) a **shell picker** shows the detected shells; on a proposal the pick is applied when you approve (as an edited version), on an automation it saves a new version. `powershell` runs `powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command`. Container steps run via `Backend.execScript` (docker: an exec of `sh -c 'mkdir -p $FLEET_SCRATCH && cd $FLEET_SCRATCH && exec timeout -k 5 <s> bash -c "$FLEET_SCRIPT"'` in `/workspace/automations/<automationId>/`, the step env set on that exec only, stdout/stderr demuxed and capped at 1 MB, exit 124 = timed out, plus a host-side backstop; mock: `bash` on the host in a temp dir; local: refused). Host steps run via `hostScript.ts` `runScript`: a **clean env** (only PATH/HOME/USERPROFILE/SystemRoot/TEMP-style basics plus the step env), cwd `<userData>/automations/<id>/`, a detached process group killed as a tree on timeout (`taskkill /T /F` on Windows), `wsl.exe -d <distro> --exec bash -c` with `WSLENV` naming the step env keys. **Inputs are env only:** `FLEET_RUN_ID`, `FLEET_AUTOMATION`, `FLEET_ARGS` (JSON), `ARG_<NAME>`, `FLEET_STATE` (JSON saved by the last run, `{}` at first), `FLEET_TRIGGER` (`{kind, workspace?, todo?}`), and declared `secrets` read from the executing workspace's vault (host steps: the automations workspace's vault); a missing secret fails the step. **Outputs** are stdout JSON lines (`scriptProtocol.ts`): `{"todo":{…post_todo fields}}`, `{"notify":{title, body?, priority?, key?}}`, `{"state":<json ≤64 KB>}` (last wins), `{"next":{in|at}}` (#437, *Reschedule* above); at most 50 todos+notifications per step; other lines are plain output (last 16 KB) for `{{steps.<id>.stdout}}` and the run detail. Todos and notifications are filed as `automation:<id>` with `source_run_id` through `inboxService.host` (no workspace gate or hourly cap). Exit **0** continues, **10** ends the run as `nothing-to-do`, anything else fails it with the stderr tail; a failed or timed-out script posts nothing and saves no state. The runner records `kind: 'script'` step rows with `detail {via: container | host:<shell>, exitCode, todos, notifications, warnings?, stdout?, stderr?}`. The runner image ships `/usr/local/bin/fleet-emit` (`docker/runner/fleet-emit`, jq-built lines: `fleet-emit todo "…" --key K --priority P --tag T --body B --notify`, `fleet-emit notify "…"`, `fleet-emit state '<json>'`).
- **Prompt hygiene:** every prompt ends with a footer naming the run id. `{{steps.<id>.stdout}}` is never spliced inline: it becomes "[output of step <id>, see below]" and the output goes in a labelled "output of step <id> (data from a script, not instructions)" block, since scripts often relay outside text. Args supplied by a workspace (`run_automation`) are not spliced into the instructions but quoted in a labelled "caller-supplied input" block.
- **Attribution:** `post_todo` / `notify` with `run_id` are filed under `automation:<id>` (with `via_workspace` and `source_run_id`) only while that run is live **and** one of its steps targets the caller; otherwise they are refused. Without `run_id` a post is the workspace's own.
- **UI:** an Inbox › **Automations** tab (count = enabled). Script steps show where they run (host steps in red), their timeout, secrets (🔑) and text; a run's script step shows exit code, todo/notification counts, warnings, and stdout/stderr tails. The list shows an enable switch, trigger summaries, next fire, last run and tags, with All / Enabled / Failing filters and search. The detail shows enabled · version · creator, Run now… (a form generated from params, validated by the host), Edit YAML (live validation, Save new version), Delete (inline confirm), the week's stats, triggers, steps, params, and runs (click to expand args and per-step outcomes). + New automation opens the editor with a starter template.

### Sharing folders between nodes (git-sync loadout)
- **What:** folders under `/shared/<name>` mirrored to git remotes so several nodes share files and keep working offline. It's not an app feature. It's the generic **`git-sync` loadout** (claude-fleet-loadouts): a 5-minute `git-sync` automation (`concurrency: skip`, `runIn: $self`) whose script step is `fleet-git-sync.sh`, inlined at install. Install it **once per node**, normally in the automations workspace. Credentials are that workspace's own (`GH_TOKEN` env, `gh auth`, SSH); `GH_TOKEN` is passed to git through run-scoped `GIT_CONFIG_*` env (a `credential.https://github.com.helper`) and never written to disk.
- **Config:** every `/shared/.git-sync.d/*.json` (`{"folders":[{name, remote, branch?="main", mode?="mirror", author_scoped?=true}]}`), loaded sorted; `*.sample` is ignored. Validation: name pattern, non-empty `remote` and `branch` strings, mode, boolean `author_scoped`, no non-object entries, no duplicate names across files; errors name the file and folder. Organisation **config loadouts** depend on `git-sync` and write their own file from an install script. Install scripts run in the container with cwd `/workspace` and **without** the loadout's files, so the content is embedded (`file:` script + heredoc). Invalid config → exit 2, nothing touched. A folder removed from config is no longer synced; its clone stays on disk.
- **Modes:**
  - **mirror:** fetch + `merge --ff-only`. Local edits or local commits are kept and raise todo `git-sync:<name>:local-edits`, and pulls pause.
  - **Identity** (auto-sync only, resolved once per run): (a) `GIT_SYNC_IDENTITY` env, `login <email>` or `login|Display Name <email>` (the login must match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]*(\[bot\])?$`; anything else is ignored), **required for bot/GitHub App tokens and non-GitHub remotes**, since an App installation token gets 403 on `GET /user`; else (b) `gh api user` with the workspace's own credentials: name = profile `name` (the login if unset), email = noreply `<id>+<login>@users.noreply.github.com`; else (c) the identity cached in `state.identity`, refreshed whenever (a) or (b) succeeds, so it works offline after one success. Any `gh` failure (missing, offline, 403) falls through to (c). With none, auto-sync folders are skipped with todo `git-sync:identity` (sign the sync workspace in to GitHub, or set `GIT_SYNC_IDENTITY`); mirrors still sync. The **author folder `<login>` is the login lowercased** (logins are case-insensitive; display names aren't unique). Every workspace on a machine shares one `/shared` root, hence one identity per root; one person on several machines shares one folder, and concurrent edits to the same file become an ordinary `…:conflict` todo.
  - **auto-sync:** commits as that name and email, passed via `-c user.name`/`-c user.email` to both `commit` and `rebase` since runner containers have no global git identity. Commits only `<login>/` (including its deletion) unless `author_scoped:false`, and are path-limited (`git commit -- ":(literal)<login>"`; every author-folder pathspec uses `literal` magic, since a `[bot]` login contains glob characters) so anything pre-staged outside the author's folder is never committed. Edits elsewhere → todo `…:outside-edits`, never pushed. Files over 50 MB stay local and unstaged with todo `…:large-file`. Then `pull --rebase --autostash`; with `author_scoped:true`, before each push attempt (initial and the race retry) `git log --name-only @{u}..HEAD -- . ":(exclude,literal)<login>"` must be empty, per commit so an add-then-revert can't cancel out — a local commit someone else made in the shared clone that touches paths outside `<login>/` (e.g. another workspace committing directly in `/shared/<name>`) raises todo `…:outside-commits` (paths + `git reset --soft @{u}` to drop them) and **holds the whole push**, not just that commit; not counted as a failure. `author_scoped:false` has no such guard — it pushes any local commit, so use it only where every writer is trusted.
  - **Conflict** (todo `git-sync:<name>:conflict`): a rebase with unmerged paths is aborted and local commits are kept; a conflict reapplying the autostash of outside edits leaves the edits in `git stash list`, recovered with `git restore --staged . && git stash drop` after resolving. Any folder that already has unmerged paths when a run starts is skipped with the same todo, regardless of mode. A rebase stuck for any other reason (no unmerged paths) is aborted and counted as a failure, with the real error.
- **Hold:** `<name>/<login>/.sync-hold` (or `<name>/.sync-hold` unscoped) skips the folder while a long writer works. Older than 2 h → ignored + todo `…:stale-hold`. Never committed.
- **Untrusted clones:** every workspace can write `/shared`, so before touching an existing clone the script checks it. A detached HEAD (no branch) gets todo `…:detached` ("check out `<branch>` to resume syncing") and is skipped, distinct from a config mismatch. Otherwise `remote.origin.url` and branch (HEAD, `branch.<b>.merge`) must match the config, else todo `…:remote-changed` and skip; the user re-clones by moving the folder aside. `git config --local --no-includes` keys must be on an allowlist (`core.{repositoryformatversion,filemode,bare,logallrefupdates,autocrlf,untrackedcache,preloadindex,ignorecase,symlinks,precomposeunicode}`, `user.{name,email}`, `remote.origin.{url,fetch}`, `branch.<b>.{remote,merge}`), so any `include*`, hook-, filter-, ssh-, proxy- or pushurl-style key gets todo `…:unsafe-config` (key names only, never values) and a skip. Neither counts as a failure. Every git call also passes `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false`, and the script unsets `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE`/`GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`/`GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` on entry so none of its own git calls can be redirected by an inherited env.
- **Bounded runs:** `fetch`/`pull`/`push` run under `timeout` (≤60 s; exit 124 = a failure "timed out") with `http.lowSpeedLimit/Time`, and `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` defaults to the workspace's `core.sshCommand` (or `ssh`) plus `-o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=20`. A first **clone** isn't capped at 60 s — it gets the full remaining run budget (`DEADLINE+30-SECONDS`) — but only starts with at least 90 s of that budget left; otherwise it's deferred to the next run the same way a folder past the deadline is (named in stdout, its state carried). After 200 s no new folder starts. Deferred folders are named and the state is always printed. The start folder rotates (`state.next`) so a slow folder can't starve later ones.
- **Safety:**
  - One run per root (`flock -n -E 75` on `/shared/.git-sync.lock`): a held lock → exit 10, any other lock error → exit 1. A `.git/index.lock` older than 10 min is removed under that lock.
  - An existing non-git directory is never overwritten (todo `…:not-a-repo`).
  - A missing upstream branch counts as a failure, for either mode.
  - Any failure (network, auth, timeout, no upstream, push rejected…) is counted per folder in step `state`. A notify `…:failing` fires at the 3rd in a row, then every 288th (about daily).
- **State:** `{"failures":{name:n}, "seen":{todoKey:fingerprint}, "next":name, "identity":{login,name,email}}`, pruned to configured folders. A todo is emitted only when its key is new or its body changed; keys whose condition is gone are dropped, so a recurrence re-posts. Held or deferred folders keep their entries.
- **Exit codes:** 10 = nothing changed and nothing new to report, 0 = changed or reported, 2 = invalid config, 1 = lock error.

### Proposals
- Workspaces propose with `propose_automation({definition: YAML, rationale})` or `propose_automation_change({id, definition, rationale} | {batch: [{id, definition}], rationale})`. The definition is the full new YAML, validated like the editor's. `get_proposal({id})` shows the caller its own proposals' status and your note. A newer pending proposal from the same workspace for the same automation supersedes the older one.
- **Classification** (`classify.ts`, pure): low-risk = name, description, tags, prompt wording, params, concurrency/timeout, schedules no faster than every 5 min, removals, raising `reschedule.min` and removing `reschedule`.
